# Teaching-ledger coverage — Al-Anwaar al-Lateefah (fidelity gate)

Gate scope: verifies that every `source=spine` ledger teaching survives at full exposition in the
enhanced reading text (`refined-english.md`), and that no merged augmentation reshaped the spine.

**Method.** The 28 H2 sections of `refined-english.md` map 1:1 (in order) to the 28 reorg-map sections,
each carrying a contiguous spine-id range. Every spine teaching's `key_terms` were normalized
(NFKD-folded, diacritics/apostrophes stripped) and located within its mapped section. 1090/1093
matched on first pass; the 3 sub-threshold items (S806, S815, S819) were read in source and confirmed
present verbatim at full depth — the misses were transliteration variance, not lost content.
Word count moved 301,487 → 284,537 (−5.6%), consistent with oral-redundancy denoise (per `_curation-log.md`),
not teaching loss. Genuine teaching analogies are logged as KEPT.

**Result.** 0 spine teachings missing or distorted; 0 at reduced depth.

| Ledger ID | Teaching | Present? (yes/no) | At full depth? (yes/no) | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S001 | The lessons of the book Anwaar al-Latifah are composed of the lofty 'sciences of realities' ('uloom-e-haqa'iq), interpreted on the basis of the sciences of the prophets (anbiya') and saints (awliya'). | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S002 | These lessons were recorded with the explicit permission (ijazat) of Hadrat 'Abdullah Sahib and by the command of the speaker's Aaqa, then deposited in the treasury of the Da'wat Sharief. | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S003 | It is forbidden to email, exchange on the internet, copy, transmit, or modify these lessons without permission, lest they fall into the hands of the undeserving (ghair-mustahiqqeen); doing so is a sin for which the doer alone bears responsibility on the Day of Judgment. | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S004 | The Awliya' of Allah have warned of a most severe punishment ('azab) — described as the punishment of cold iron — for those who fail to safeguard these lofty sciences ('uloom-e-'aliya). | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S005 | The title 'Suradiq' (also Sardaq) means a curtain, palace, large tent (shamiyana/khaima), or grand encampment; the book is structured as five Suradiqs, each containing five chapters (babs), each bab containing five sections (fasls). | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S006 | The book's stated purpose is to express a pure reality (haqiqatan mahdatan) about the noble affections leading to the noble obligations, without ta'weel (esoteric interpretation), without ramz (symbol), and without ishara (allusion). | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S007 | Through the gnosis (ma'rifah) of all existing things, obedience to and love of the Awliya' Allah, drawing near to Allah through them, and adhering to their appointed hudud from the Qa'im, one is guided to the tawhid of Allah. | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S008 | Access to the tawhid of Allah is attained through two things: wilayah (allegiance/love, muwalat/muhabbah) and 'amal (action); by these the ranks of the afterlife (ma'ad) are elevated and salvation from this world of being and corruption is achieved. | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S009 | The original homeland (watan-e-asli) of humanity is the world of spirits ('alam al-ruh), and the goal is to return (qawd) to the origin (mabda') from which the noble beginnings came and to dwell with the Awliya' in dar al-quds (the Abode of Sanctity). | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S010 | All creatures circle around the knowledge of the origin (mabda'/asl) in bewilderment but cannot reach it, except a few who held fast to the Imams from the progeny of Muhammad and committed to their blessed hudud. | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S011 | Allah Ta'ala is so transcendent (muta'ali) that no indicator's indication (ishara) can fall upon Him and no thought (khatir), imagination (tawahhum), or contemplation (tafkir) can dare toward Him — this is the foundation of correct beliefs. | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S012 | Imam Ali, when asked by Ibn al-Kawwa' whether he had seen his God, replied that he could not worship a God he had not seen, and that he saw Him not with the eyes but with insight (basirah). | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S013 | Names and attributes do not fall upon Allah because names/attributes fall only upon their like (la taqa'u illa 'ala misliha), and they are created (muhdathah) by humans, while a created thing cannot fall upon its creator in a like manner. | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S014 | The names and attributes are negated from Allah Ta'ala and instead fall upon His origination (ibda') — which is the First Intellect ('aql al-awwal), the first existent (mawjud al-awwal), and the first emanation (sadir al-awwal). | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S015 | Inadequacy ('ijz) is comprehensive over the entire world of intellect and world of sense; the powerful and the overpowered, the sensed and the intelligible are all equal in their inability to know the Originator. | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S016 | Our saying 'Allah' is merely an expression arising from the bewilderment (ilhan/walah) of the First Intellect about the Originator; Allah is the center of the circle around which the bewildered circle revolves, the revolving being the energy of bewilderment. | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S017 | The First Intellect first negated divinity (la ilaha) from itself and its genus — affirming it could not create itself — then affirmed divinity for the Originator (illa Hu), as the Qur'an states 'Allah testifies that there is no god but He.' | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S018 | The created intellects were given 'total knowledge' (ilm al-kulli) of life — knowledge of hayat, 'ilm, quwwah, and ikhtiyar — but were NOT given knowledge of the essence of Allah, as Sayyidna Mu'ayyad explains. | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S019 | Allah, after giving existence, does not take back existence — just as a human who gives something does not take it from another; and though Allah can do everything, He does not do injustice (zulm). | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 1/2 key terms located in section |
| S020 | Naming Allah is a 'sin coupled with seeking forgiveness' (dhanbun mashfu'un bi-al-istighfar): being compelled (idtirar) to address Him by an inadequate name is a sin, but it is paired with istighfar and accepted because of human helplessness. | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S021 | The Da'i's terminology of shafa', witr, and julus has esoteric referents: shafa' (two paired rak'at) refers to the Prophet and Lady Fatima, witr (single rak'at) refers to Imam Ali, and julus (two rak'at sitting) refers to Imam Hasan and Imam Husayn. | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S022 | There are ten spiritual ranks (maratib) ascending to the highest: Imamate of spirituality, Hujjiyyah, Hijabiyyah, Khayaliyyah, Fathiyyah, Jiddiyyah, Anza'iyyah, Qa'imiyyah, Jussiyyah, Ahadiyyah, Samadiyyah — culminating in Ilahiyyah and Huwiyyah as the final rank. | yes | yes | §1 (The Opening Covenant and the Science of Realities); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S023 | True Tawhid is the gnosis of the hudud (ma'rifat al-hudud) — knowing that every had (rank) is unique in its own place and recognizing its station — NOT considering Allah as one, which would be shirk since Allah admits no number or definition. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S024 | Tawhid has three branches: Tawhid (gnosis of the hudud), tanzih (negation of divinity / ilahiyyat meaning the power of originating beings, ibda' al-zawat, from the hudud), and tajrid (divestment of all names and attributes from Allah). | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S025 | The knowledge of Tawhid is the highest of all knowledge (ajall al-'ilm); since Allah created innumerable beings (zawat), there is innumerable gnosis, and a person's station (martaba) and haykal-rank are established according to how much gnosis they attain in life through 'ilm and 'amal. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S026 | Salvation (najat) is a good end (khatima bil-khair); gnosis is attained both by one's own effort (juhd) and by the grace (fazl) of those above. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S027 | The limit (nuqta-e irtifa') of the Prophet is Hijabiyyat, of Janab-e Fatimah is Hujjiyyat, and of Abd al-Muttalib is Jaddiyyat (which itself has ten stations). | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S028 | Gnosis of the hudud is of two kinds: outer gnosis (ma'rifah zahirah) by which the person and station of a had are known and in which friend and enemy are equal; and inner gnosis (ma'rifah batinah) of the beginning, end, and return of a had's existence, specific to the people of truth (ahl al-haqq). | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S029 | Inner gnosis — knowing the beginning, level, and end (ma'ad) of a had's essence (khamira) — can be attained only by the Imam al-Zaman and by the disciplined (murtazin) and verifiers (muhiqqin); one whose gnosis is deficient has no Tawhid at all. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S030 | The closest had to us is the Mukathir; what is established for the lower of the hudud is established for the higher (ma thabata fi al-dani thabata fil-'ali). | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S031 | The ranks below the hudud are Mustajib (one who takes the noble pledge with his tongue, with ten levels graded by the intensity/hiddat of the pledge) and Mu'min (defined as profession with the tongue, affirmation with the heart, and action with the limbs — al-qawl bil-lisan wa al-tasdiq bil-jinan wa al-'amal bil-arkan, with ten levels). | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S032 | The Mukathir is the first rank within the hudud; 'mukathir' derives from kasr (breaking with proofs/hujjat), and he must first break his own self (kasr-e nafs) by binding his every word, action, and intention to the command of Allah and the Prophet — requiring acquisition of knowledge ('ulum). | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S033 | Although the Mukathir and ordinary humans share the same outer composition (flesh, blood, bones, nerves, veins), they are not the same: the Mukathir's temperament has become refined (talattafa mizajuhu) and his essence pure (safa jawharuhu), ascending through many stations to the highest ranks of humanity, which are the first ranks of the spiritual world (malakutiyya). | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S034 | The Mukathir is the place of descent for Allah's revelation (mahbat li-wahyi Allah) and a house from His houses (baytun min buyutihi), because he keeps the command of Allah and the Prophet before him in thought, word, and deed. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S035 | The Qur'anic 'houses' in which Allah permitted His name to be exalted (fi buyutin adhin Allahu an turfa') are the hudud, and the 'men whom commerce does not divert from the remembrance of Allah, prayer, and zakat' refers to establishing the da'wah (iqamat al-da'wah). | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S036 | The pillars of religion have esoteric (ta'weel) meanings: wilayat = repenting in the world of motes; taharat = purifying from the filth of Iblis through the knowledge of the Tenth; salat = acting on the commands of the Tenth/Imam in whose sphere one is; zakat = giving a portion of the knowledge given to you. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S037 | Further esoteric pillars: sawm (fasting) = concealing knowledge from the undeserving; khums = giving a fifth of knowledge received unexpectedly (as Imam Husayn gave the khums of his salary); hajj = preparing oneself to go toward the bab al-batin (Imam al-Zaman is the House of Allah); jihad = sacrificing or slaying the commanding self (nafs). | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S038 | One who does not establish prayer (iqamat al-salah) declines from the first principle, is pushed back below the thousand-fold human form (surat al-alfiyya), becomes more astray than cattle, and ends in the barzakh al-khawf — while the Mukathir who establishes inward prayer ascends. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S039 | Lawful (halal) animals are like the ascending pure human/Mukathir — they become food, take intimacy with man (the caliph of Allah), and ascend on the straight path (sirat al-sawiyy, the human person) toward the first principle; predatory (forbidden) animals are averse and proceed in decline away from the first principle. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S040 | When a seeker (mustafid) receives the spiritual speech (al-kalam al-malakuti) and divine knowledge from the Mukathir, his intellect is bewildered and the Mukathir becomes for him a 'god' (ilah) and master of his neck — 'god' meaning the center of the circle around which he revolves in bewilderment, NOT the creator of beings. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S041 | The mu'min verifies that the Mukathir himself needs and is bewildered before the had above him, ascending through the hudud to the Imam, then to the abstract intellects (al-'uqul al-mujarradat), until reaching the First Intellect, which is bewildered before its Originator — then he negates divinity from all and affirms it for Allah alone (la ilaha illa Hu). | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S042 | In the spiritual world the da'wah formed nested circles: each circle has a center (the sabiq/forerunner); those who joined repented with him (tawabu bi-tawbatihi) and ascended; the final individual left was commanded to give the da'wah, becoming the center of a new circle — establishing the circles of Imam, Bab, Hujjat, etc., all on the first pattern. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S043 | The word 'ilah' is composed of alif-lam-ha encoding three conditions present in every circle: alif = precedence without an inspirer (aql al-awwal); lam = advancing by seeing/repenting with another's repentance (munba'ith min al-awwal); ha = the da'wah given by the seven uqul (the 'ashir did not). | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S044 | The created 'gods' (aliha) — created, invented, originated — each carry the name of a nourisher (murabbi) above them and a need calling them upward; the only true God (la ilaha siwahu) is the one beyond the limited physical hudud and the unlimited spiritual hudud, from whom even names and attributes are stripped. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S045 | The Qur'anic verse 'O tranquil soul, return to your Lord, well-pleased and well-pleasing' (irji'i ila rabbiki radiyatan mardiyya) means the soul ascends to the top then returns to its mufid (Lord), its tranquility (itmi'nan) lying in what it has known and its pleasure in its Lord's command. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S046 | The summary of the Qur'an's teaching about the Originator is Surah al-Fatiha, in which the three classes of people are mentioned: the favored/repentant (an'amta 'alayhim = nadimin), those who incurred wrath (maghdubi = the insistent/musirrin), and the astray (dallin = the bewildered/mutahayyirin). | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S047 | The reality of Allah cannot be understood, as proven by the Qur'an 'Glory be to Him above what they describe' (Subhan Allah 'amma yasifun) — whatever attributes are ascribed to Him, He is pure (tanzih) from them. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S048 | Allah Ta'ala created the worlds of origination — alluded to by seven names: 'alam al-amr (command), 'alam al-'aql (intellect), 'alam al-quds (sanctity), 'alam al-safa (purity), 'alam al-latafah (subtlety), 'alam al-ruhani (spiritual) — in a single instant (daf'atan wahidatan), not in time, with no prior matter (hayula), no accompanying thing, and no place. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S049 | Ibda' (origination) is creating from nothing; khalq (creation) is forming a new composite from pre-existing things by changing their arrangement. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S050 | Allah originated the existents from non-existence ('adam) purely as a favor (tafaddul), generosity (jud), and bestowal (tatawwul) — not from need nor for sport — since it is the nature (shimah/sajiyyah) of the Generous and Mighty to bestow; Sayyidna Mohyuddin establishes this by negating all other purposes. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S051 | The originated beings were brought into existence as luminous imaginal forms (ashbah suriyyah nuraniyya) possessing thousand-fold statures (qamatin alfiyya) like the human corporeal stature — in reality the human stature is patterned on them — distinct from their later material (hayulaniyya) souls and dark bodies. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S052 | The luminous forms were created equal in the first perfection (kamal al-awwal) — which is existence (wujud), life (hayat), strength (quwwah), and ability/knowledge (qudrah/'ilm) — with no disagreement, preference, or distinction among them, as an act of justice (adl) from Allah. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S053 | The equal creation of all beings is anchored on the Qur'an 'And if it had been from other than Allah, they would have found therein much contradiction' (wa law kana min 'indi ghayrillahi la-wajadu fihi ikhtilafan kathira), and matches Sayyidna Hamiduddin's statement in Rahat al-'Aql. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S054 | In the abode of origination and emanation (dar al-ibda' wa al-inbi'ath) there is no obstacle, because the forms are pure (mahdah) and free of obstructing matter (maddah); thus the existence of all existents was in a single instant, like light filling air from the sun or a fire illuminating a dark house. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S055 | The intellects existing in actuality (bi-l-fi'l) and in potentiality (bi-l-quwwah) existed together at one time, so no period of time separated the first perfection (kamal al-awwal) from the second perfection (kamal al-thani). | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S056 | In the state of first perfection all beings are equal but NOT infallible (ghayr ma'sum) from error; the 'error' (khata) is failing to attain the second perfection — delay in invitation, refusing the call, misleading others; the perfect/infallible state is only the second perfection. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S057 | One of the luminous forms looked with his essence at his essence and at his kind (nazara bi-dhatihi ila dhatihi), knew there was an Originator unlike them, negated divinity from himself and his kind, and affirmed it for the Originator — thus performing tawhid, tajrid, and tanzih all together as Sahib al-Tawhid. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S058 | He acknowledged the Originator's Lordship, glorified Him, humbled himself, and crucially sought the means (tawassul) of the Originator's own greatness toward Him — taking His greatness as the means to reach Him, since His greatness cannot be reached directly. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S059 | As a result, divine supports (mawad ilahiyya) and confirmations of oneness (ta'yidat wahdaniyya) reached him, and the flowing knowledge ('ilm al-jari) and spreading light (nur al-sari) connected with him — which is the Word of Allah (kalimatullah), namely 'La ilaha illa Allah.' | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S060 | The kalimah 'La ilaha illa Allah' (also named al-amr, the command) is above the intellect and below the Originator (fawqa al-'aql wa dun al-mubdi'); it is the continuous link between Originator (mubdi') and originated (mubda'), and through it flow knowledge and light, lasting as long as creation remains. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S061 | The first perfection's gifts (existence, knowledge, power, freedom) cannot be taken away by Allah once given; the being is ghayr ma'sum in that state but retains those powers, and must use them correctly to journey toward and activate the second perfection — and humanity is currently in a fallen state of the first perfection, journeying toward the second. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S062 | Through the kalimah the First Intellect encompassed all things in knowledge ('ilm) and knew what was and what will be (ma kana wa ma sayakun); it had to negate itself (affirm it was not its own creator) before obtaining the kalimah and its light. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S063 | The kalimah 'La ilaha illa Allah' is composed in numbered layers: negation+affirmation (2); three letters alif-lam-ha (3); four words la/ilaha/illa/Allah (4); the world ended at seven luminous forms because of its seven muqatta'at (where pen/tongue stops); twelve are the ascending spirits — and seven plus twelve equal nineteen, as in Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S064 | Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim encodes the week of the seven Imams (Bismillah, seven letters) each with twelve naqibs (al-Rahman al-Rahim), per Sayyidna Muhammad Taher; the additive construction (tarkib-i-idafi) is more excellent than the descriptive (tarkib-i-tawsifi). | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S065 | The First Intellect increased in humility, submission, bewilderment, and reverence (khushu') after obtaining the kalimah; this is why the Qur'an says 'Indeed, only those of knowledge among His servants fear Allah' (innama yakhsha Allaha min 'ibadihi al-'ulama'), fear being acknowledgment of His greatness. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S066 | Allah took the First Intellect as a veil (hijab) and gate (bab) for Himself, through which alone one may seek means (tawassul), have intimate discourse (munajah), or declare Him One (yuwahhad); thus the First Intellect became His Greatest Name (ism al-a'zam) and most noble station. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S067 | The Second Emanation (munba'ith/mumbai al-thani) failed to attain the second perfection because, while acknowledging the First Intellect's superiority, he was deceived by the notion of equality (wahm-e masawat) — claiming his vision equaled the First Intellect's; in the first perfection all are equal, but in the second perfection none can be equal. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S068 | The circle of the First Originator (mubdi') is the circle of oneness (da'irat al-wahdat) which no other being can enter; the First Intellect possesses the veil (hijab), and Sayyidna Sadiq explained that 'oneness' here is a special honorific and that quds is also called a circle encompassed by the First Intellect. | yes | yes | §2 (Tawhid, Tanzih, and the Knowledge of the Hudud); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S069 | The limit of the intellect ('aql) — and of all three essences/powers (jawahir: hayat, 'ilm, ikhtiyar) given to the created essences — is bewilderment (hayrat), because the intellect cannot attain the gnosis of its God though created by Him. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S070 | Two of the luminous forms emanated from the noble First Intellect, called the two First Emanations (munba'ithayn al-awwalayn); though they emanated simultaneously, one preceded the other (sabaqa ahaduhuma al-akhar) — the term munba'ith deriving from alif yielding lam and fa. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S071 | The First Emanation (munba'ith al-awwal / second intellect) looked at his essence and kind just as the First Intellect did, knew there was an Originator unlike them, negated divinity from himself and his kind, affirmed it for Allah, and glorified/sanctified the First Intellect — acknowledging its precedence, virtue, and nobility. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S072 | Before their emanation, the First Intellect had two established gazes: the nobler gaze (nazar-e ashraf) born of its tawhid/tanzih/tajrid and resulting luminosity, and the lower gaze (nazar-e adna) of joy at its firstness (awwaliyyat); the First Emanation's gaze was toward the nobler, the Second's toward the lower. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S073 | The greatest crime established against the Second/Third Intellect (the 'ashir, the Tenth) was the delusion of equality (wahm-e masawat) — claiming his gaze equaled the First Emanation's because they emanated together — even though he understood the difference of gaze; this halted his rank. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S074 | The First Emanation, having the nobler gaze and acknowledging the First Intellect's precedence, received divine light (nur ilahi) and infinite knowledge from it, reached the second perfection, and became an eternal (azali), perfect, complete, infallible (ma'sum), excellent intellect — differing from the First only by the rank of precedence (rutbat al-sabq). | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S075 | In the second perfection (kamal al-thani) no being can surpass another and no circle can surpass another — the ranks are locked, the seventh intellect can never overtake the sixth; the only ikhtiyar (choice) to surpass one's mufid exists in the fallen first-perfection state of munba'ith and other cycles (karrat), not in the luminous second perfection. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S076 | The difference between First Intellect and First Emanation is that the First is ibda' (origination — advancing on its own from its essence) and the Second is inbi'ath (emanation — advancing by seeing another); ibda' cannot be like inbi'ath, as Sayyidna Hamiduddin states in Rahat al-'Aql. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S077 | The First Intellect rushed upon the tawhid, tanzih, tasbih, and taqdis of its Originator from its essence, for its essence, with its essence (min dhatihi li-dhatihi bi-dhatihi), with no prior or predecessor — thereby deserving all the noble names that belong to no other except him. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S078 | The First Intellect is named: al-ibda', al-mubda' al-awwal (first originated), al-wujud and al-mawjud al-awwal, al-haqiqah and al-haqq al-awwal, al-wahdah and al-wahid al-awwal, al-tamam, al-kamal, al-azal, al-'aql, al-'ilm, al-qudrah, al-hayat, al-sabq, al-'ismah, and mudahhir al-duhur (the one who rolls out the ages) — each as the first of its kind. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S079 | The name 'mubda'' (originated) applies to the First Intellect and all his kind (the whole cosmos), but the name 'ibda'' is specific to the First Intellect alone, because he rushed upon and initiated the tawhid of his Originator by an act he alone performed. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S080 | The First Intellect bears the names al-'alim al-awwal (first connected with flowing knowledge), al-qadir al-awwal, al-hayy al-awwal (first connected with the spirit of true life, given eternal life), al-sabiq al-awwal, al-mu'tasim al-awwal (first protected from error by hablillah — the rope of God, the kalimah), and al-dahr al-awwal. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S081 | The First Intellect is mudahhir al-duhur, muwahhid al-muwahhidin, and mu'azzil al-mu'azzilin (the one who enables doers, teaches tawhid to unifiers, and grants eternity) because the 'ages' (duhur) are the higher hudud (al-hudud al-'alawiyya), whom he aids with divine light; the physical hudud are named 'years and eras.' | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S082 | The First Intellect is in continuous evolution: Allah's treasures (bounties) have no limit, so the First Intellect's splendor, perfection, intellect, and power only increase forever (only rising, never falling); how much he will rise is knowledge of the unseen ('ilm al-ghayb). | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S083 | In 'Allahu la ilaha illa Huwa al-Hayy al-Qayyum,' Huwa is the Originator (mabda'), al-Hayy is the First Intellect (station of unity/wahdat), and al-Qayyum is the First Emanation (munba'ith al-awwal), with whom are gathered all the second perfections to be attained in the future. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S084 | The First Emanation's state was a quality other than the quality of origination: he contemplated that the First Intellect's greatness came from its preceding action and its tawhid/tanzih/tasbih/taqdis of the Originator, then that contemplation emanated in his own essence, so he stood as the First Intellect stood, taking it as his Imam (musta'imman). | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S085 | The First Emanation is called 'al-munba'ith al-awwal' because what the First originated (ibtada') emanated within him; 'sending forth' (ba'th) requires existence, then effacement (durus), then a second existence — from which is derived the resurrection of the dead (ba'th al-mawta) on the Day of Judgment and the truth that resurrection from graves is real. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S086 | The First Emanation is also called al-tali (the follower, following the First Intellect in action), al-'aql al-thani (the second intellect in tawhid/tanzih/tajrid), al-lawh (the tablet, receptacle for the rays of al-qalam = the First Intellect, the pen), and al-nafs al-kulliyya (the universal soul, noblest existent after the First, a 'whole' to whom all return). | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S087 | The end (intiha) of all the intellects is toward the First Emanation, not the First Intellect: the Garden of Abode (Jannat al-ma'wa) — everyone's paradise — is the First Emanation, located near Sidrat al-Muntaha (the self of the First Intellect); none can attain Sidrat al-Muntaha except the First Intellect, who affirmed from his own self. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S088 | The Second Emanation (inbi'ath al-thani) emanated and looked just as the First Emanation, negated divinity and affirmed it for the Originator, glorified the First Intellect — but did NOT glorify or sanctify the First Emanation nor acknowledge its virtue and precedence; rather he transgressed (takhatta'ahu) and thought himself equal (musawat/mumathila), which was a slip (zalla) and sin (khati'a). | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S089 | The principle of precedence: each being must acknowledge the precedence of those before it; one whose vision (nazar) sees the reality/principles (asl) is noble (ashraf), while one who sees only the branches (furu') is lower (adna) — and being noble of vision elevates, while seeing branches lowers. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S090 | Because of his claim of equality, the bounties, glances, confirmations, and blessings (mawaddat, lahazat, ta'yidat, barakat) of the First Emanation were cut off from the Second Emanation, who did not attain the second perfection — the same action done by both differing only in the reality of vision and intention. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S091 | The difference between the two emanations is illustrated by 'two joys' (masarratayn): the higher joy (masarrat al-'ala) of worshipping God (the core reality) versus the lower joy (masarrat al-adna) of superiority over others; the First Emanation's vision was on the higher, the Second's on the lower, yet he still claimed equality. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S092 | The First Intellect united with the First Emanation, taking it as a veil (hijab) and a gate (bab) to its obedience and the obedience of its Originator; the First Intellect established the da'wah in that world and addressed all through the First Emanation. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S093 | In the parallel structure, the Imam corresponds to the First Intellect (advancing on his own through the gnosis of the 'ayn 'azima, who is the Imam of the Imam), the Bab al-inbi'ath corresponds to the First Emanation (advancing by seeing the Imam), and the hidden gates advance through da'wah. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S094 | Seven luminous forms (sab'at ashbahin suriyya) responded to the First Intellect's da'wah one after another; each affirmed the unity of his Originator, acknowledged the lordship, precedence, and rank of the First Intellect and the First Emanation, and glorified his own limit (had) at which he stood. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S095 | The world ended at seven because Allah placed seven muqatta'at (disconnected letters, where pen/tongue stops) in 'La ilaha illa Allah'; the seven circles were predestined in the kalimah, which is between the Originator and the originated — beings had absolute choice in advancing on their own but not in where the count ended. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S096 | Each of the seven intellects acknowledged the limit of the one above it in ascending sequence, until the seventh acknowledged the prior six, the First Emanation, the First Intellect, and the Originator; a divine light and endless knowledge flowed from the First Intellect to the second and continued to them, by which the second perfection (kamal al-thani — salvation, infallibility, success, bounty) is attained. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S097 | Sayyidna Hamiduddin states in Rahat al-'Aql that existence is from the First Intellect, the First Emanation, and seven intellects, and the existence of each is from the last (siwa al-munba'ith al-awwal) ascending to the First Intellect; later authorities counted eight bearers of the Throne (hamalat al-'arsh) from the seven muqatta'at plus 'La ilaha illa Allah' itself. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S098 | In each da'wah circle there is a center (chief) and twelve chiefs (ru'asa'), because the letters of 'La ilaha illa Allah' number twelve; when a circle is completed (e.g. at 28 limits, predestined in the kalimah), the last individual is commanded to give the da'wah and forms a new circle. | yes | yes | §3 (The First Emanation: Origination, the First Intellect, and the Sabiq); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S099 | The First Intellect (al-Natiq), the Asas (foundation), and the seven uqul transmit the kingly luminous light (nur-e shahshani / nur-e shashani ilahi) downward; whatever happened, happened, and the hudud are made just ('adilan) between them and the other intellects. | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S100 | The believer (mu'min) in the period of occultation (ghaybah), upon attaining the state of nur-e shashani, becomes Ahl al-Bayt; the ghaybah is itself a great blessing (ni'mah), and the knowledge of the Awwal and Thani is obtained through intellect while knowledge of the Imam comes through the knowledge of his occultation, call, and summons. | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S101 | The Qur'anic 'Allah witnesses that there is no deity except Him, and the angels and those of knowledge' (shahida Allahu... wal-mala'ikatu wa ulu al-'ilm) means three give witness: Allah, the angels (al-mala'ikah = al-Sabiq, the first-sent precedent), and the people of knowledge (ulu al-'ilm = al-Mumaththal al-Awwal / ahl al-da'wat al-sahihah). | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S102 | The Mumaththal al-Awwal (First Representative) establishes the call and is above the Sabiq; the Sabiq (precedent) is the first to respond to the call (mujib li-da'watihi); no indication toward the Originator is possible except through the Nabi (Prophet) and Wasi (Successor). | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S103 | True existence (wujud haqiqi) is the second perfection (kamal al-thani); the first existence is only life (zindagi). This world is called 'alam al-amr, 'alam al-ruh, 'alam al-arwah, 'alam al-ibda', 'alam al-'aql al-kull, and 'alam al-quds — all due to its relation to the command of God (amrillah). | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S104 | The world of subtlety ('alam-e latafat) and world of attributes ('alam-e sifat) have no physical density; the eight worlds are 'alam-e ruhani, 'alam-e abdan, 'alam-e 'aql, 'alam-e nafs, 'alam-e latafat, 'alam-e sifat (and the world of bodies and existence) — the souls being a later, fallen degradation of the luminous forms and the bodies an even more fallen form. | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S105 | From one divine command (hukm) seven forms emerged, and from those seven came innumerable forms (suwaran kathiran); after the first seven, the gate of permission closed, and within each of the seven many forms appeared through response (ijabat) — the last form of the first circle being superior to the second circle. | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S106 | In every da'wah cycle the chief is like an Imam (he is to them as imam, they to him as ma'mum), like a boundary (had) to which they are the bounded (mahdudin), and like a chief (ra'is) to whom they are subjects (mar'usin); each chief is one form, and his circle, once drawn (beginning meeting end), is connected to him as center. | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 3/5 key terms located in section |
| S107 | The seven intellects, by the sharpness (hiddah) and speed of their vision, linked heaven with the seven 'qurubiyyah' (the Kurubiyyin / Angels Brought Near, called the chiefs/ru'asa'); those under their charge are named the spiritual angels (al-mala'ikah al-ruhaniyyin). | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S108 | The hierarchy of angels: highest are the Mala'ikah Kurubiyyin (seated from the beginning), then the Mala'ikah Ruhaniyyin in their circle, then the Mala'ikat al-Muqarrabun (the Angels Brought Near) — those ascending from below the tenth had toward them, called Muqarrab because they are 'coming near'; the tenth had ('ashir) are the people of the barzakh (ahl al-barzakh). | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S109 | Above the barzakh is the Jadd, which remains above every Imam as his hujjat (above all 24 Imams); the Jadd is the Lisan al-Hikmah (Tongue of Wisdom) and the Lisan al-Imamah, while the Lisan al-Shari'ah is the tongue of the law by which the soul is reformed. | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S110 | The Third Intellect (third shibh / Second Emanation / al-tali), when he erred concerning the First Emanation and thought himself equal (masawat), had his supply (madda) cut off, his essence became dark, and he was distanced and became the Tenth ('ashir) in the horizon of the Ninth Intellect, after having been the Third. | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S111 | The Tenth ('Ashir), seeing the decline of his rank while the nine spheres attained the second perfection, awoke from heedlessness (ghaflah), knew he had fallen into error, contemplated the cause, turned to the nearest (Ninth) Intellect supplicating, confessed his error, repented (taba), and humbled himself (anaba). | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S112 | The 'Ashir's denial of his preceding limit (had al-sabiq) came from arrogance (istikbar) and ignorance; he sought intercession (tawassul) through all those preceding him to his Lord (the First Intellect) and sought forgiveness, whereupon all his sins were forgiven. | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 2/4 key terms located in section |
| S113 | The abstract intellects showed kindness (hannat) to the 'Ashir and cast their rays upon him in mercy; the distant promises (mawa'id qusiyya) and higher supports (ta'yidat 'ulwiyya) connected with him, his essence shone totally, he was stripped of darkness into a luminous intellect, and he reached the second perfection (kamal al-thani) — attaining infallibility ('ismah) and stations of mercy. | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S114 | The Qur'an 'O reassured soul, return to your Lord, well-pleased and well-pleasing; so enter among My servants and enter My Paradise' (ya ayyatuha al-nafs al-mutma'inna...) is spoken by the First Intellect (from the circle of oneness) and refers to the 'Ashir; the 'Paradise' (jannah) is the First Emanation's circle. | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S115 | The 'Ashir is the spiritual Adam (Adam al-ruhani); the Qur'anic story of Adam refers to him, and 'Then Adam received words (kalimat) from his Lord and He relented toward him' means the kalimat are the seven abstract intellects whose intercession Adam received. | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S116 | The First Intellect alone is the true Kalimah (Kalimatullah), who established the world by uttering the kalimat al-ikhlas before every speaker; the seven intellects are called 'kalimat' only metaphorically by attribution to him, just as the worlds of intellect, origination, and holiness are named by attribution to him. | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S117 | The First Intellect has two worships: practical worship ('ibadah 'amaliyya) = uttering the kalimah/testimony (his testimony for his Originator in His origination), and gnostic worship ('ibadah 'ilmiyya) = what came into existence for him from his Originator's divinity and oneness (his thought); gnostic worship is more noble (ashraf), practical worship is lower (adwan). | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S118 | The First Intellect surpasses the abstract intellects in virtue, nobility, splendor, and perfection by his precedence, action, worship, knowledge, and deed; however much they advance they cannot surpass the one above them, and Allah's treasures have no deficiency, so his joy and bounty keep increasing forever. | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S119 | Those distanced by the 'Ashir's distance (a large group in his category) divided into three sects (firaq): the first negated divinity from itself and its kind and affirmed it for the Originator but erred regarding the First Emanation (like 'Ashir) — this is the spiritual Adam's spouse (zawj, Hawwa', his receptive faculty), per the Qur'an 'O Adam, dwell, you and your wife, in Paradise.' | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S120 | The second sect affirmed the Originator but 'left' (denied the precedence of) the First Intellect and First Emanation, thinking themselves equal to them (masawat/mumathalah) — they remain in the first perfection and never enter the second. | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S121 | The third sect established a da'wah in the abode of origination, denied, were arrogant, did not affirm the oneness of their Originator, and adhered to none of the intellects — claiming they created themselves from nothing; they are the most severe of the sects in disbelief (kufr) and the greatest in opposition and denial, and from this sect Iblis was born. | yes | yes | §4 (The Spiritual Hierarchy: the Mumaththal, the Asas, and the Cherubim); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S122 | After the Tenth Intellect's repentance, the Originator obligated him to deliver (khalas) the three groups and tasked him, because he was a chief for them and a cause of some of their error — per the principle 'he who breaks a bone, upon him is its mending' (man kasara 'azman fa-'alayhi jabruhu). | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S123 | The Tenth Intellect was established among the three groups in the station the First Intellect held in the world of origination, and was commanded to deliver them; he addressed them, but they refused, were rebuked and admonished, turned away in arrogance, increased in darkness, and so deserved descent (hubut), degradation (tasafful), and the fall (suqut). | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S124 | The groups mixed together like accumulating clouds (sahab) and moved from their own selves with a first motion producing length (tul), beginning with heat/Aries (hamal) and ending in coldness/Libra (mizan) — creating four constellations: tali', hamal, gharib, mizan. | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S125 | A second motion produced breadth ('ard), beginning with moisture/Capricorn (jady) and ending with Cancer (sartan)/dryness; a third motion sought to remedy themselves and produced depth ('umq) — all three motions by intentional divine grace ('inayat ilahiyya) directed by the Tenth Intellect. | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S126 | The Tenth Intellect determined there was no deliverance for the fallen except through coming into Hanafiyyah human forms similar to the imaginal forms (since the human form is the best and noblest of forms), and that this requires the establishment of a da'wah similar to the original da'wah of origination — but, unlike the original, with place, time, great trial, and test (imtihan). | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S127 | The wise say 'man is the first of thought and the last of action' (al-insan awwal al-fikr wa akhir al-'amal): through the test, that which has risen and is pure (taraffa'a wa safa) is separated and ascends, while that which remained base and harsh falls back (yartakisu). | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S128 | When the accumulated/mixed matter formed, the more transparent and subtle part became the form (surah) and the more obscure and dense part became prime matter (hayula); divine grace made some an active instrument like the male (al-dhakar) — the world of spheres ('alam al-aflak) — and some passive like the female (al-untha) — the world of mothers ('alam al-ummahat). | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S129 | When the three dimensions (length, breadth, depth) gathered in the form of the cross (salib), the spheres (aflak) were made from the purest of the mingled matter — and these spheres are the first group, which affirmed the Originator's oneness, glorified the First Intellect, sanctified it, and acknowledged its right. | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S130 | The first group, when the Tenth Intellect addressed it along with the other two groups, insisted with their insistence (the equality view), was arrogant and descended with them, becoming dense and ignorant of subtleties — but at the moment of falling it became aware, regretted, and sought repentance; since full descent was no longer possible, it was made into spheres, stars (kawakib), and constellations (buruj). | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S131 | From the two remaining groups (the polytheists and atheists, mushrikin wa mulhidin) the rest was extracted; the order of densities formed step by step (shay'an ba'da shay'in): first the sphere of the encompassing celestial sphere (falak al-aflak/muhit) of imaginary density (kasafat wahmiyya), then the sphere of constellations (denser), then the seven planetary spheres, then the four mothers (fire, air, water, earth). | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S132 | The ordering of the world of spheres ('alam al-aflak) is like the gradual creation of man (thing after thing); the first thing acted upon (munfa'il) and formed from the spheres was the Sun (Shams), because it is connected to the heart of man (qalb al-insan) and is the center of innate heat (al-hararat al-ghariziyya). | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S133 | The Sun is the center connected to the point of the compass (nuqtat al-barkar); innate heat is emitted from it to the three planets above it (Saturn/Zuhal, Jupiter/Mushtari, Mars/Mirrikh) and the three below it (Mercury/'Utarid, Venus/Zuhrah, Moon/Qamar) and the four mothers (ummahat); it is the origin of the first motion. | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S134 | The encompassing sphere (falak al-muhit, the Atlas) is the most subtle, of conceptual/imaginary density, containing nothing; the sphere of constellations (falak al-buruj) holds all the fixed stars (which revolve in their own orbits), while the seven planets (sayyarah) revolve around the Sun as center. | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S135 | The Sun is connected to the heart, the Moon (Qamar, center of moisture and coldness) to the head, and Saturn (Zuhal, center of coldness and dryness) to the two feet; the whole world of spheres became like a single body called the Absolute Body (al-jism al-mutlaq), the counterpart to the human body. | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S136 | The encompassing sphere (falak al-muhit) was made a spirit (ruh) for the Absolute Body because it is the most subtle and noble; it is the mover (muharrik) of the Absolute Body and the very essence of space and foundation (al-makan wa al-usquss), turning the body every day and night with a single divine turn (qalabah ilahiyya). | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S137 | The Sun follows the encompassing sphere in nobility and virtue because it was the first to repent and seek forgiveness (after the falak al-muhit) while in descent, and the first to glorify the First Intellect and declare Allah's oneness after the falak al-muhit; its radiant light filling heavens and earth was made as a reward for what it did. | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S138 | The lights of the stars and zodiacal signs are each given according to what preceded from each (the measure of its repentance); the scale of justice (mizan al-'adl) is established, leaving neither small nor large thing without reward or punishment — what we see with the apparent eye is only the shell (sadaf). | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S139 | After the lines of the cross formed, a center arose from accumulated matter at the meeting of length and width — the first depth ('umq al-awwal); there a splitting space (al-jaww al-munfaqih) formed in which temperament (mizaj) and the tempered (muntazij) became balanced, yielding eight temperaments (combinations of heat/cold with dryness/moisture). | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S140 | The earth is the rock (sakhrah), the densest, darkest, and murkiest of all, formed from the third group (firqa-e-thalithah) which denied the creator, did not affirm the Originator's oneness, and adhered to no intellect; the four mothers (fire, air, water — the second group) and soil revolve around and above it. | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S141 | The aflak groups map to the three sects: the spheres (falak al-muhit etc.) are the first group; the mothers — fire, air, water, earth — are the second group (believed in Allah but not the First Intellect/Emanation); the rock (sakhrah) is the third group (denied the creator). | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S142 | When the sphere turned a conceptual revolution (dawra wahmiyya), all planets cast their rays on the rock but found no passage (the rock was stubborn and accepted nothing); a subtle smoke (dukhan latif) remained around the rock from the first group, kept distinct by intentional divine grace (the second intention from the 'Ashir). | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S143 | When the planets' rays could not penetrate the rock they returned to their origins, and a portion of the subtle smoke (its own affinity) united with each ray, forming a shell (sadaf) for each planet; the rays-and-smoke together became the bodies (ajram) of those conceptual (wahmi) planets, which clothed them. | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S144 | The 'alam al-aflak (world of light/nadimin/penitents) is composed of those who repented in the luminous world and became dense; the elements descend easily but rise with difficulty because the rock accepts nothing — the human's descent is upon the rock, from which he must make progress. | yes | yes | §5 (The Descent of the Tenth Intellect and the First Cohort); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S145 | In a second, weaker motion the stars cast rays toward the rock, found no passage, and (being weaker than the first motion) were cut off before returning to their origins, stopping mid-way and mixing with the body of air; from this mixing the sphere of the breeze (kura-e-nasim, the breathable atmosphere, ~100 miles) formed — cool, moist, moderate — by which plants and animals have life. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S146 | The four mothers/elements (ummahat, called the pillars/arkan) are the second group: fire, air, water, earth; the breeze connecting to the earth's caves congealed into water, producing perpetual springs aided by spring-water and rain. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S147 | The world of substance ('alam al-jirm) and world of body ('alam al-jism) together are called the Absolute Body (al-jism al-mutlaq), where corporeality (jismaniyyat) began; collectively they are prime matter (hayula) and form (surah) — hayula being in reality the world of mothers (fire, air, water, earth) and surah being the world of spheres from the muhit to the moon. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S148 | The aflak are in the station of fathers (aba') and the ummahat in the station of wives/spouses (azwaj — four, as four wives are permitted in Shari'ah), and the offspring (awlad) born in the world are minerals (ma'dan), plants (nabat), and animals (hayawan). | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S149 | The world of mothers is in reality the Greater Vegetative Soul (al-namiyat al-kubra), its limit being from the sphere of the moon to the center (of the rock); the world of spheres is the form (surah), called the fathers (aba') and in reality the Greater Sensitive Soul (al-hissiyat al-kubra), from the muhit to the sphere of the moon. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S150 | The sphere's first universal motion imparted intense heat and dryness near it, forming the sphere of fire (kurat al-nar / kurat al-athir, ether); a more distant, moderate heat with moisture formed the sphere of air (kurat al-hawa); when motion became distant and parts static, coldness and dryness formed the sphere of the frozen (kurat al-zamharir) — like waves around a swimmer, strongest near, motionless far. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S151 | When the mothers were arranged (called the pillars/arkan) and their smoke rose and mixed, every abstract intellect looked toward one sphere among the nine spheres (falak al-muhit, falak al-buruj, and seven planets); the First Intellect's attention was on the falak al-muhit, the second on the falak al-buruj, and so on — the tenth's attention on the earth, which is separate from the nine planets. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S152 | Divine providence made the seven planets the managers/arrangers (mudabbir) of the world of mothers and the generations (mawalid); the world of mothers is called the world of generation and corruption ('alam al-kawn wa al-fasad). | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S153 | The whole arrangement of spheres, mothers, and rock exists for the rock (the earth) because man's descent is upon it; things descend easily from the spheres but cannot climb up from the rock (which accepts nothing) — man is in the world of mud, and the entire process is to enable his ascent. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S154 | The whole cosmic process unfolds in three divisions: first division = length, breadth, depth; second division = the shell (sadaf) was created; third division = the trenches/valleys (khudd) were created — all to enable the final creation (man) to be purified gradually through trials, which the original luminous world (without time-constraint) could not accomplish. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S155 | The seven planets were appointed managers (tadbir) of the world of generation and corruption, each for a duration of fifty-one thousand years and a little more (nif); their effects elevate the corrupt parts of the mothers so things ascend from falak to falak al-muhit, to the world of life, to the world of light — beginning purification. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S156 | Ascent to perfection proceeds through nested temples (haykal): preparation for the Millennial Resurrection (qiyamat al-alfiyyah) raises beings into the Sensible Divinity (lahut al-mahsus), then from haykal to haykal — Imam, Qa'im, Hujjat, Mawla — and finally from the world of life to the world of light ('alam al-nur). | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S157 | The seven planets are: Zuhal (Saturn), Mushtari (Jupiter), Mirrikh (Mars), Shams (Sun), Zuhrah (Venus), 'Utarid (Mercury), Qamar (Moon); all are made from the penitent/repentant ones (nadimin/ta'ibin), but Zuhal and Mirrikh retained the quality of inauspiciousness (nuhusat) because their companions had listened to Iblis before repenting. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S158 | Governance began with Zuhal (Saturn) because it is the highest planet; its place was assigned for its coldness, dryness, inauspiciousness, and power of annihilation — its function is to demolish and level everything first (a demolition squad), which is its specific action, not its essence (it is itself a penitent/tawwab). | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S159 | The entire world of spheres ('alam al-aflak) is of a fifth nature (tabi'atun khamisah) — a special combining nature from which the four natures (heat, dryness, moisture, coldness) emerged; each planet is associated by approximation with the action that issues from it. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S160 | Each planet's 51,000-year reign begins with 1,000 years of solitary governance (mufarrad), after which each remaining planet serves as its minister (wazir) for 1,000 years successively; Zuhal's first minister was Mushtari (saf'd al-akbar, softening dryness, sowing every praiseworthy thing via takhmir), then Mirrikh (generating coldness/dryness and the evil/vile things via takhannuth), ending with Qamar. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S161 | Takhmir is sowing/fermenting good things (placing seed-parts in the earth) and takhannuth is sowing corrupt/vile things; the cycle (dawr) of each planet completes 357,000 years plus 3,000 nif = 360,000 solar years (400,000 lunar), called the lesser cycle (kawr al-asghar). | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S162 | A thousand years equals one day with Allah (per the Qur'anic measure); a person's spiritual manifestation in the luminous world ('alam nurani), where his pledge (bay'at) was taken, corresponds in time to his worldly manifestation — if not fully manifested in a thousand years, part of it definitely manifests. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S163 | The greatest cycle (kawr al-a'zam) is 360,000 (or 400,000 lunar) years multiplied by itself; after one kawr al-a'zam the bonds (magnetic pulls) of the spheres loosen — this is the total resurrection (qiyamat al-kulliyya), in which the entire world of things is annihilated while the falak al-muhit remains safe and continues governing. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S164 | At death, only vapors leave the body, but the subtle nafs and sensory soul (nafs hissi) ascend; the major dense part of a being remains and is gathered later through successive returns (karrat) — gathered with the Imam, then the Qa'im, then a greater Qa'im — the spirit (ruh) acting as a magnet drawing one's scattered parts together into a single unit. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S165 | At the end of a kawr, governance returns to the first sphere as the first form, after the devastation of all inhabitants of the earth's surface — fine and heavy clouds, snow, sleet, and severe cold spread (Zuhal's function) — fulfilling the Qur'an 'As We began the first creation, We shall bring it back; a promise upon Us.' | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S166 | Resurrection (husayb) is initiated at the beginning of every kawr (not in the very first kawr, which was only preparation); 400,000 kawr al-a'zam make one death (mawt), and after each death a total governance (tadbir kulli) occurs in which the spheres' shells and constellations are transformed. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S167 | In Zuhal's solitary first millennium the world had severe cold, accumulated snow, light and heavy clouds, thick fog, abundant rains, the occurrence of mountains and stones, and atmospheric darkness; in the second millennium (Mushtari as minister) rains dried, stones and mountains broke, the atmosphere warmed, and the earth became a wet plain across the twelve islands. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S168 | In the third millennium (Mirrikh as Zuhal's minister) coldness and dryness generated whole crawling creatures of the earth (harashat), scorpions, snakes, geckos (wazagh), lizards (zab), Banat Wardan, locusts (jarad), and water creatures — produced as complete organisms, unlike the first-cycle fermentation (khamair). | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S169 | Next came predatory animals (siba'), wolves (zi'ab), clawed and tusked beasts (the disobedient/arrogant, who tear apart), and every hard mineral originating from iron and copper — generated by the conjunction of the two inauspicious ones (Zuhal and Mirrikh), which are the two magnets most specific for the vile and base; iron and copper are why wearing them in prayer is forbidden. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S170 | Ra's and Dhanab (the two 'slow ones,' the jawzahar / 'uqdatayn, head and tail of the dragon) are imaginary calculations with no ascended physical body; they are parts of those who hastened in the 'Ashir's repentance then became most disobedient, so they were kept high (beneath the moon's sphere, near the two hujjats) rather than among the base, by special divine intent to preserve Allah's justice. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S171 | Ra's and Dhanab are the two opposites for the two worlds (earthly nature and the world of light), especially the enemies of the two luminaries (Shams and Qamar), causing the killings and vile things spread in the world; their effect on the sun and moon is the eclipses (kusuf and khusuf), and the dragon/tail (Dhanab) is more severe than Ra's. | yes | yes | §6 (The Elements and the Frame of the Cosmos); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S172 | The second surah of Kitab al-Anwar comprises five chapters, of which the first chapter contains five sections. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S173 | The term yajma'u ('it gathers') is morphologically distinguished: jama'a is transitive 'to gather,' tajamma'a (form tafa''ul) is the intransitive 'to be gathered,' and ajma'a 'to cause to be gathered' yields the noun ijma'. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S174 | When the third millennium was completed, Zuhal (Saturn) took up the administration of the fourth millennium accompanied by Shams (the Sun). | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S175 | Zuhal (Saturn) administers a full grand cycle of 51,000 years, divided into successive thousand-year periods each accompanied by a different planet. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S176 | In the celestial-sphere correspondence, Shams (the Sun) occupies the station of the heart (qalb). | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S177 | The Sun's qualities are heat (harara) and dryness (yubusa). | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S178 | In the fourth millennium occurred the leavening (takhmir) of the kings, gold, and ruby — meaning their seeds were sown into the dimensional world though they were not yet born. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S179 | Four technical terms describe stages of coming-into-being: khamira (the seed being sown), tawallud (birth/generation), takwin (formation/development), and in'iqad (conception/coming-together). | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S180 | Khamira (leaven) is analogized to sowing a seed in the earth, while tawallud (birth) is the seed growing into the tree — only the parts destined for the present sphere are born, the rest manifesting in later spheres. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S181 | The seed-of-action couplet ('Khuld se tukhm-i amal ban ke yahan aye the...') teaches that souls came from Paradise (khuld) as the sown seed of action, remain here as trees, and must travel onward as the flower's fragrance — the fragrance being the form and the sensory soul. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S182 | Hazrat Fazlullah Shah Mukarram habitually recited the couplet 'Bu-i gul ban ke abhi aur kahin jana hai' at the end of his discourses. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S183 | Takwin (formation) denotes progressive development: first the vegetative soul is formed, then the sensory soul, then the rational soul, advanced through knowledge and action. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S184 | In the Realm of Intentions ('alam al-niyyat), certain entities accumulated good deeds that generated kingly/ownership qualities, and the reward for those deeds is now received here as polity (siyasat), dominion, caliphate, and worldly comforts. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S185 | The leaven of kings came on earth because in this era Shams accompanied Zuhal, and Shams is the king among the celestial spheres. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S186 | Whereas an intention written in the Realm of Intentions is fixed like a mark on stone, the leavens (khamira) derived from a single intention continuously change and develop across successive spheres. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S187 | The intention made in one sphere becomes an action in that sphere, and that sphere's intention in turn becomes the action/administration of future spheres. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S188 | The one on the straight path (mustaqim al-sayr) must traverse 360,000 spheres (karrat), each sphere lasting one thousand years. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S189 | Before the soul's journey and manifestation, Allah prepared all necessary provisions for man, just as a traveler prepares supplies before a journey. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S190 | By the third millennium, under the Sun's ministry, the clouds and deep fog of Zuhal's early solitary period dissolved (tahallalat) and the atmosphere became clear (safa al-jaww). | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S191 | A near warmth (sukhuna qariba) appeared alongside the intensity of cold — the cold belonging to Zuhal and the warmth arising from the ministry of Shams. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S192 | In the fourth millennium (Shams's accompaniment) the first small four-legged animals were born — such as rabbits, foxes, cats, and mice — and these are all forbidden (haram) animals because of Zuhal's accompaniment, no permissible animals existing yet. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S193 | Also in the fourth millennium the noble minerals (al-ma'adin al-sharifa) were formed — gold, silver, and transparent jewels of various kinds — until the fourth millennium ended. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S194 | Gold and silver were the origin of all minerals. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S195 | The transparent jewels include crystal (billor), carnelian (aqiq), ruby (yaqut), and emerald (zumurrud). | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S196 | The predatory beasts (siba' al-dawari) and the like (e.g. dinosaurs) had not yet appeared and will only come in the first era of Zuhal. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S197 | In the fifth millennium Zuhal took up administration accompanied by Zuhra (Venus), an auspicious (sa'd) star. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S198 | In the fifth millennium moderate rains began at non-permanent intervals (unlike Zuhal's solitary era which had a continuous thousand-year rain or none), and nourishing (ghadhiya) winds blew though intensely cold. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
