| S199 | In the fifth millennium plant life began: rivers flowed, trees grew, flowers and blossoms (azhar, anwar) opened, pure fragrances spread, and delicious-tasting fruits appeared. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S200 | Azhar (flowers) and anwar/nawr (buds/blossoms) are distinguished as flower versus bud. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S201 | In the fifth millennium were born the praiseworthy (mahmuda) and permissible-beneficial (muhallala nafi'a) animals — cattle, sheep/goats, horses, and camels — which are safe from evil and helpers to mankind. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S202 | The animals were born from caves (maghara), as later also the Dhu Sa'id al-Da'i and his parts would emerge from caves. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S203 | In the sixth millennium Zuhal took up administration accompanied by 'Utarid (Mercury) sharing in the administration. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S204 | In the sixth millennium the life-giving and nourishing winds that pollinate (mulqiha) plants and trees became numerous, and animals nourishing for human bodies multiplied. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S205 | Pollination (liqah) denotes the meeting of male and female (in plants and animals) from which a third thing is born. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S206 | Date palms require human-performed pollination because they do not self-pollinate. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 1/2 key terms located in section |
| S207 | Across the first four planetary thousand-year companions (Shams, Zuhra, 'Utarid) Zuhal remained the king but all its ministers were auspicious, so no harshness came from the ministers. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S208 | Jism (plural ajsam) and jasad (plural ajsad) are distinguished, with jism applying to the human body. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S209 | Every kind of animal and tree, whether permissible or forbidden, appeared as a prelude (muqaddaman) to the human species before man himself came. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S210 | After all else, the administration (tadbir) of man was then initiated. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S211 | Man's genesis began when a subtle, moderate vapor (bukhar latif mu'tadil) ascended from the purest, sweetest, most pleasant and most balanced of the sweet (non-saline) waters and seas, not from saline water. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S212 | The vapor for which permission was given (from the remnants of the males toward establishing physical forms) deserved its precedence because it had preceded in remorse (nadam) over its prior state while in the state of falling (inhidar). | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S213 | When the command 'descend' was given a 'Big Bang' (inhidar) occurred, and whoever repented first in the state of falling had their parts manifest first and highest. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S214 | Adam al-Ibda'i (the Primordial Adam) belongs to the Realm of Life, while his veil/counterpart (hijab), the Dhu Sa'id al-Da'i, is the one who appears in the world. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S215 | The Prophet's name is Ahmad in heaven and Muhammad on earth, meaning Muhammad is the hijab (veil) of Ahmad. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S216 | From the ascended vapor a subtle cloud (sahab latif) formed, which propelled itself (indafa'a) as a light rain upon a land of pure soil. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S217 | The receiving land was pure soil: safe from every unpleasant taste, pure in color, of sound light dust, loose and uncompacted (mutakhalkhila), free of prior stickiness. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S218 | The pure land contained shallow (not deep) caves, and Allah set the standard of a grave for the great ones such as the Dhu Sa'id al-Da'i at no more than six cubits depth. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S219 | The water remaining in the caves was the like of a woman's semen (nazir mani al-imra'a), being the earlier water from snows and the rains of the preceding epochs that flowed in those caves. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S220 | The water springing from the earth (from earlier snows and rains) constituted the woman's part, while the rain descending now from the clouds constituted the man's part (the man's semen). | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S221 | The two waters mixed in a complete mixing (imtizaj kulli), so that formation occurred in those caves just as the drop of semen (nutfa) is formed in the wombs — the caves themselves becoming wombs (arham). | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S222 | An inner, earthly heat (harara batina ardiyya) from below then heated the mixture, just as the womb's heat acts upon the embryo. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S223 | At the moment of man's formation all the planets were in their houses of honor (buyut sharafiha), as if attending a wedding in their finest, and the Sun specifically was in the house of Aquarius (burj al-dalw), its most noble house. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S224 | Because the Sun was in Aquarius (its house of honor, with maximal heat-sending power), the inner earth was intensely hot (hamiyan) yet its heat was gentle and moderate (layyina mu'tadila). | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S225 | The water repeatedly rose attracting heat, then upon meeting coldness became heavy and descended to its resting place, ceaselessly wavering up and down (mutaraddidan sa'idan wa munhadiran). | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S226 | Through this repeated ascent and descent the water progressively densified and was acted upon bit by bit until it became an oily, moist, flowing, moderate substance — like a man's semen, completing the creation of the semen. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S227 | The birth/formation of the Dhu Sa'id al-Da'i occurred within Zuhal's 51,000-year grand cycle, specifically in the sixth millennium during the era of 'Utarid (Mercury). | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S228 | The planetary companionship sequence of Zuhal's cycle is: Zuhal (alone), Shams, Zuhra, 'Utarid, Mushtari, Mirrikh, Qamar. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S229 | It is a common misconception that the creation of the Dhu Sa'id al-Da'i occurred in the era of Qamar; no book of the Da'wat Shareefa mentions Qamar — all mention 'Utarid. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S230 | The complete formation of the Dhu Sa'id al-Da'i occurs in the era of 'Utarid, and he emerges from the cave in the subsequent era of Qamar. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S231 | The 'thousand' (alf) in these accounts is understood not as a literal thousand years but as one epoch. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S232 | Pending any explicit statement from Mawla, the believer's conception must be based on the book: the birth of the Dhu Sa'id al-Da'i happened in the sixth millennium in the era of 'Utarid. | yes | yes | §7 (The Governance of the Spheres); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S233 | When the primordial water reached the state previously described and the sun entered the zodiac sign of Jauza, the air grew hot, the bawarih (hot summer winds) blew, and the earth's surface heated, causing the oil-like substance to dry and congeal. | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S234 | Divine providence (al-inayat al-ilahiyyah) outlines and forms the human shape out of heat and cold as God wills. | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S235 | Every star (kawkab) was placed in charge of forming a specific member of those primordial bodies. | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S236 | The human form was engraved (naqasha surat al-insaniyyah) when the governance reached Utarid (Mercury) with the assistance of Zuhal (Saturn) and Qamar (the Moon). | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S237 | The first man grew from the earth, rising from its place little by little like a fetus, seated with chin on knees and arms wrapped, as the Quran states 'Allah grew you from the earth as a [progressive] growth.' | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S238 | The nafs al-namiya (vegetative soul) is what governs the being in the womb, and its center/origin is the moon (Qamar). | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S239 | At birth into the world God blew into man a life-giving divine spirit (al-ruh al-muhyiyah) connected to the rays of the sun and moon, which is the nafs al-hissiyyah (sensory soul). | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S240 | Amir al-Mu'minin Ali ibn Abi Talib, asked about the nafs al-hissiyyah, defined it as a celestial power (quwwah falakiyyah) whose origin is the celestial spheres, whose union with the body is at physical birth, whose seat is the heart, and whose action is sensation, movement, assault, and dominance. | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S241 | The cause of the nafs al-hissiyyah's separation from the body is the disharmony of the four humoral materials (safra, sawda, balgham, damm), and on death it returns to the sun whence it began. | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S242 | Each of the seven planets governs (tadbir) for 51,000 years in sequence beginning with Zuhal (Saturn), then Mushtari, Mirrikh, Shams, Zuhra, Utarid, and Qamar. | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S243 | The creation/impression of the human form (naqsh surat al-insaniyyah) occurred in the governing cycle of Utarid with the companionship (murafaqat) of Zuhal and Qamar, after roughly 250,000-300,000 years of celestial revolutions. | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S244 | The celestial revolutions before human creation are an uncounted period (fatrat) of preparatory arrangements for the Juz' al-Ibda'i; the dawr al-kashf is counted only from the beginning of human creation. | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S245 | When man appeared, Zuhal began the governance of the seventh millennium (tadbir alf al-sabi') with the participation of Qamar. | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S246 | Human forms appeared with the millennial stature in each of twelve islands (jaza'ir) according to the variety of their races: Arabs, Persians, Indians, Romans, Sindhis, Ethiopians, Berbers, Khazars, Zayalis, Slavs, Turks, Zanj, and others. | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S247 | On the island of Sarandib (on the equator) many humans appeared, among whom twenty-eight individuals were the purest, most refined, most noble, and most perfect of all creation, the pinnacle of the human species. | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S248 | The twenty-eight individuals correspond to the twenty-eight abjad letters (huruf al-mu'jam) and to the twenty-eight lunar mansions (manazil) of the moon's monthly cycle, to which they are attributed. | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S249 | These first human individuals reached perfection of body, speech, and action without passing through ordinary maturation (ihtilam), turning their vision toward the Tenth (Ashir) with divine assistance. | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S250 | The Ashir (Tenth) is the ancestor of the final ancestor, Adam al-Hayat, who carries out the governance, occupies the station opposite the First Intellect in the world of origination (alam al-ibda'), and is the place where the rays of the First Intellect fall. | yes | yes | §8 (The Moon, the Sensory Soul, and the Great Cosmic Cycle); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S251 | The most virtuous single person among the twenty-two/twenty-eight individuals is, among the kingdoms, like the red ruby among mineral gems, pure gold among malleable bodies, the date palm among plants, and man among animals, yet more noble, subtle, and exalted than all. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S252 | This virtuous person is the quintessence (zubdah) and choicest of the two worlds, the celestial (jirmani) and the physical (jismani). | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S253 | The first man, looking at himself, his kind, and the heavens, earth, stars, rivers, spheres, night and day, came to know by his own self that there is a Creator unlike them, negated divinity from all created existents, and affirmed it for their Originator alone, bearing witness to His oneness (wahdaniyyah). | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S254 | Through this witnessing the man connected with the divine sources, the radiant lights, and the angelic supports, reached the second perfection (al-kamal al-thani), was supported, illumined, and made to know all the arts and what was and what will be (ma kana wa ma sayakun). | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S255 | He stood in the world of bodies in the station of the First Intellect / Tenth Intellect, unique in the rank of oneness (wahdah), marked with origination (ibda'), and called the twenty-seven persons to the oneness of Allah (tawhid), purification (tanzih), and abstraction (tajrid). | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S256 | He took confirmed covenants from them, taught them the lawful and forbidden, the rulings and laws, legislated the laws of religion (qawanin al-diyanah), and commanded them to marry forbidden women whom the sunnah made permissible so as to populate the world. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S257 | He chose sixteen persons: a Bab, a Hujjah, two da'is (da'i balagh and da'i mutlaq), and twelve made hujaj al-layl kept in his presence; the remaining eleven (and a twelfth in his own island) were stationed in the islands as preserved ranks until the end of time. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S258 | Each of the chosen ones was commanded to conduct the mission (da'wah) in his assigned island and given twelve hujaj al-nahar; these hujaj are free of the afflictions of falsehood and of veiling/deception owing to the loftiness of their ranks. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S259 | The twenty-eight individuals are the 'possessors of knowledge' (ulu al-'ilm) and people of witnessing whom Allah mentioned in 'Allah bears witness that there is no god but Him, as do the angels and the possessors of knowledge,' where Allah=First Intellect, angels=other intellects, and ulu al-'ilm=the twenty-eight. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S260 | The twenty-eight exist upon originating bodies (al-jasad al-ibda'iyyah) without procreation (bi-ghayri tanasul); their head, the negator-affirmer-unifier who established the shari'ah, is opposite the First Originator and learned tawhid without any teacher preceding him, by his own self. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S261 | With every Imam the twenty-eight individuals manifest: the Imam (Dhu'l-Sayf Da'iyah) with twenty-seven, and the Qa'im with twenty-eight; Allah established this pattern (naqsh) to recur perpetually. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S262 | Allah created the world in six days, and one day with Allah equals one thousand years, so creation occurred over six thousand years. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S263 | The kalimah 'La ilaha illa Allah' has twenty-eight letters/qualities and structures the ranks: nafi=Bab, ithbat=Dhu'l-Sayf Da'iyah, with the common letters and the four words mapping to the Imam's hierarchy. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S264 | Between the Originator and the originated is a Word (bayna al-mubdi' wa al-mubda' kalimah), the light of the kalimah 'La ilaha illa Allah,' which is higher than the First Originated. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S265 | The Pillar of Light (amud al-nur) is the knowledge flowing from Allah to the First Intellect, then through each successive intellect down to the Tenth Intellect, the governor of the world of nature. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S266 | Whoever connects with the Pillar of Light through the noble covenant (ahd al-sharif) attains success (fawz) and grace and becomes among the people of mercy; it is the strong rope of Allah and the firmest handhold (al-urwat al-wuthqa) that never breaks. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S267 | The Pillar of Light is spiritual, not physical light; it descends not upon the body but upon the rational soul (nafs natiqah), which is present at the time of the noble covenant. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S268 | The Pillar of Light is a magnet (maghnatis) that attracts the forms of the beneficiaries to the forms of the benefactors, holding and preserving their forms and souls after death. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S269 | From the hypocrite (munafiq), apostate (murtadd), and the one who incurs wrath (maghdub alayhi), the divine form connected to them is snatched away by the appropriate pillar, and their soul returns deficient and base into the body. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S270 | The Pillar of Light preserves and conveys to each person only the portion of food/sustenance (his ma'bar) destined for him, distinguishing it from others and extracting it from wherever it may be; this is divine providence and divine decree. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S271 | The Pillar of Light is reserved for those who take the noble covenant and possess wilayah (devotion), and the wilayah of Mawla Ali is necessary for it. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S272 | It is called the flowing knowledge, the pervading light, the spirit, and the Word; all forms of spiritual and physical existence from the First Intellect to the respondent are connected and joined to it, as in 'a person will be resurrected with whom he loves' (al-mar'u yuhsharu ma'a man ahabba). | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S273 | The forms cling to the Pillar of Light like flies stuck to an extended stick smeared with honey, since it carries, attracts, and elevates them at the time of their ascent. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S274 | When one of the ranks or respondents has intimacy with his spouse, the divine forms turn away and deviate from him, returning only after completion (state of purity is restored by ghusl), as a kindness and mercy from Allah. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S275 | The Prophet said 'in every hair there is janabah' (fi kulli sha'ratin janabah), so one must wash every hair thoroughly in ghusl, and clothes worn during intimacy should not be worn for prayer. | yes | yes | §9 (Man, the Quintessence of the Two Worlds); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S276 | Jibra'il is part of the Pillar of Light; the Natiq of the sixth era (Muhammad) at the beginning of his affair would faint from the greatness of the angel's light when he saw the form named Jibra'il. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S277 | The opponents and disbelievers mistook the Prophet's fainting (al-ghashyah) for jinn-possession or epilepsy (junna/sara'), while Waraqa bin Nawfal, a scholar and soothsayer awaiting the sixth Natiq, recognized it as prophethood. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S278 | Waraqa instructed Khadija to test the visitor: an angel (true Natiq's messenger) turns away from the private parts and would say 'I see nothing,' whereas a jinn would say 'yes' when the head/hair is uncovered. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S279 | Because angels do not look at private parts, a woman's prayer without covering her hair is not accepted; the same principle explains why Amr ibn al-As escaped death by uncovering his private parts before Ali at Siffin. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S280 | The Pillar of Light is cut off when private parts appear before it; the Prophet's connection (ittisal) with the Pillar of Light is established through Jibra'il. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S281 | The Quran alludes to the Pillar of Light in 'He sends down the Spirit (al-ruh) by His command upon whom He wills of His servants'; the Spirit (Pillar of Light) is the reality of revelation (haqiqat al-wahy). | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S282 | Revelation flows in three modes per the verse 'It is not for any human that Allah should speak to him except by wahy, or from behind a veil, or by sending a messenger': these correspond to the ranks of Jadd, Fath, and Khayal. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S283 | Above every Imam (and Natiq, Prophet, Ayn Azimah, Wali) stand three higher ranks, Jadd, Fath, and Khayal, through which divine commands are conveyed; the rank of Jadd is the highest, flowing without intermediary from the Ruh al-Quds. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S284 | The rank of Fath is revelation 'from behind a veil' (min wara'i hijab): perception of things through pure thought and struck parables (amthal madrubah), conveying meaning in veiled, non-public form. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S285 | The rank of Khayal is the angel sent appearing as a well-proportioned man with power flowing from the Abode of Holiness (Dar al-Quds), perceived either as a heard voice or a form seen by the eye. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S286 | The flowing power connecting from Dar al-Quds is the limit flowing from Ruh al-Quds (the First Intellect), forming a luminous chain through which Khayal is heard or seen. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S287 | Sayyidna Ibrahim bin al-Husayn al-Hamidi, the second Da'i, at the passing of Sayyidna Ali bin al-Husayn said 'al-laqab al-sari ta'allaqa bi'l-jari' (the flowing title became attached to the flowing), indicating the deceased was drawn into the Pillar of Light. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S288 | When one connects with the flowing grace he appears to the people of bliss in the manifest presence, the gathering place of pure parts; when the number is fulfilled and the term reached, the next paradise (Abbasid dome) appears at the gate of the Imam's veil (Jannat al-Ma'wa). | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S289 | The ascending rational soul (nafs natiqah) of one with a good end joins and connects its light/virtues to the Pillar of Light according to its portion. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S290 | The virtuous person (second ibda'iyyah) established his mission until he brought forth a son who deputizes in his place, designated al-inbi'ath (the Emanated), counterpart to himself who is designated al-ibda' (the Origination). | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S291 | In the world of nature the second ibda'iyyah (at the Imam's rank) is the counterpart of the First Originated, and his son the first Emanated (munba'ith al-awwal); every rank thereafter is called ibda' and its extracted son inbi'ath. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S292 | After conferring the ruling of Imamate (nass) on his son, the virtuous person passes to the rank of the Tenth Intellect to govern the world of nature, remaining until one like himself succeeds him in that lofty station. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S293 | The intellects ascend successively: the Tenth passes with its subordinates to the rank of the Ninth, the Ninth to the Eighth, and so on up to the Second Intellect (Jannat al-Ma'wa), above which is only the First Intellect, the rank of oneness (Sidrat al-Muntaha) reachable by no other intellect. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S294 | All rewarded souls, spiritual and physical, ascend by this transition over the passing of cycles and aeons (adwar wa akwar), to which the Quran alludes in 'At the Lote-tree of the utmost boundary, near it is the Garden of Abode.' | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S295 | The Prophet said of paradise: 'In paradise there is what no eye has seen, no ear has heard, and what has not occurred to the heart of a human,' for the greatness Allah prepared therein for His friends. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S296 | The Torah reports Allah saying 'Obey me, O son of Adam, I will make you like me: living, you will not die; mighty, you will not be humiliated; rich, you will not become poor.' | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S297 | Only the Qa'im al-Qiyamah, who comes at the last mawr, can pass to the rank of the Tenth at a single stroke because he was originated standing in oneness like the First Originated, becoming the quintessence of all worlds. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S298 | All the lights of the speakers (nutaqa'), foundations (asas/usus), and Imams gather in the Qa'im al-Qiyamah; relative to him every station is a part and he is the whole (kull). | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S299 | The son designated by nass establishes the mission, calls to the oneness of Allah for the salvation of souls drowned in the sea of primeval matter (hayula) and the prison of the physical world, then brings forth his successor and passes to the Tenth's horizon, his duties being only governing the physical world and appointing a successor. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S300 | Souls passing from each rank become stopped (mawquf) with their predecessor until their number completes and their period passes; this stopping-place is the barzakh, to which the Quran alludes: 'And behind them is a barzakh until the day they are resurrected.' | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S301 | The completion of their number is 124,000 forms (surahs) and their period is 7,000 years (the dawr al-kashf); upon completion their Qa'im, extracted from his father's mission, rises and the stopped stations in the barzakh connect with him. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S302 | Each of those stations becomes a limb of the Qa'im's luminous subtle structure (haykal nurani latif), his subtle self being to them like the spirit to the body, so together they form one luminous divine structure and he becomes a holy abstract divine intellect. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S303 | The Natiq of the cycle said 'Indeed for Allah there are 124,000 prophets,' alluding to this noble great structure, and the Quran alludes in 'Say: indeed the first and the last will surely be gathered for the appointed time of a known day.' | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S304 | The 'known day' is the dawr al-kashf, which is 50,000 years, as the Quran says 'in a day whose measure is fifty thousand years'; at the rising of this Qa'im the reckoning, reward, and punishment occur before him. | yes | yes | §10 (The Pillar of Light and the Sixth Speaker); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S305 | Upon completion of every 7,000 years a Qa'im arises like the previous one, continuing until the completion of 50,000 years (the seven Qa'ims of the dawr al-kashf). | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S306 | The governance (tadbir) of the primordial body (jussa ibda'iyyah) of the first mawr extends over the entire kawr, including all natural laws and even partial phenomena like viruses and plagues, which are corrupted particles drawn to one by magnetic affinity from a prior connection. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S307 | Allah forgives His own due (sins against Him), but debts and wrongs between servants must be settled by the servants themselves or compensated through other means. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S308 | When the seventh Qa'im of the dawr al-kashf rises he has power, honor, and grace not possessed by the preceding six, gathering all six Qa'ims with him, since he stands for them in the station of the 'other creation' (khalq al-akhar) which is the spirit to the body and the culmination of the dawr al-kashf. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S309 | Upon the seventh Qa'im's passing the dawr al-sitr begins; his son rises after him, weakness (du'f) enters the mission, the dawr al-kashf reaches its term, and the mission goes into concealment (tasattur), known only secretly among its people through symbols and indications. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S310 | In the dawr al-sitr no apparent knowledge exists except mathematical sciences (engineering, astronomy, and the like), the Imams are in extreme taqiyyah and concealment, and the hands of opponents are dominant for the first 3,000 years. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S311 | The first of the concealment-cycle (mutaqa'idi dawr al-sitr) was Adam (the mustawda' Adam, Abdullah bin Tayjun), established as Natiq by the command of his Imam (Hunayd), each Natiq governing by the command of the Imam of his time. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S312 | Each Natiq establishes laws and a shari'ah whose outward rites are symbols pointing to knowledge of God's spiritual and physical limits (hudud) and His oneness, and appoints a wasi/asas to interpret the inner meaning, the Imamate continuing in succession. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S313 | The succession of Natiqs in the dawr al-sitr: Adam (opponent unnamed), Nuh (Imam Hud, opponent Awj bin Unaq), Ibrahim (Imam Salih, opponent Namrud bin Kan'an), Musa (Imam Ud bin Udad, opponent Fir'awn), Isa (Imam Khuzayma), and Muhammad (Imam Abu Talib, opponents Abu Lahab and Abu Jahl). | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S314 | Every prophet has an opponent (zidd/enemy), as the Prophet said, since every light has a shadow. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S315 | Tawhid, tanzih, and tajrid are distinct: tawhid is recognizing the createdness of all hudud, tanzih is purifying Allah from all attributes and names, and tajrid is abstracting the state of ibda' from all creatures (a state none can perform). | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S316 | The wasis of the Natiqs: Adam's was Shith (after Habil), Nuh's was Sam, Ibrahim's was Isma'il, Musa's was Harun, Isa's was Sham'un al-Safa, and Muhammad's was Ali ibn Abi Talib, who designated Hasan as mustawda' and Husayn as mustaqarr. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S317 | Basis of precedence (awwaliyyat) is not the light but who reaches the homeland (watan) first; despite Natiqs being established first but appearing later, the first remains first. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S318 | The line of Imams ran to Muhammad ibn Isma'il (the seventh, Natiq al-Sadis), then the a'immat al-khulafa' beginning with Abdullah ibn Muhammad ibn Isma'il, completing with al-Mu'izz li-Din-illah; then the dawr al-ashhad beginning with al-Aziz billah, completing with al-Imam al-Tayyib Abi'l-Qasim; then the a'immat al-abdal beginning with the son of al-Tayyib. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S319 | Muhammad ibn Isma'il is the Natiq al-Sadis and gatherer of the six nutaqa'; his rank is very high and his lights are acquired from the Prophet's horizon, from Imam Hasan and Imam Husayn. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S320 | In the Tayyibi era 'Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim' is interpreted of the Imams: Allah=Muhammad ibn Isma'il, the ism=Imam al-Tayyib (21st Imam), and al-Rahman al-Rahim=Ma'add (14th Imam al-Mu'izz) and Ma'add (28th Imam Ahmad). | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S321 | After al-Tayyib's line, the Hujjah of the Qa'im al-Qiyamah rises and the 7,000 years of the dawr al-sitr (the dunya) complete, as the Natiq said 'the age of the dunya is 7,000 years.' | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S322 | The book Anwaar al-Latifah was copied by Sayyidna Da'ud bin Ahmad, the first mansub (buried in Ahmedabad), who marked two places where Sayyidna Ja'far derived proofs (hujjat) by istimbat from the original text. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S323 | The original Natiq al-Sabi' is the Qa'im al-Qiyamah who gathers all six nutaqa'; but because Muhammad ibn Isma'il's rank rose to hijabiyyah (the veil of Ayn Azimah), higher than Natiq al-Sabi', the point of that rank was conferred upon him. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S324 | The Prophet's final station is hijabiyyah (the maqam mahmud), the veil of Ayn Azimah, giving him superiority over all mustawda' and mustaqarr Imams up to the Qa'im. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S325 | The noble stations connect with the Qa'im as the gathered forms near the Bab (bab al-abwab) connect with the soul of the born Imam, his soul becoming a spirit (ruh) for them and forming one luminous structure and holy intellect. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S326 | All people of the dawr al-sitr, first and last, are gathered to the Qa'im, who takes account of each, makes them stand upon their deeds, and personifies every Imam with the people of his cycle, per 'the prophets and witnesses will be brought forward and judged with justice.' | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S327 | The believer will be shown a record (kitab) accounting every small and great deed ('what is this book that leaves neither small nor great but has accounted for it'), and the Qa'im is the Lord who rewards and punishes, the total reward and punishment being established before him. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S328 | Accepted supplications (du'a) in this world are recompensed in the world; supplications not accepted are stored and rewarded at the reckoning, leading the believer to wish that none of his prayers had been hastened ('layta innaka lam tu'ajjil li shay'an'). | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S329 | Every rank (Imam, Bab, Hujjah, Da'i, Ma'dhun) is a partial (juzwi) Qa'im al-Qiyamah for those who return to it, while the rank above is its universal (kulli) Qa'im, ascending until all reach the Qa'im of the entire resurrection, master of the greatest resurrection (qiyamat al-uzma) and the greatest seizure (batshat al-kubra). | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S330 | The perfected form (the luminous intellect / haykal nafsani) is prepared in 7,000 years in the dawr al-kashf (where there is purity and openly manifested wisdom-sciences), whereas the Qa'im al-Qiyamah's form takes 10,000 years in the dawr al-sitr due to density, doubts, turbidity, and abundance of trials. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S331 | In the dawr al-kashf a Qa'im (qiyamah) occurs every 7,000 years (seven Qa'ims), but in the dawr al-sitr one Qa'im comes after 10,000 years (the 7,000 of sitr plus the 3,000 of fatrat), because the 3,000 fatrat years belong to the domain of dawr al-sitr thought. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S332 | Wiping the hand over the face after Bismillah signifies connecting the five lower (adwan) ranks with the five higher (ashraf) ranks; the face's five (intellect, soul, jadd, fath, khayal) are non-repeated (achieved second perfection), while the hands' fives are repeated (manifested in the world: Imam, Bab, Hujjah, Da'i, Ma'dhun). | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 8/8 key terms located in section |
| S333 | All points appearing until the universal resurrection are present in the primordial initial body (jussa ibda'iyyah) of the first mawr; their manifestation occurs in appointed time, and the one holding the mawr governs its qawr until he prepares a successor like himself, whereupon he is elevated. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S334 | When the Imam manifests in the world of intention his Bab manifests with him in a dual capacity (qiran), and ranks subordinate to the Qa'im (jadd, fath, khayal, and his physical kin such as Abd al-Muttalib, Abu Talib, mothers) correspond as a reflection of the original assigned points. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S335 | Man's existence is from two ways: as a plant grown from the earth and through inter-marriage/lineage (munakahat), per 'Allah grew you from the earth as a plant' and 'We created man from an extract of clay, then made him a drop in a firm resting place.' | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S336 | The formal apparitions (ashbah suwariyyah), having fallen into the three dimensions through their slip and error, became distant, dense, and turbid; divine providence dispersed (tabdid) them into the spheres and elements to purify them through trials, as gold is purified by fire. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S337 | After punishment and trial, divine providence gathers what was prepared and manifests it as the gnostic human form (qamat al-arafiyyah) from the earth as a plant, then by a second, faster, closer way encompasses the three kingdoms (mineral, plant, animal) as food. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S338 | The primordial body eats these foods, taking the subtle and discarding the dense, making them flesh and blood combining the four natures (the four humors); the body so formed is a physical veil over the primordial body. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S339 | At intimacy between man and woman the subtlest extract descends from both bodies, ascends to the brain to be refined and whitened (from dense blood drawn from every organ), then descends from both into the womb to become a single entity gathering the four natures. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S340 | Within that single entity is a complete vegetative life hidden like fire in flint-wood, named tabi'ah, the primordial leaven (khamirah ibda'iyyah), the vegetative soul (nafs namiyyah), and the (growing) form (surah). | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S341 | When the entity is balanced in temperament and given permission to manifest, it settles in the pure balanced womb, faced by the ascendant, descendant, tenth, and fourth astrological houses by the decree of the cosmos, fermenting like rennet and milk becoming cheese. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S342 | The first organ formed from the drop (nutfah) is the heart (the point of the compass, nuqtat al-birkar), whose nature is the sun's action (heat and dryness); it attracts the subtle entity and becomes its house and center, after which the remaining organs form. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S343 | The fathers (planets/kawakib) act upon the forming organs and the mothers (wombs/spheres) nurse them; each organ receives its share of nourishment through its mother's corresponding organ, missing nothing. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S344 | The sun, heart of the celestial sphere, provides aid to what is above and below it step by step; likewise the sun of the world of religion, the Natiq, gives the divine sciences and angelic confirmations to the hudud above and below him. | yes | yes | §11 (The Cycles of Concealment and Unveiling); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S345 | Each of the seven planets governs (tadbir) the fetus for one month in sequence (Zuhal, Mushtari, Mirrikh, Shams, Zuhra, Utarid, Qamar), so it is born complete in the seventh month under the moon's governance. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S346 | The heart's formation and movement occur in the fourth month under the sun's (Shams) governance, which is why the fetal heartbeat is felt then. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S347 | If the child is not born in the seventh month it remains until the eighth, when governance returns to Zuhal (the inauspicious star, nahs al-falak) whose action is coldness and dryness (the nature of death), so a child born in the eighth month appears dead. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S348 | If the child remains until the ninth month it comes under Mushtari (the greater good fortune, sa'd al-akbar) whose action is heat and moisture (the nature of life), so it is born alive; the nayyirayn are Shams and Qamar, the sa'dayn are Mushtari and Zuhra, and Utarid is the scribe of the sphere (katib al-falak). | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S349 | Amir al-Mu'minin Ali defined the nafs al-namiyya (vegetative soul) as a natural power (quwwah tabi'iyyah) whose origin is the four natures, whose union is at the deposit of the drop, whose substance is from refined foods, whose action is growth and increase, and whose separation is caused by disharmony of materials, returning to the moon whence it began. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S350 | Everything beneath the sphere of the moon down to the point of the earth (the rock, sakhrah) is the Greater Vegetative Soul (namiyyat al-kubra), from which every lesser vegetative soul branches; the lesser vegetative soul is in reality the animal (hayawan). | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S351 | The Greater Sensory Soul (hissiyyat al-kubra) extends from the encompassing sphere (muhit) to the sphere of the moon; the lesser sensory soul (hissiyyat al-sughra) is the rational obligated human soul; the greater rational souls are the Hudud, and the lesser rational soul is the respondent (mustajib). | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S352 | These realities were never disclosed except by allusion (ramz) and from a tongue to an ear, but the author wrote them plainly without symbol or ta'wil at the questioner's request, in the language of haqa'iq. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S353 | When the newborn appears into the expanse of the earth a sensory power connects with it from the rays of the sun (heart of the sphere, center of the sensory), spreading through the body, sending movement, and uniting with the vegetative soul to become one thing nourished by the temperate breeze (nasim). | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S354 | Ali defined the nafs al-hissiyya as a celestial power (quwwah falakiyyah) whose origin is the spheres, union at physical birth, seat the heart, action sensation/movement/assault/victory, separation by disharmony of materials, returning to the sun whence it began. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S355 | Sayyidna al-Mu'ayyad (Hibatullah ibn Musa al-Shirazi, Bab al-Abwab) in his Majalis admonished: 'I wish I knew why you were drawn out from the spheres and bodies and made to descend stage by stage into the narrow places of the loins and wombs and brought out into the expanse of this abode.' | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S356 | The nutfah enfolds the vegetative soul drawn from terrestrial nourishment of the three kingdoms (mineral, plant, animal), which is drawn from the four mothers (fire, air, water, earth), and these four mothers are the powers of the celestial spheres. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S357 | Allah created only spiritual essences (zawat ruhaniyya / ashbah nuraniyya), granting them existence, knowledge, life, and choice (ikhtiyar); those who misused their choice fell stage by stage into nufus, then sensory and vegetative souls, then bodies, animals, plants, and minerals, all being fallen spiritual essences. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S358 | Salvation (najat) is the return of all things to being luminous specters (ashbah nuraniyya); the units of a person's various fallen parts are held by the Governor (Mudabbir) and gathered at the Qa'im, then at greater Qa'ims, ascending ultimately toward the Ashir and the Jannat al-Ma'wa, none reaching Sidrat al-Muntaha. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S359 | God created the essences in the human form (shukhusan ala shakli al-insan) before man existed; the one who arrived first occupies the station of the heart/head and the one who came last the station of the feet, and they ascend in that same human-form arrangement. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S360 | The human form has 28 parts corresponding to the 28 letters of 'La ilaha illa Allah': the head is 'illa Allah,' the body is 'La ilaha,' with three parts, four parts, seven parts, and twelve apertures (eyes, ears, nostrils, mouth, nipples, navel, chest openings, lower orifices). | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S361 | Sayyidna al-Mu'ayyad in Taj al-Aqa'id refutes that God created out of need, to take work, or to be worshipped, concluding that the cause of creation is unknowable except that God by His grace (fadl) and kindness brought existence from non-existence. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S362 | The newborn learns speech from its parents through babbling (munaghat) until its speech matures, and learning the Book of Allah increases its speech and perfection. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S363 | When the person connects with the true teacher (mu'allim al-sadiq), is given longing to enter the circle of the people of truth, takes the noble covenant (ahd al-karim) through the Imam of the time, and enters the secure sanctuary of Allah, a point of light neighbors his soul and connects with the Pillar of Light flowing from the Imam of the time. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S364 | The more one benefits from the knowledge, gnosis, and love of the Imam and his hudud and walks in chastity, purity, and obedience to the Wali al-Amr, the more the point of light increases in vastness and light and the soul's illumination and substance increase. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S365 | The luminous point is a form (surah) which is the rational soul (nafs natiqah) imaged by the beneficiary from his benefactor; Ali defined it as an intellectual power (quwwah ilmiyyah), a transparent substance knowing in potentiality and actuality, living by nature. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S366 | Once the nafs natiqah is formed it cannot be destroyed, per the principle 'evil has no origin in the primary creation' (al-sharr la asla lahu fi'l-ibda'): evil, lying, darkness, and coarseness are not things but the absence/distance from good, truth, light, and subtlety. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S367 | Allah created only truth and sustenance; man himself created the body and turned sustenance into excretions and falsehood, so man is the cause of his own coarseness, which is removed by progressing on the path. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S368 | The spirit (ruh) is 'from the command of my Lord' (qul al-ruhu min amri rabbi); ruh is also used for the nafs natiqah, and when the nafs natiqah ascends high the command of the Lord (amr-i rabb) is at work. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S369 | The subtle nafs natiqah, by abiding with the coarse nafs hissiyya, gradually transforms it into itself, which is salvation, as a stone takes on fragrance from prolonged company with perfume. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S370 | Ali defined the nafs al-kulliyyah (Universal Soul) as a divine power (quwwah ilahiyyah), a widespread substance knowing in potentiality and actuality, whose origin is the (First) Intellect, from which it takes support and toward which it calls, its union being at the religious birth (ahd al-sharif) and its seat being knowledge and reality. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S371 | Each soul returns to its origin: the nafs namiyya to the moon, the nafs hissiyya to the sun, the nafs natiqah to the Pillar of Light / world of life, and the nafs al-kulliyyah to the Intellect / world of lights; the lower's origin is always the rank above it. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S372 | The nafs al-kulliyyah is the supreme Word of Allah (kalimatullah al-ulya), the tree of Tuba (shajarat al-tuba), and the Garden of Abode (Jannat al-Ma'wa) near the Sidrat al-Muntaha (the First Intellect); whoever knows it precedes and is saved, whoever is ignorant of it is misguided. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S373 | Every station in the world of religion corresponds to the First Intellect (named Sidrat al-Muntaha) and every Imam to it; the Bab corresponds to the First Emanated (muba'ath al-awwal, named Jannat al-Ma'wa), so Ali alluded to the Bab by the nafs al-kulliyyah and to Jannat al-Ma'wa by the muba'ath al-awwal. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S374 | The Pillar of Light flows from the Bab to the Hujjah, to the Da'i, to the Ma'dhun, to the believer (mu'min), and to the respondent (mustajib) who takes the noble covenant. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S375 | Ali ibn Abi Talib founded Arabic grammar by explaining to Abu'l-Aswad al-Du'ali that the 28 letters form meaningful words (kalimah) of three kinds, ism (noun), fi'l (verb), and harf (particle), corresponding to luminous essences, their intentions, and the relation between them. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S376 | The grammatical rules mirror the luminous world: every subject (fa'il) is raised (marfu') because it acts and advances foremost, every object (maf'ul) is established (mansub), and every preposition-governed noun (majrur) is broken (maksur). | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S377 | Arabic is the original, still-existing language because the foundation of 'La ilaha illa Allah' rests on its 28 letters, which encode all the laws, and Allah revealed the Quran in 'a clear Arabic tongue.' | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S378 | At the time of a respondent's departure, the Tenth Intellect (receiving the command, linked to the Imam) moves the Pillar of Light named 'the flowing' (al-jari), and the movement connects to the Imam of the time, who knows it signals the soul's purification and time of transfer. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S379 | The Imam moves the Pillar of Light and attracts the departing one toward himself; the luminous form (nafs natiqah) is drawn by the pillar's attraction, and the neighboring sensory soul (nafs hissiyya) is drawn along with it to the extent it obeyed the rational soul in the world, in the form of the universal body, leaving nothing small or great. | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S380 | Souls cannot pass through the regions of heaven and earth except by an authority (sultan), which is the noble form (haykal) of the Imam where all forms gather, per 'you will not pass through except by an authority.' | yes | yes | §12 (The Souls and the Planetary Spirits); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S381 | The first chapter of Mabda' wa-Ma'ad contains five principles, with the sixth being a debate and the seventh expounding the reality of the fifth. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S382 | At the moment the sensory soul (nafs-e-hissiyah) is drawn out of its body, it mixes (tamtaziju) and merges with its spiritual form and becomes connected to the rational soul (natiqah). | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S383 | After this merging the soul and its form become one single thing, one essence, and a radiant, angelic, Gabrielic light (nuran sha'sha'aniyan malakutiyan jibriliyan). | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S384 | In life the soul and its form are merely adjacent (mujawir) while in death they become intermixed (mumazij) — a major difference. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S385 | The departing soul becomes a luminous substance (jawhar-e-nurani) that is living, powerful, knowing, speaking, hearing, seeing, smelling, and tasting — these being the qualities of the sensory soul. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 8/9 key terms located in section |
| S386 | The substance is called luminous because it has become one with the rational soul (nafs-e-natiqah), the qualities of the natiqah having entered it. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S387 | Once the soul separates, the body remains a corpse devoid of perception and feeling, because all its powers (life, light, power, knowledge, speech) were due to the soul. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S388 | Only the soul and form of one whose end was good (khatima bil-khayr, on the Sirat al-Mustaqim) become pure; the process described applies to such a purified, righteous soul. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S389 | The purified lower soul-and-form unite and then intermix with the higher luminous form of their Exalted Limit (hadde aali), becoming one essence with it while remaining adjacent to its sensory soul. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S390 | The Exalted Limit (hadde aali) does not die but remains alive, so the believer's purified soul attaches to a living higher being. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S391 | The Arabic poetic line about the sun (shams) being grammatically feminine and the moon (qamar) masculine illustrates that grammatical gender does not diminish the sun's superiority, since the sun's rays make the moon luminous. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S392 | The moon gathers little by little the aeolian souls (nufus-e-rihiyyah) carried in the sun's light, and the moon of the fourteenth night's brilliant fullness is the result of this accumulated light from those souls. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S393 | The aeolian souls (nufus-e-rihiyyah) initially carry impurities (kudurat); the moon's early dense light comes from these impure souls, and purification then progressively increases their luminosity. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S394 | Fasting on the white nights (ayyam al-biz: the 13th, 14th, and 15th) is recommended (mustahabb). | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S395 | After the full moon the moon returns the subtle vapor (bukhar-e-latif / nufus-e-rihiyyah) to the sun bit by bit (shay'an ba'da shay'in) via the stations of Mercury ('utarid) and Venus (zuhra). | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S396 | The celestial ordering from lowest to highest is moon, then Venus, then Mercury, then the sun. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S397 | The bodily corporeality (jismaniyyat) acquired by the aeolian souls during burial with the body is what is purified through the planetary stations, the heat removing accumulated denseness like cleaning trash from a house. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S398 | Instead of hellfire's heat, the souls being purified are given the sun's heat, though hell's heat is a thousand times greater. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S399 | The lunar cycle is mapped to ascent and descent: from the 1st to the 12th is ascending, the 15th is the zenith (intila'), and from the 16th the vapors are delivered down to the world. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S400 | When the aeolian souls reach the precinct (hayyiz) of the sun, the sun's light is the quintessence (khulasa) of the heat of the subtle instinctive bodies (ajsam-e-ghariziyyah), serving as vehicle and spouse of sensory life and center of the instinctive heat (hararat-e-ghariziyyah). | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S401 | The sun refines the souls by making them sweat ('araq), breaking them down, and fermenting them until they are fit for the door of the totality of subtle, spiritual, noble forms. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S402 | The sun delivers the purified whole up to Jupiter (mushtari), the Greater Fortune (Sa'd al-Akbar), and to Venus (zuhra), the Lesser Fortune (Sa'd al-Asghar), Mercury being the scribe of the heavens (katib-e-falak), until it is a pure vessel prepared for the command of the Imam. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S403 | The rays of the two Fortunes deposit the purified spiritualities onto fruits eaten or waters drunk (like dew) that become the food of the Imam, the moon, and the sun, guarded by divine providence until it reaches the Near Station (maqam-e-qarib). | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S404 | The Prophet's birth is said to have come from the apple of paradise or from a spring of water (dew) divinely indicated to be drunk, after which he was born from Sayyidatuna Amina as the Seal of the Messengers (khatim-ul-mursalin). | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S405 | The Imam of the Time (Master of the Era and the 'asr) and his pure spouse take the descended subtle vapor as their food, and from it the wife's purified quintessence (sulalah) forms. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S406 | Sulalah denotes the purified parts placed in the father's loins and the mother's womb; when both combine a nutfah (sperm-drop) is formed, this being the stage of sulalah. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S407 | At the moment of conception the Imam (the Station) knows by Allah's decree the reality of the coming child and is filled with indescribable joy. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 0/0 key terms located in section |
| S408 | At that time the Imam gives good tidings of the coming successor to the deputy of the station who will succeed and return to him, alluding to the child's matter. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 0/0 key terms located in section |
| S409 | The deputies of deputies are gathered with the purified matter so it becomes a single whole ascending in ranks, from which the fetus's food is made and on which it feeds and grows in the womb. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 0/0 key terms located in section |
| S410 | The Imam's body in the womb is nourished not by menstrual blood but by the pure purified aeolian whole, making it free from the impurity (tams) that affects other fetuses. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S411 | The verse 'Indeed Allah desires to remove all impurity from you, O People of the House, and to purify you' (Innama yuridu-Llahu liyudhhiba 'ankumu-r-rijsa Ahl al-Bayt wa-yutahhirakum tathira) is cited as scriptural proof of the Ahl al-Bayt's freedom from impurity. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S412 | Membership in the Ahl al-Bayt requires being free from the impurity of tams (menstrual-blood nourishment) and not consuming the wealth of charity (sadaqah). | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S413 | The hadith 'sadaqah is not permissible for the Ahl al-Bayt' (sadaqatun la tahillu li-Ahl al-Bayt) is anchored to the Prophet removing a date from young Imam Hasan's mouth, demonstrating the prohibition. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S414 | The Prophet's wives claimed Ahl al-Bayt status yet consumed sadaqah (and 'Aisha demanded treasury wealth), so by the sadaqah criterion they cannot be reconciled with true Ahl al-Bayt membership. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S415 | A believer's purified growth-soul (nafs-e-namiyyah) and sensory-soul (nafs-e-hissiyyah) go into the Imam and meet his rational, growth, and sensory souls; the believer's part becomes the spirit (ruh / nasut) from which the Imam's son's body is formed. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S416 | Upon the begetting of the Imam's son three streams — the believer's growth-soul, sensory-soul, and form (surah) — converge and gather in one ocean and connect with the nasut, having been separated only so they could reunite. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S417 | The order of ascent of the believer's faculties is: the rational soul (nafs-e-natiqah) first and most superior, then the sensory soul (nafs-e-hissiyyah), while the growth soul (nafs-e-namiyyah), having a defect, ascends later through perfection. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S418 | Whereas a human body's parts are fire, air, water, and earth, the Imam's son's body is formed from the growth-souls (nufus-e-namiyyah), and its growth-soul is the single most subtle part ascended from the entire universe. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S419 | On the fourth month the growth-soul (nafs-e-namiyyah) — the most subtle ascended part — is obtained from the sun and connects with the developing whole, becoming its nafs. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S420 | When the whole completes its term in the womb a complete, knowing, powerful, subtle, transparent man appears with the five senses, a noble disembodied (mujarrad) substance (jawhar-e-latif) whose faculties are pure. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S421 | The believer's parts are already fully processed and purified in the celestial spheres before entering the Imam's nasut; the purification process ends there, with only give-and-take continuing once in the world. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S422 | When the born one (mawlud) reaches the four years of upbringing (sini al-tarbiyah), the inner gate (baab-e-baatin) of his father transfers to him. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S423 | Grammatically, 'iza' before a past-tense verb conveys present-tense meaning, and sini (from sinin, original sanun) drops its final nun because it is in genitive construct (mudaf ilayh). | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S424 | The baab is appointed by the Imam (the son's father); the father has two gates — an outer gate (baab-e-zaahir) connected to the inner gate (baab-e-baatin), and the inner gate connected to the Imam's son. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S425 | The son of the Imam has no baab-ul-abwaab of his own; instead his heart (qalb) becomes a temple (haykal), and the father's baab-ul-abwaab with its gathered forms (suwar) and souls (nufus) becomes connected to him. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S426 | It is a general rule (kulliyah) that the baab's transfer and the gathered forms and souls (al-mustakhrajah) connect to the soul of the born one, who is thereby the Imam. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S427 | From birth an Imam is an Imam, but the general rule is that designation (nass) — which may be manifest (jali) or hidden (khafi) — usually occurs upon the Imam's son at four years of age. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S428 | When the son becomes the Imam of the Time (Imam-e-Zamana) his father's rank is elevated, and the new Imam gradually takes charge with jadhb (attraction), fath (opening), khayal (imagination), and all ishraqat (illuminations). | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S429 | The Prophet's saying 'Al-Hasan and Al-Husayn are two Imams whether they rise or sit' (Al-Hasanu wal-Husaynu imamaani qaama aw qa'ada) was said when they were around four years old, evidencing the four-year connection of their special forms and gates. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S430 | The gathered forms and souls become a thousandfold luminous human glorious structure (haykal al-alfi an-nurani al-insani), and the Imam's soul becomes the spirit (ruh) for this haykal. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S431 | The haykal of gathered forms is the lahut, which connects with the nasut (the mawlud descended from the moon); lahut is the forms-and-souls (suwarat wa nufus) and nasut is the born body. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S432 | In the haykal the luminous human form is constituted in the shape of a body (hay'at al-jism) with all limbs, nerves, veins, instruments, head, eye, ear, nose, mouth, hand, and foot — made of luminous apparitions (ashbah nurani). | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S433 | Each form moving in the haykal and its attracting sensory soul (nafs-e-hissiyah) are one and the same thing and one kind upon the form of the body, leaving out nothing small or large. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S434 | Due to the gathering of the insights (basa'ir) of the brothers (ikhwan) in the noble luminous assembly, each soul's life, strength, power, knowledge, sharpness, intelligence, hearing, and sight become many times greater than in its singularity. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/4 key terms located in section |
| S435 | The one who makes that spirit flow through all of them is the soul of the designated Imam (al-mansus 'alayhi), and their reaching that high rank is the paradise (jannah) of this world attained in the haykal. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S436 | The divine light's connection with these souls is a total connection (ittisalan kulliyan), and the connection of the subtle entities with the soul of the Imam occurs after the completion of four years because religion is a support for the heart (ad-dini da'amatan lil-qalb). | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S437 | A seeker of proof (mustadill) is only called a mustadill after knowing the Speaker (Naatiq), the Legatee (Wasi), the Imam of the Age (Imam al-'Asr), and the Proof (Hujjah) — the four things taught first in the religious sciences (haqaiq), also stated after four years. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S438 | Each of the four ranks (hudud al-arba'ah) is attributed to one year of the four, so the connection of the subtle entities cannot occur except after the completion of four years, named the age of upbringing (sinn al-tarbiyah). | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S439 | The exotericists (Ahl al-Zahir) do not pronounce a ruling on a newborn's age until at least four years have passed, which they too name the years of upbringing. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S440 | The prophet who brought the science of the stars ('ilm an-nujum) was Idris, peace be upon him, and his establishing it was a miracle (mu'jizah) for the people of his time. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S441 | Idris devised the science of the stars because he lived in a time of precautionary dissimulation and concealment (taqiyyah wa sitr). | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S442 | When the Imam of Idris's time died having willed the Imamate to Idris, the people did not accept it; Idris argued that Allah's justice cannot leave His earth empty of His Proof (Hujjah), and they agreed. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S443 | Idris taught the people astronomy and had them repeatedly divide the earth into quarters (arba'an), each time finding God's gaze upon the quarter they were in, until the division led to Idris himself, whom they then acknowledged as the Proof and witness of God. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S444 | A narration of Amir al-Mu'minin Ali in Kufa relates that, asked the whereabouts of Jibra'il, he searched all directions and the seven earths and finding him nowhere concluded the questioner himself was Jibra'il, who then ascended. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S445 | Praise is given to God who specified His friends (awliya) with the secrets of His vicegerency in the knowledge of the Imam, making them upholders (quwwam) of the gnosis of His oneness (ma'arif al-tawhid) and treasurers (khuzzan) of the depths of His wisdom. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 6/7 key terms located in section |
| S446 | The sciences of the Imams operate through symbols (rumuz) and indications (isharat), as in the recorded conversations of Dawud, Musa, and Sulayman, understood only by the speaker and the intended hearer. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S447 | Allah created numbers and placed the arrangement of everything in numbers, so that everything (e.g. derivations from 'La ilaha illa Allah') is related to numbers. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 1/1 key terms located in section |
