| S448 | The sciences of concealment (sitr) are those that were manifest in the time of unveiling (kashf) but, since the time of Baqiyyat al-suwakh, have been expressed in a hidden (makhfi) manner. | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S449 | The chapter closes with blessings upon Muhammad the Prophet and master of the legatees and upon their family, who are the pure and most noble Imams (a'immat at-tayyibin al-akramin). | yes | yes | §13 (The Soul's Ascent through the Ranks of Nature); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S450 | At death the Nafse Namiyah of believers travels with the senses in the form of rihyat from the moon (qamar) toward the sun (shams), then through the kawakib and aflak, descending onto things fire does not touch. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S451 | The Imam and his household consume the descended rihyat, and from it the Imam's offspring are formed, their bodies taking shape from rihyat and their Nafse Namiyah from the sun's subtleties. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S452 | When the Imam's child reaches four years, the gathered forms and souls of the believers reach the Babul Abwab in order and connect with the nafs of the Imam's child, while the Imam's form becomes a ruh. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S453 | The human form at the Babul Abwab becomes the haykal, and the Babul Abwab becomes the qalbe haykal, with each rank retained at its proper station. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S454 | When the Qaimul Qiyam (Imam of the Imams) comes, a similar grand gathering occurs forming qawaim of qawaim and oceans of oceans, including all departed believers. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S455 | Every Imam is brought out (istikhraj) from the da'wat of his father, each establishing his da'wat until he extracts a successor, continuing forever in the Daur al-Kashf and Daur as-Satr. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S456 | In the Daur al-Kashf the esoteric/real sciences appear and are read in gatherings and from pulpits, with no enforcement of the apparent (zahir) ordinances. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S457 | Lahut cannot manifest without Nasut, and the Nasut-e-tabi'i (in which relationships exist but the five senses do not) is necessary for the manifestation of the relationships of the khuddam and the Imam. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S458 | Although Shari'ah injunctions are not enforced in the Daur al-Kashf, its people are so pious that their voluntary acts (tatawwu'at) of prayer, fasting, and charity exceed our obligatory duties (fara'id). | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S459 | In the Daur as-Satr the da'wat is of two kinds: an esoteric hidden da'wat (batinah khafiyyah) and a manifest clear da'wat (zahirah jaliyyah). | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S460 | The esoteric hidden da'wat is the division of the divine stations (maqamat ilahiyyah), luminous structures (hayakil nuraniyyah), and their abwab, hujaj, du'at, and ma'dhun. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S461 | In the era of Adam the people of the esoteric da'wat were in extreme concealment (istitar) out of fear of opponents, pharaohs, and adversaries due to scarcity of affinity, as the progeny of Qabil gained dominance and Habil's progeny went into concealment. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S462 | The manifest du'at openly call toward the Imam of the time because of the proximity of affinity between them and the rest of the world, and they extract those with sound guidance toward the esoteric da'wat of their masters. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S463 | The du'at act under the command of the Imams, take responsibility from them, speak by their word, recognize the loftiness of their stations, and continue thus through all eras. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S464 | Sayyidna Ali (cited as Ali bin al-Husayn al-Jamali) said in a khutbah, 'I am nothing but like a pen in the hand of the Imam,' as a pen in the hand of a scribe. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S465 | When Adam's era completed, Nuh rose as Natiq with his wasi Sam, then the Imams of his era and their du'at, until Ibrahim rose as Natiq. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S466 | Ibrahim was a confluence (majma') of the forms of the people of the two preceding eras (Adam and Nuh), unlike Adam and Nuh who were themselves da'i for that confluence. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S467 | When a Natiq abrogates his Shari'ah, the emergence of fudala' necessitates changing some Shari'ah laws (concerning halal/haram), while the worship and commands of Allah remain unchangeable, anchored in 'Innad-dina indallahil-Islam'. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S468 | Ibrahim is a luminous structure (haykal nurani) who attained all three ranks, with Adam standing in the station of tanzil, Nuh opposite ta'wil, and Ibrahim opposite the haqa'iq, anchored in the Quranic 'wa Ibrahimalladhi waffa'. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S469 | Ibrahim is the son of the preceding Imam Salih, the one who brought him into being and pointed toward him, and in him were gathered prophethood, messengership, successorship, and imamate. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S470 | The Daur al-Fitrah is the last 3,000 years of the 50,000-year Daur al-Kashf, in which two kinds of people emerged: bearers of the sciences of realities and bearers of the apparent sciences. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S471 | The total count is 57,000 years (50,000 Daur al-Kashf plus 7,000 Daur as-Satr) multiplied to reach 400,000 years, and the present is the last part of this first 400,000-year dawr. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S472 | Adam began the Daur as-Satr after the Daur al-Fitrah; in the Daur al-Fitrah there were prophets, and the era of priesthood, astrology, and sorcery was at its peak. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S473 | The Qaim of the Daur al-Kashf is Imam Hunaid, who became the Hijab-e-A'la (Highest Veil). | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S474 | With the nutaqa' the people of their eras gather as a gathering of forms, but no haykal is formed; the term haykal applies only when all forms gather at the Babul Abwab to form the complete human form (form of alif). | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S475 | The ten ranks ascend in order: Mustajib, Momin, Mukasir, Ma'dhun, Da'i, Hujjat, Bab, then Imam at the eighth rank, with Wasayat and divine speech (nutq-e-ilahi) above imamate. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 9/9 key terms located in section |
| S476 | The bearers of divine speech (nutq-e-ilahi) are the three Ha's: Hunaid, Ibrahim, and Hashim, with the Prophet in the second rank, distinguishing a Natiq from a divine Natiq. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S477 | Allah created from non-existence the intellects (uqul), luminous specters (ashbah nuraniyah), and essences (zawat), not forms, and the word nafs was not yet used for them. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S478 | Allah bestowed three things upon the created intellects: life/existence (hayat), knowledge (ilm), and free will (ikhtiyar) — gifts that no one can take away and present in every human. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S479 | The tenth intellect (ashir) delayed in using its free will due to corrupt imagination (wahm-e-fasid), descending from intellect into the form of a nafs, anchored in 'Ya ayyatuhan-nafsul-mutma'innah... irji'i'. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S480 | The word nafs (and nufus) originated only from the tenth intellect (ashir); when he later returned to the form of intellect he became the 'Aql-e-Kulli (Universal Intellect) and attained flawless perfection. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S481 | The Nafse Kulli is the ashir (also called Nafse Mutma'innah); the souls of the Imams are also called Nufuse Kulli because they immediately accepted the call of the ashir and gained a station in the realm of life. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S482 | The center of the Nafse Hissiyah is the sun, the center of the Nafse Namiyah is the moon, and the Nafse Kulli is in the dimensionless realm of life encompassing all, with effects flowing into the dimensional world. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S483 | Through further descent the nafs becomes a body (jism); the body was a nafs and the nafs was an intellect, showing the chain of descent. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S484 | The Nafse Natiqa is the spiritual form of knowledge and action created by one's own essence (which Allah created), making it the believer's real acquisition through free will. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S485 | A non-believer (ghair-e-momin) is a fallen physical form of a believer who has a soul but not a Nafse Natiqa, lacking the form of knowledge and action established through the 'Ahd-e-Sharif. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S486 | The essence is all intellect; soul and body are merely changed forms of intellect, and the return journey ascends in the reverse order of descent (body first, then soul). | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S487 | At the 'Ahd-e-Sharif one receives the Nafse Natiqa as the khameer-e-nizamat performed by one's predecessor a thousand years earlier, who planted a seed now obtained by the successor. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S488 | When the four ranks gathered in Ibrahim, he established his da'wat until he brought forth his son Isma'il, though his son Ishaq had been born before Isma'il. | yes | yes | §14 (The Cycle of Unveiling and the House of Ibrahim); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S489 | Ibrahim gathered both the eras of Adam and Nuh — all individuals who had entered the haykal of the Natiq — and the four ranks (Nubuwwah, Risalah, Wisayah, Imamah) gathered in him. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S490 | There are two Ibrahims: Ibrahim the Natiq 'alladhi waffa', and a man named Ibrahim who was a Nabi of his time, mentioned in the Quran as 'kana rajulun yuqalu lahu Ibrahim'. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S491 | The thalathaha (three) — Maulana Hashim, Maulana Hunayn, Maulana Ibrahim — hold a special khilafah and rank among the khilafats of the Qa'im. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S492 | The wazir of the Qa'im is 'Ayn 'Azim, whose khilafah is taken by Mim ('Abd al-Muttalib, then Abu Talib, then 'Abdullah), with two qiran and then Mim Jadd al-Qa'im, and Fatimah as khalifah of Mim. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S493 | The mutimmin of the Dawr-e-Muhammadi and every seventh Imam (Muhammad bin Isma'il, Ma'add al-Mu'izz, Imam al-Tayyib, the 28th Maulana Ma'add, 22nd Maulana Ahmad, 42nd Maulana Ali, 49th Abu al-Qa'im) hold a very high rank. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S494 | Hazrat Nasrullah Sahib taught that the atimmah of the Prophet's dawr reached so high a rank that a qiran (equality) occurred with the sevenths, making them sometimes equal in taking khilafah. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S495 | Among the atimmah, Maulana Muhammad bin Isma'il holds the highest rank (gathered with a personality like Imam Husayn), established in the dawr-e-kashf, and thus does not fall into the category of the seventh class. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S496 | Of the seven, four are greater (Muhammad bin Isma'il, Imam al-Tayyib, Maulana Ahmad, Abu al-Qa'im), three gathering two ranks each, while Muhammad bin Isma'il alone holds the special rank of sahib-e-dawr-e-kashf, anchored in Surat al-Ikhlas. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S497 | The Qa'im's name is 'Ali, and Sayyidna referred to him as Abu 'Ali in his poetic responses. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S498 | Because Anwar is a foundational book from which most Du'at take proofs, it places Wisayah before Imamah (unlike most books), and Wisayah is the higher rank. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S499 | Ibrahim established Isma'il as Wasi entrusting him Wisayah and Imamah by Allah's command because he is a maqam ilahi and haykal nurani, while entrusting Ishaq the ranks of Nubuwwah and Risalah and making him a khadim, hijab, and da'i to Isma'il. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S500 | Isma'il and his progeny are divine stations (maqamat ilahiyyah) and possessors of luminous temples (the people of permanence, ahl al-istiqrar), while Ishaq and his progeny are proofs and manifest summoners (hujaj and du'at zahirun) and veils upon them. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S501 | The chain of Du'at and hujub (manifest summoners) ran from Ishaq through Ya'qub, Yusuf, and the named prophets down to 'Isa, then Sham'un, 'Abd al-Masih, and Bahira al-Rahib — all the ahl al-istida' tracing their lineage back to Ishaq. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S502 | When the Prophet was manifested, the rank of Nabuwwah that had been with Bahira the monk was taken by Ubayy ibn Ka'b. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S503 | Hashim (Hasham al-sarid) gathered the four ranks and gave them completely to 'Abd al-Muttalib, who divided them: Nubuwwah, Risalah, Imamah, Wisayah to 'Abdullah, and a second part to Abu Talib; when 'Abdullah died all four gathered in Abu Talib. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S504 | Abu Talib gave Nubuwwah and Risalah to the Prophet and Imamah and Wisayah to 'Ali, but since they were young, all ranks gathered in the Prophet through the kafalat-e-'uzma (great guardianship), whose power overshadowed all the mustawda'in and mustaqarrin gathered with 'Ali. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S505 | The Qa'im of the progeny of Isma'il is 'Ali and the Qa'im of the progeny of Ishaq is the Prophet, but the succession occurred with the Prophet, who gathers them when joined with 'Ayn 'Azima. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S506 | The first of the A'immah (divine stations) after Ibrahim was Isma'il ibn Ibrahim, entrusting to Qaydar, with a long named chain of fathers-to-sons down to 'Udad bin 'Udad whose muqim was Musa. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S507 | When the time bound the manifestation of a Natiq, 'Udad established Musa from among his Du'at, and the chain continued through 'Adnan, Ma'add, Nizar, Mudar, Ilyas, Mudrikah, Khuzaymah, whose da'i was commanded to establish 'Isa. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S508 | The muqim of Adam is Maulana Hunayn, of Nuh is Maulana Hud, of Ibrahim is Maulana Salih, of Musa is 'Udad bin 'Udad, and of 'Isa is Khuzaymah. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S509 | The chain continued from Khuzaymah through Kinanah, Nadr, Malik, Fihr, Ghalib, Lu'ayy, Ka'b, Murrah, Kilab, Qusayy, 'Abd Manaf, Hashim, to 'Abd al-Muttalib, with whom the four ranks (Wisayah, Imamah, Nabuwwah, Risalah) gathered as they had with Ibrahim. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S510 | When the four ranks gathered with 'Abd al-Muttalib, two (Wisayah and Imamah) were reserved for the Qa'im of Isma'il's progeny and two (Nubuwwah and Risalah) for the Qa'im of Ishaq's progeny, and Imamah and Wisayah are never separated. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S511 | From Allah's justice and grace, Muhammad became a divine station (maqam ilahi) and luminous structure (haykal nurani), which is impossible unless one's father is likewise a haykal nurani; thus 'Abdullah being a haykal nurani is the precondition for Muhammad being one. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S512 | Because the Prophet received the great guardianship, despite bearing only Nubuwwah and Risalah he achieved superiority over all ranks gathered with 'Ali, becoming the deputy of 'Ayn, with no rank of the Qa'im remaining except the thalathah sukra (the three intoxicated ones). | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S513 | After Maulana 'Abd al-Muttalib, the haykal-nurani condition requires that an Imam's father and grandfather be haykal nurani, but two sons of one father can both be Imams (one in the mustawda', one in the mustaqarr), as with 'Abd al-Muttalib's two sons. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S514 | 'Ali said 'ana abdun min abid Muhammad' (I am one of the slaves of Muhammad), illustrating the loftiness of the awaited one's station and the three courses (salasa majari) of the relationship: adna (lower), masawat (equality), and a'la (higher). | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S515 | Qaba qawsayn aw adna corresponds to the stations: 'Abd al-Muttalib and Abu Talib are 'qaba qawsayn' and 'Abdullah is 'adna'. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S516 | 'Abd al-Muttalib set up two gates (babayn) to receive ascending forms from his da'wah, one higher than the other: the higher gathering the forms of accomplished believers and virtuous Hudud, the second gathering the forms of steadfast respondents. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S517 | Each Imam's bab al-abwab is his wife, who gathers the forms and then becomes the qalbe haykal of his son the next khalifah: Hashim's bab Salma submerged in 'Abd al-Muttalib; 'Abd al-Muttalib's bab Fatimah bint 'Amr in Abu Talib; Abu Talib's bab Fatimah bint Asad in 'Ali; 'Ali's bab Salman-e-Farsi in Imam Husayn. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S518 | The Prophet's bab al-abwab is Khadijah, submerged in Fatimah; 'Abdullah's bab al-abwab is Aminah, submerged in the Prophet — distinguishing being a bab al-abwab from becoming a qalbe haykal. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S519 | The four Ya Fateru sajdahs/recitations correspond to the four Fatimahs: first sajdah (100x) Fatimah al-Zahra, right cheek (100x) Fatimah bint Asad, third Fatimah bint 'Amr, fourth sajdah (110x) Zawjat al-Qa'im (also named Fatimah), with Fatimah al-Zahra superior to all. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S520 | In sajdah seven parts of the body must touch the ground (forehead/face, two hands, two knees, two feet), signifying that the complete order of the da'wah requires seven limbs/hudud; the cheek-only position signifies a lesser rank. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S521 | The fifth chapter restates that Qa'im min wuld Isma'il is 'Ayn-e-'Azimah and Qa'im min wuld Ishaq is the Prophet; the haykal-nurani requires receptive strength (quwwat) to gather all forms, formable only if the father is a haykal nurani. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S522 | The bab gathers all the world's forms and, when it is time to install them in the Imam's son (nasb), becomes the son's qalbe haykal; the Imam (and the Imam of Imams, 'Ayn-e-'Azimah) decides which form is ready for inclusion. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S523 | 'Abd al-Muttalib made his son Abdullah's matter famous through the sacrifice (dhabh) — sacrificing 100 camels in place of Abdullah — to make known among the ahl al-haqa'iq Abdullah's virtue as one prepared for the station of Nubuwwah and Risalah. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S524 | The present Imam Maulana Muhammad bin Ali holds the lofty station of jadd-e-qa'im (grandfather of the Qa'im), who will have two sons by custom: one at Abu Talib's station (from whom the Qa'im comes) and one at Abdullah's station (Sayyidna Mahdi), whose daughter Fatimah will marry the Qa'im producing Hasan and Husayn. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S525 | 'Abd al-Muttalib handed Wisayah and Imamah to Abu Talib (an imam-e-natiq, like Husayn) and Nubuwwah and Risalah to Abdullah (silent/samit, like al-Hasan); Abdullah fathered Muhammad and Abu Talib fathered 'Ali. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S526 | When Abdullah's transfer (nuqlatu) came, he entrusted Nubuwwah and Risalah to his father 'Abd al-Muttalib for his son Muhammad, and when 'Abd al-Muttalib transferred he entrusted them to Abu Talib because Muhammad was in childhood (tufuliyyah), Abu Talib becoming custodian (kafil) of all four ranks. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S527 | When Muhammad reached maturity Abu Talib handed over the entrusted Nubuwwah and Risalah, and Muhammad ascended through the ranks until reaching the rank of divine speech (nutq-e-ilahi), the final/highest rank above Nubuwwah, Imamah, and Wisayah. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S528 | The station of maqam-e-mahmud changes according to the 'ayn — a single-cycle 'ayn versus a seven-cycle 'ayn — and at the khutbat al-tasmeem the station of the 'ayn changes into the form of the Qa'im. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S529 | The completion of the religion ('I have perfected for you your religion') refers to the completion of the obligations of the din, with the command of wilayah announced last after Hajj, not to a change in station. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S530 | There are three courses (salasa majari) of the Prophet-'Ali relationship: 'Ali's 'ana abdun min abid Muhammad' (majra-e-adna), 'ana wa Aliyyun ka-hatayn' (equality), and the elevation of Hijab-e-'Ain at the conquest of Makkah (a'la). | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S531 | Muhammad said 'I am the prayer (da'wat) of my father Ibrahim,' gathering all the lofty stations and luminous structures of all cycles from the first three thousand years (the higher suwarat of the dawr-e-kashf) within his haykal, which became 'Ali. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S532 | Successive du'at (e.g., Sayyidna Sulayman, then Sayyidna Ja'far) explained the du'as of the da'i but each kept some knowledge received from the Imam hidden, a series of concealment continuing from da'i to da'i. | yes | yes | §15 (The Prophetic Genealogy: from Abd al-Muttalib to the Qa'im); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S533 | When Amir al-Mu'minin was in the unmatched state no one else reached, the Tenth Intellect connected with him totally (ittisalan kulliyan), the creative intellects glanced at him eternally and cast their rays, and the divine substances connected with him above every other station except the Qa'im. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S534 | Amir al-Mu'minin's nuqta of the muhtajib has the highest station after Dhu al-Sa'y al-Ibda'iyah, manifested from Sarandip at the start of the Daur al-Kashf, until the inbi'ath of the Qa'im's nuqta which will be greater still. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S535 | Amir al-Mu'minin is a part (juz') in relation to the Qa'im who is whole (kull), and likewise the stations of the Imams are partial relative to 'Ayn al-'Azimah the Imam of Imams, who is in turn partial relative to the Qa'im, who is partial relative to Dhu al-Sa'y al-Ibda'iyah. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S536 | The Prophet alluded to this with 'al-Hasan wa al-Husayn imamani bi-haqqin qama aw qa'ada wa abuhuma khayrun minhuma' (Hasan and Husayn are two rightful Imams whether they rise or sit, and their father is better than them). | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S537 | The Prophet placed qiyam (rising, Husayn) before qu'ud (sitting, Hasan) though Hasan is the earlier Imam, because to enact the imamate openly among the people is qiyam (as Husayn did) while Hasan remained silent. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S538 | From his state divine miracles and great signs appeared in Amir al-Mu'minin, all of nature became obedient to him, and some Shi'a out of deification (ilahiyat) called him God. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S539 | 'Ali's saying 'ana al-awwal wa al-akhir' (I am the First and the Last) means he is the first of the forms of the preceding cycles and the last of them (the last being the Qa'im), being first and last of the adwar. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S540 | Surat al-Shams takes 14 oaths and demonstrates Arabic grammatical gender (shams feminine, qamar masculine, sama' and ard feminine by convention), with all pronouns 'ha' referring to the sun. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S541 | 'Ali's saying 'ana al-batin' refers to the hidden knowledge from which the subtle hidden divine essences and substantial luminous souls (forming the haykal of 'Ayn al-'Azimah) came; 'ana al-zahir' is his appearance to sight through divine miracles. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S542 | The righteous manifest deeds are the first forms established for the awliya' from interpretive hearts and divine wisdoms in all cycles, all interpretive/real/divine wisdoms ultimately connecting through their Imams to the Imam of Imams Abu Talib. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S543 | All Imams gather through the bab/bab al-abwab process (da'i, then hujjat, then bab) and connect with 'Ayn al-'Azimah through the Prophet as the bab; capable forms join that haykal while the rest are given to the coming ones. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S544 | 'Ali's saying 'ana nuqiltu Adam wa Nuh wa Ibrahim wa Musa wa Isa, wa ana naba'tu al-nabiyyin wa ana arsaltu al-mursalin' (I transported the prophets, I made the prophets prophets, I sent the messengers), the speaker of these forms from the end of the dawr al-kashf until his rising. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S545 | When the flowing light connected with 'Ali totally he knew what was and what will be, and all the world of nature became before him like a thrown ball with nothing hidden, anchored in 'inna li manzilah lam takhtur ala qalb bashar'. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S546 | Lordship (rububiyah) and Divinity (ilahiyah) do not occur to human hearts except for the Qa'im among them — interpreted not as a divinity claim but as the relation of the lord above and the one below. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S547 | All creation knows Allah with outward gnosis (zahir al-ma'rifah), the people of realities know Him with the reality of His gnosis, and no one knew 'Ali with the reality of his gnosis except the Messenger and the Imams of his progeny. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S548 | When people heard 'Ali's claims, groups variously said he is al-ghayb (the Unseen), the First Originated (al-mubda' al-awwal), or the First Emanation (al-inbi'ath al-awwal), and voices diverged; the subtle secret was hidden from all but the fewest of the awliya'. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S549 | 'Ali's hijab was revealed (after Dhu al-Sa'y al-Ibda'iyah) so that he as the mustaqarr could settle the accounts owed to the mustaqarrin by the prophets who served them in earlier eras, while the Prophet, the qaim over prophets and messengers, gathers all the prophets. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S550 | Imam Husayn performed qiyam to settle the accounts of the transgressions in the wars of the Prophet's and 'Ali's times; without it the haykal of Imam Husayn (with the Master of the Universe) could not have developed. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S551 | The reward in the durud (salutation) upon the Prophet and his family exists to establish one's spiritual connection with them; it is a prayer whose acceptance is the reward, drawn from their vast unseen treasuries containing gathered forms of crores of years. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S552 | A qawr (also dawr) equals 400,000 years; 399 qawrs have passed since Dhu al-Sa'y al-Ibda'iyah manifested, and the world is in the last part of the first 400,000-year dawr whose forms are with 'Ayn al-'Azimah. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S553 | Ja'far ibn Mansur al-Yaman stated that Allah does not accept the prophethood of any prophet, the selection of any wasi, the imamate of any wali, nor any act of obedience — however exhaustive the worship — except through the wilayah of 'Ali ibn Abi Talib, and whoever comes without it has his prophethood, successorship, and deeds nullified. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 3/6 key terms located in section |
| S554 | 'Ali is the confluence (majma') of all the Imams and successors (awsiya') from the beginning of the cycles until his advent, which is why nothing is accepted without his wilayah. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S555 | Maulana Abdan's saying (in Majmu' al-Tarbiyah) that 'the jum'ah is upon the service of the master for his slave' is proven, because the children of Ishaq (manifest da'wat) served the children of Isma'il (the lofty divine stations) throughout the past cycles. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S556 | When the forms gathered with 'Ali (Qa'im of Isma'il's progeny) and the children of Ishaq gathered with the Natiq (the Prophet, Qa'im of Ishaq's progeny), justice made it obligatory for 'Ali to serve the Prophet as requital (qadha') for the prior service of Ishaq's children to Isma'il's children. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S557 | The Arabic qadha' means to complete/fulfill (qadha'an ma fata) what was missed, not merely a missed-then-made-up prayer as commonly understood in Urdu. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S558 | This service of the master for his slave — 'Ali standing before, striving with, fighting the enemies of, and defending the Prophet — was requital for the service rendered by the prophets in the eras of Ibrahim, Musa, and 'Isa, measure for measure (hazwan bi-hazwin), with nothing small or great left unaccounted because the scale of justice is established. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S559 | The Tenth Intellect ('Aql al-'Ashir), the musta'id da'iya in the realm of life, is veiled by Muhammad, supports and looks upon him, and aids him through the mediation of jadd, fath, and khayal upon his reaching the rank of hijabiyyah. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S560 | Every natiq, wasi, and imam must receive instruction and progress rank by rank, anchored in 'wa Allahu akhrajakum min butuni ummahatikum la ta'lamuna shay'a' (Allah brought you from your mothers' wombs knowing nothing). | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S561 | The Prophet acquired knowledge from Ubayy ibn Ka'b (alluded to as Jibra'il) at his beginning, then was elevated to Zayd ibn 'Amr the da'i of his island (alluded to as Mika'il), then to the one above (Israfil). | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S562 | Ibrahim's progression through the ranks is shown by the Quranic account of seeing the star, moon, and sun and rejecting each that sets, anchored in 'fa-lamma janna alayhi al-laylu ra'a kawkaban'. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S563 | 'Ali progressed similarly, learning at the side of the Prophet and of Khadijah bint Khuwaylid (the hujjah of Imam Abu Talib), and was alluded to as Ibn Maryam (analogized to 'Isa, Khadijah to Maryam), anchored in 'wa lamma dhuriba ibnu Maryama mathalan'. | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S564 | 'Ali said the Messenger taught him a thousand doors (chapters) of knowledge, and from every door a thousand more opened (one lakh / a thousand thousand). | yes | yes | §16 (Husayn, the Imamate, and the Line of Wilayah); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S565 | Due to a hidden secret, Amir al-Mu'minin said 'Ana wa Muhammad min nurin wahid' (I and Muhammad are from one light), the light of God. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S566 | Allah commanded that one light to split into two halves, saying to the first 'Be Muhammad' and to the second 'Be Ali,' indicating the subtleties of the children of Ishaq and Isma'il and their da'wat of Nubuwwah/Risalah and Imamah/Wisayah. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S567 | 'Ali said 'Muhammad is the yellow sapphire and I am the red ruby' (the most excellent), and the Prophet said 'Aliyyun minni wa ana minhu' and 'la yu'addi anni illa Ali' (none delivers on my behalf except Ali). | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S568 | When Muhammad ascended to the rank of hijabiyyah and nutq al-haqiqi, the Imam of the Age Abu Talib transferred (passed away). | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S569 | 'Lawlaka lama khalaqtu-l-aflak' (Were it not for you I would not have created the celestial spheres) means without the Prophet bearing the forms of Nubuwwah and Risalah — upon whom retributions (qisasat) rested — the aflak and their cycle of accounts would not have been created, while 'Ali's people (Imamah/Wisayah) are not meant for the world's revolutions. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S570 | The Prophet's final station is rutbat al-hijabiyyah and the nutq al-haqiqi, anchored in 'an tab'athahu maqaman mahmudan alladhi wa'adtahu', and as the 'ayn is, so is the mim (manifestation) — rising together at corresponding ranks. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S571 | After Abu Talib's transfer he became in his station a veil (hijab) for the Tenth Intellect and a place for its rays to fall, aiding the Prophet through the mediation of jadd, fath, and khayal (the thalathat sufara', alluding to the 'ayn). | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S572 | Jadd, fath, and khayal are the three spiritual emissaries in the world of veiling, corresponding to the three corporeal ones — 'Abd al-Muttalib (jadd), 'Abdullah (fath), and Abu Talib (khayal) — who became intellects divested of the senses, emissaries between the Prophet/'Ali and the Tenth Intellect. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S573 | The Tenth Intellect stands in the station of the First Intellect for the world of nature, and 'Ayn-e-'Azimah takes on the deputyship of the Tenth, in the spiritual world of life. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S574 | Jibra'il is a station (maqam), not a self-existing entity; whoever occupies the helper's station for the one being trained (at whatever rank) becomes Jibra'il, with the helper changing as the receiver's ranks increase. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S575 | In the tongue of the Shari'ah 'Abd al-Muttalib (jadd) is Israfil, 'Abdullah (fath) is Mika'il, and Abu Talib (khayal) is Jibra'il, the one who supports Muhammad in his beginning and end. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S576 | The complete light from the Tenth connected with Muhammad (making him natiq), while with 'Ali it was a ray (shu'a'), making him samit (silent) — the Prophet natiq and 'Ali the silent opposite. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S577 | The Prophet told 'Ali he was given five things the Prophet himself was not given, including a father-in-law (the Prophet), a wife (Fatimah), and sons (Hasan and Husayn), illustrating the third (highest) course of their relationship. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S578 | With the Prophet was the knowledge of what was and what will be (he made prophecies), while with 'Ali was the knowledge of what has been (what happened), due to the veil (hijab) of 'Ali. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S579 | When the Prophet fulfilled his service, appointed 'Ali (nass), and transferred to Allah's abode, 'Ali became in his place a veil for the Tenth Intellect and a place for its rays, with the Tenth manifesting through him via the mediation of jadd, fath, and khayal — now 'Abd al-Muttalib (jadd), Abu Talib (fath), and Muhammad (khayal) for Amir al-Mu'minin. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S580 | 'Abd al-Muttalib, 'Abdullah, and Abu Talib did not enter the haykal of the Prophet or 'Ali but remained on the horizon of the Tenth as intermediaries between it and Muhammad and 'Ali, and will go into the haykal of the Qa'im. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S581 | By a general rule, every establisher (muqim) of a station after his transfer is the khayal for it, the one before him is the fath, and the one preceding them both is the jadd, each an agent within his extension (mandud). | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S582 | The Prophet said 'Takhaffafu talhaqu' (Lighten yourselves and you will catch up) — reduce sins to join the angels — and 'the first of you only await the arrival of the last of you,' the departed awaiting those who come later. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 1/2 key terms located in section |
| S583 | When the sixth Natiq Muhammad rose with the command, announced the two testimonies (la ilaha illallah, Muhammadun Rasulullah), and established his exoteric (zahir) mission, people accepted Islam, performed jihad, established the seven pillars (da'a'im as-sab') and the sunnahs, and were true in love (mawalat) of him and his cousin outwardly. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S584 | When the believers' souls became jewel-like (tajawharat), their forms hidden, and their essences shone, those who passed away with outward (zahir) allegiance to 'Ali returned to the luminous haykal of Fatimah, the essence of the Islamic jewel (khulasat al-jawhar al-islami). | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S585 | When the luminous forms were complete, Fatimah's father (the Prophet) married her to Amir al-Mu'minin by Allah's command and revelation, the forms of iman gathering with 'Ali and forms of islam with Fatimah, so the system was established and iman and islam were wed. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S586 | The exoteric mission continued with the esoteric mission within it, until Hasan was brought from the exoteric da'wat (the portion/qist of the Natiq) and Husayn from the esoteric da'wat (the portion of the Wasi). | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S587 | The Prophet entrusted his son Hasan the rank of Nubuwwah and Risalah and his son Husayn the rank of Wisayah and Imamah, placing the entrusted (mustawda') ranks for Hasan with Amir al-Mu'minin since the children were seven or eight years old. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S588 | Amir al-Mu'minin stood in the Prophet's station with the four ranks, then entrusted Hasan Nubuwwah/Risalah and Husayn Wisayah/Imamah; in the imamate as in the human being there are ten degrees beginning with responsiveness (istijabat) then faith (iman). | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S589 | In precedence (sabaqiyat) Hasan was first, but in sharpness of vision (hiddat-e-nazar) Husayn was superior; the four ranks became permanent (mustaqarr) in Husayn, not departing to his progeny, until handed to the Qa'im al-Qiyamah (per Da'wat-e-Ashir). | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S590 | In the haykal of the Qa'im al-Qiyamah, the soul of the Qa'im is its spirit (ruh), Amir al-Mu'minin 'Ali is its heart (qalb), and the Natiq Muhammad is its head (ra's). | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S591 | Every seventh Imam (Qa'im) appears every thousand years; over seven thousand years the Imams gather into the horizon of their father, and at forty-nine thousand years the seventh gathers his era plus the Imams of seven thousand and forty-nine thousand years. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S592 | Sayyidna Hamiduddin al-Kirmani said in Rahat al-'Aql that 'the sun of the Abode of Origination (dar al-ibda') and the moon of Emission (inbi'ath) shine upon them,' the sun pointing to the Natiq and the moon to the Wasi. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S593 | All the Imams from the children of ' Abd al-Muttalib, Abu Talib, 'Abdullah, and Fatimah become limbs (a'da') of the Qa'im's luminous haykal, but 'Abd al-Muttalib, Abu Talib, and 'Abdullah remain in the fold of the Tenth Intellect as ambassadors between it and Muhammad and 'Ali. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S594 | On the day of Badr the gathered angels (the stations that were with Amir al-Mu'minin) became personified, and Abu Talib was the master of Hayzum (Jibra'il's horse) commanding 'Ali to advance and attack in battle. | yes | yes | §17 (Muhammad, Ali, and the Light of the Ahl al-Bayt); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S595 | Maulana Husayn departed and the rest of the A'immah mutimmin (completers) continued until reaching Maulana Muhammad ibn Isma'il, the seventh, to whom the ultimate goal of the religious laws culminates and who is the Qa'im bil-quwwah (potential Qa'im), master of the first unveiling (sahib al-kashf al-ula). | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S596 | The Qa'im bil-fi'l (kinetic Qa'im) who appears at the end of the dawr is the master of the later unveiling (kashf al-ukhra) and the great strike (batshat al-kubra), while Muhammad ibn Isma'il is master of the first unveiling. | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S597 | The qiyamat are many: a qiyamah is a gathering (ijtima') of forms with hashr and nashr, and the ranks (ma'dhun makfuf, ma'dhun mutlaq, da'i muharram, da'i mutlaq, da'i al-balagh, hujjat, abwab) each represent a qiyamah for those connected to them. | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S598 | These minor qiyamat are followed by the Qa'im of the great resurrection (qiyamat al-kubra), the master of the batshat al-kubra (great strike), with whom gather all the noble stations and all the Imams from the first 3,000 years (dawr al-fitrah) to the end of 7,000 years of the dawr al-satr. | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S599 | Amir al-Mu'minin alluded with 'ala ra's kulli asharat alaf sanah yaqumu min al-suwarat' — at the head of every 10,000 years a form stands up — pointing to the Qa'im who gathers the dawr al-fitrah (3,000) and dawr al-satr (7,000), totaling 10,000 years. | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S600 | Muhammad ibn Isma'il retains the name 'seventh Natiq' for his subtlety in the affair of God, yet he is unique in the rank of divine oneness (rutbat al-wahdat al-ilahiyyah), not arranged in the chain (silk) of the dawr al-satr but its terminator, having surpassed the seventh-Natiq rank to a higher one. | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S601 | Counting any Natiq with his wasi and six mutimmin yields a seventh Natiq: Adam yields Nuh, Nuh yields Ibrahim, Ibrahim yields Musa, Musa yields 'Isa, 'Isa yields Muhammad, and Muhammad yields Muhammad ibn Isma'il. | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 9/9 key terms located in section |
| S602 | The three concealed completers (mutimmu dawr al-satr) of Muhammad ibn Isma'il's dawr are the concealed Imams Maulana 'Abdullah, Maulana Ahmad, and Maulana Husayn, with their three abwab commanded to keep the Imam in taqiyyah and present themselves as Imam, the fourth being Imam 'Ali the Natiq to complete the count of seven. | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S603 | The Ahl al-Haqq (People of Truth) are of two kinds: those of true gnosis and divine sciences, and those of pure devotion and righteous deeds; both perform righteous deeds and have loyalty to all the Imams. | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S604 | Upon separating from their dense bodies (kasa'if), the Imams who possess gnosis become the chief members (a'za' ra'isah) of the luminous temple (haykal nurani) of the Imam, with the form (surat) pulling the sensory soul (nafs hissiyah) to reach the haykal. | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S605 | The level of luminosity in the haykal is according to each one's measure in knowledge (ilm), gnosis (ma'rifat), and devotion (wilayat); those with pure devotion and righteous deeds but without true knowledge follow them at lesser ranks (lata'if below kasa'if). | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S606 | The dense bodies (kasa'if) are preserved in the mightiest protection: returning to earth, then the tempered mixture, becoming rain, dissolving to vapor, ascending through the rays of the seven planets to the falak al-buruj (Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon below it). | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S607 | The body parts ascend and take a place in the buruj as a successor (khalifa); from the buruj the prior occupant's portion (flesh, skin, marrow, nerves, veins) descends as a fine dust to earth and eventually becomes a human in the alam al-alfiyyah (thousand-year realm). | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S608 | Allah created only essences (zawat/uqul); soul (nafs), body (jism), animals, plants, and minerals are progressively fallen forms of the intellect that delayed (ta'khir) in using its God-given powers, with the hardest fallen state being rock (sakhrah) — and we, not Allah, caused this fall. | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S609 | When these descended human forms reach maturity (bulugh al-hulm) they are called, respond, and the noble covenant (al-'ahd al-karim) is taken from them, ascending in religious ranks and true knowledge without doubt because their first karra was on certainty. | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S610 | A good deed once established (a created point) cannot be destroyed and its reward remains forever — its reward is tenfold while evil is onefold and can become non-existent — anchored in 'qad aflaha al-mu'minun' and the reward principle. | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 2/4 key terms located in section |
| S611 | Sayyidna 'Ali ibn Husayn taught that all the points (nuqta) of those who will become believers in a given kawr are present with the Sayyid al-Ida'iyya, manifesting in the era appointed by his governance; likewise the mudabbir of the mawr holds the points of his whole mawr, and Adam al-Ibda'i holds the points of all who will progress. | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S612 | Each rank returns to its same rank — bab as bab, hujja as hujja, da'i as da'i, mu'min as mu'min, mustajib as mustajib — and no one transgresses his first state even if he reaches higher knowledge; breaching ranks (kharq-e-maratib) like the hour-to-hour transition requires traversing all sixty minutes (degrees). | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S613 | When a soul is purified and ascends to the Chosen One, another soul (a member of the body that became worthy of ascent) is sent forth in the remaining body by the governance of the governors, eventually becoming a single essence; for martyrs (shuhada') both heart and liver become soul, granting a degree or two of advance. | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S614 | These successful believers are those alluded to in 'qad aflaha al-mu'minun' — humble in prayer, turning from vain speech, active in zakat, guarding chastity except with wives or right-hand possessions — these qualities being their distinguishing mark (sima) of mustaqim al-sayr in outward and inward. | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S615 | Prayer is founded upon four pillars/shares (as-hum): wudu' (ablution), ruku' (bowing), sujud (prostration), and khushu' (humility), the first degree of khushu' being awareness of the meaning of what is recited. | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 5/6 key terms located in section |
| S616 | The purification of a mustaqim al-sayr (one of upright conduct who committed no gravely forbidden sin) is completed over 360 karrat, in which his whole body becomes pure — the sole of the foot reaching the station of the heart. | yes | yes | §18 (Muhammad ibn Isma'il and the Cycle of Concealment); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S617 | The fifth chapter applies the statement on the ma'ad (return) to the righteous (sulaha') of the Ahl al-Zahir — those who hold to the outward, follow the Islamic rulings, believe in the Prophet, but in whom love and obedience to the Imam have not fully entered (sulh-e-kul), including the Ahl al-Tashayyu'. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S618 | The chaste, virtuous, pure man of the Ahl al-Zahir who establishes the pillars of Islam, holds no enmity, rancor, hypocrisy, injustice, falsehood, lying, or betrayal toward the Ahl al-Haqq, and adheres to the wilayah of the Natiq of the era and his Wasi and recognizes their pure progeny — though such people are very few. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S619 | Even a small good deed of anyone (believer, hypocrite, or disbeliever) is never wasted by Allah; good (khayr) can never become non-existent (kal-'adam) while evil (sharr) can — anchored in 'Inna al-hasanat yudhhibna al-sayyi'at'. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S620 | Without the wilayah of 'Ali one cannot have a good end (khatima bil-khayr) or cross the Pulsirat, yet a good deed still earns its reward and pulls the doer toward the wilayah of the Wali in the end. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S621 | When the righteous Ahl al-Zahir person nears death, the spiritualities of the sun and Venus move him, his soul (nafs) spreads in his body, his heat cools, and his blood freezes — the soul spreading into the body being a sign of inability to cross the Pulsirat. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S622 | The good concept (tasawwur) of such a righteous person — preservation of the Book of Allah, love of the Messenger and his Wasi, mercy, chastity, modesty, and fear of Allah — becomes a luminous form (surat-e-nurani) that ascends with the spiritualities to the moon, then is handed to the sun where it ferments and is purified until Allah permits its return. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S623 | The body of the righteous dissolves bit by bit, ascends as vapor, enters the tempered mixture, falls as rain, becomes pure foods and sweet drinks and fragrant smells, nourishes a human, appears as a sperm-drop, ascends through ranks, and at birth the purified form connects with it along with the sensory soul it deserves. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S624 | The vegetative soul (nafs namiyah) connects from the sphere of the moon when the sperm-drop forms, and the sensory soul (nafs hissiyah) connects from the sun at birth from the womb. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S625 | Because the scale of justice (mizan al-'adl) is established, the purified concept returns to the righteous person in a state greater, more glorious, and more noble than at death — like a traveler who leaves home, earns wealth in exile, and returns more abundant than before. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S626 | The concept of the disobedient and rebellious has no return ever to their body and soul at death — like a traveler who leaves family and home without intending to return — which is a type of punishment (per 'according to what his hands have put forth'). | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S627 | The fourth chapter gathers five sections; after clarifying the transition and progress of the awliya' to the gates of paradise and the fulfillment of the righteous Ahl al-Zahir, the discourse turns to the opposites (azdad) — the people of corruption and what torment they reach. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S628 | When death comes to one of the wicked opposites (azdad), the dark celestial jawzahar (placed beneath the sphere of the moon, comprising the two nodes ras and dhanab) moves him, being the magnet for the wicked (khabith) and the vile (khasis). | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S629 | In opposition to the 'amud al-nur (pillar of light, descending from the First Intellect and connected through the kalima la ilaha illallah to every person on the 'ahd-e-sharif), Allah established the dark celestial jawzahar; one is either connected to the 'amud al-nur or pulled to the jawzahar with no space between. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S630 | The jawzahar-e-zulmani is made from the parts (ajza) of people who were elevated but ended in wickedness, placed beneath the moon's sphere (not reaching the rock) as justice from Allah, from where they carry out their ploys (tadabir); the dhanab (tail) is more wicked than the ras (head). | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S631 | True progress (taraqqi) is the body becoming soul, while the soul becoming body is decline (tanazzul); at the wicked one's death his soul and body become a single thing and one part transforms into another. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S632 | The wicked one separates from his dark destructive sciences, his disbelief about the Ahl al-Haqq, his concealed enmity and hatred, his wicked deeds, and his corrupt beliefs — all of which build a subtle dark wicked form (surat zulmaniyyah khabithah) mirroring the noble luminous form built from interpretive sciences and divine gnoses. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S633 | That dark soul is called the dark, Iblisi, satanic soul (nafs zulmani iblisiya shaytaniyya) and becomes a fiery incinerating form, just as the luminous form is an angelic speaking soul (nafs malakutiyya natiqa). | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S634 | Evil has no root in the primordial creation (al-sharru la asla lahu fi al-ibda'); evil is distance from good as darkness (zulmat) is distance from light (nur), and corporeality/density (kasafat) was created because of us, not by Allah. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S635 | A created thing cannot become non-existent (fana); its form (shakl) can change but its existence cannot be annulled — no one but the Originator has the power to turn existence into non-existence. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S636 | The wicked dark soul-form is buried with the body and becomes an incinerating, tormenting fire for a period of days, so that grave is seen aflame at night, though one who approaches perceives nothing; its owner is the most wicked, most criminal, most hostile to the Ahl al-Haqq, and most oppressive of creation. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S637 | Sayyidna Ikramali Sahib narrated (1255 Hijri) an incident on the journey from Bharuch to Baroda where lightning-fire descended and burned only the clothes in one room while he and his students (Sheikh Ibrahim, Fardali) were safe in another. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S638 | Sayyidna Sheikh Adam Sahib verified that light (nur, distinct from fire nar) descends from the sky onto the grave of Muhammad Arif Sahib in Mumin-bagh, Baroda — his reward for endowing (waqf) his land for the jamatkhana and masjid. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S639 | Sheikh Adam Sahib ruled by nass that whichever portion is designated as a masjid is a masjid (tahiyyatul masjid may be prayed in it) regardless of a residence below it, and the portion not so designated is not. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S640 | When a person passes away, the soul's/form's attention is directed first to the body's parts, then to the shroud (kafan), then to the grave's soil (which is why his own grave-soil should be used), then to his house and dwelling. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S641 | Once separated from the dense body (which was like a prison/sijn), the wicked dark form takes whatever shape it wishes and does what it wills, since the body's density no longer restrains it, having become the extremity of evil as the luminous form is the extremity of good. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S642 | By Allah's justice the wicked soul is not prevented from what it deserves of torment, because the scale of justice (mizan al-'adl) is established; from the moment it separates from its body it is in torment in all it does, by its will and without its will. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S643 | When the wicked soul leaves its body it seeks ascent to pierce the veils (kharq al-hujub) but the rays of the celestial spheres reflect it back to earth — anchored in 'la tanfudhuna illa bi-sultan' — and it seeks its old body but does not find it, remaining terrified and regretful, roaming the place of the body and grave (called mun'if by the common people). | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S644 | The wicked soul roams between earth and sky, anchored in the Prophet's saying 'al-hawa' la khala'un wa la mala' (the air is neither empty nor full), and takes refuge in dark places, foul urinals, desolate wildernesses, and filthy slaughterhouses — hence the prayer 'a'udhu billahi min al-rijs al-najis...' upon entering the lavatory. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S645 | The wicked soul appears in the dreams of like-minded evil people (ahl al-nufus al-khabithah), affirming their corrupt beliefs and increasing their enmity to the Ahl al-Haqq and inclination to their opposites, anchored in 'shayatin al-ins wa al-jinn yuhi ba'duhum ila ba'din zukhruf al-qawl ghurura'. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S646 | The wicked form mingles with an ignorant man, woman, or boy and speaks through his tongue, sometimes uttering unseen matters or events (not necessarily true, used to deceive); the common people call this the ruh or ruhiyyah, and it can only affect things through using another body. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S647 | The wicked soul disparages the Ahl al-Haqq before its present helpers, affirming their opposition and corrupt beliefs and inclining their hearts to obey it — all from inspiration, whispering, and beguiling (anchored in 'yuwasswisu fi sudur al-nas min al-jinnati wa al-nas') increasing them in devilry and Iblis-like behavior. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S648 | When that wicked man or woman dies, the wicked form leaves and the dark celestial node (the abominable jawzahar/node of the sphere, Rahu and Ketu) pulls it after it completes its reckoning, being the dark isthmus (barzakh) and magnet that pulls the evil souls into its horizon, where it becomes a Satan and gathers with its kind for as long as the Governor wills. | yes | yes | §19 (The Two Paths: Reward, Punishment, and the Opposites); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S649 | The shayatin (devils) are devilish forms that reside in the 'uqdatain (two knots), which constitute the dark isthmus (barzakh al-zulmani). | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S650 | These devilish forms descend to the earth by the will of the Mudabbir (Director) and enter into edibles and drinkables (ma'kulat wa mashrubat). | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S651 | The respite (postponement of punishment) is given so that the one from whom qisas (retribution) is due can have his manifestation (zuhur), enabling the full retribution to be exacted. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S652 | Nourishment (ghiza) from the poisoned foods can be taken by either a wali (friend) or a zidd (opposite/adversary). | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S653 | The poisoned food becomes a passageway (ma'bar) through which an evil form manifests, e.g. as Qabil manifested from Adam, and from various Imams enemy-sons were born. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S654 | The evil form appears from a man at the time of intimacy with his wife, producing a son who progresses in ranks until born and matured. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S655 | Such a matured son becomes the zidd (opposite) of the natiq, his wasi (legatee), or the Imam of the Age. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S656 | The zidd transitions from being a shaytan bi-l-quwwah (devil in potentiality) to a shaytan bi-l-fi'l (devil in actuality). | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S657 | The zidd claims the rank of the high station (maqam al-'ali), conceals the true holder's status (manzilah), and denies (jahada) his station. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S658 | Concealing a hadd's station is a spiritual killing (batini qatl), exemplified by al-Harith ibn Murrah who concealed the station of Adam. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S659 | Qabil concealed the station of Habil, claimed his maqam, and killed him. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S660 | Rasib was the zidd (opposite) of Nuh, though he was not necessarily his son. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S661 | Ham was the zidd of his brother Sam (Shem) and killed him. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S662 | Nimrud ibn Kan'an was the zidd of Ibrahim. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S663 | Al-Walid ibn Mus'ab was the zidd of Musa. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S664 | Sahbuta killed 'Isa (Jesus). | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S665 | Abu Lahab and Abu Jahl were the two opposites (zidds) of the Prophet Muhammad. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S666 | Abu Bakr and 'Umar were the two opposites (zidds) of Ali ibn Abi Talib, with the curse of God upon them. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S667 | Ibn Muljam was the one who killed Ali ibn Abi Talib lastly (akhiran). | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S668 | Mu'awiyah was the zidd of al-Hasan and then poisoned him. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S669 | Yazid was the zidd of al-Husayn, and Shimr killed him. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S670 | Mawlana al-Mustansir Billah and Mawlana al-Amir were each killed by poisoners/killers who were zidds. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S671 | The woman of Khaybar (al-Imra'at al-Khaybariyya) poisoned the Prophet in his food, and the poison remained until it became the cause of his passing. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S672 | It is a sign of the Prophet's greatness that even a cooked, severed goat's leg testified to him that it was poisoned (masmum) after he had eaten. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S673 | The hadith 'li-kulli nabiyyin shaytanun wa liya shaytanan' (for every prophet there is one devil, and for me there are two) identifies the Prophet's two devils as Abu Lahab and Abu Jahl. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S674 | The vices (qaba'is), which are forms gathered in the two knots (the dark isthmus), can flow upon vegetation and become a deadly poison destroying the noble stations, as with the poison that destroyed the Natiq. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S675 | The poison from the two knots destroyed al-Hasan, and likewise Mawlana Muslim and Mawlana al-Shams. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S676 | When the vices fall upon minerals (ma'adin), they become swords, knives, and arrowheads with which the noble stations are killed. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S677 | Every poisoned/weaponized thing is preserved (mahfuz) for its owner (sahib), and its preserver is the dark atmosphere (al-jaww al-zulmani). | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S678 | This preservation operates by the will of the Mudabbir, the requirement of wisdom (hikmah), and the scale of God's justice (mizan al-'adl). | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S679 | If the Imam's era is one of the sword, killing, and dominance, the forms of the slain opposites ascend to the two knots to take revenge from that noble station, returning swiftly as devils to attack. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S680 | If the Imam's era is one of commands, prohibitions, establishing punishments (hudud), flogging, imprisonment, and exile, the forms of the opposites executed upon them descend from the isthmus as poisons. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S681 | Retributions (qisasat) must be completed so that the wronged opposites' transgressions are settled and the further promotion (taraqqi) of the gathering of Imam Husayn can occur; this is a purification. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S682 | A person who delivers the poison to the noble station is himself a collection of curses gathered in the two knots, like Ja'dah the wife of Imam Hasan. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S683 | Curses (la'anat) from the stations and punishments gather with the conceptions of the opposites in the two knots, producing extreme oppressors most confirmed in enmity and devilry. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S684 | Amir al-Mu'minin Ali cursed the people of Kufa/Iraq who betrayed him, foretelling a severe tyrant would be imposed upon them; these curses gathered and manifested as al-Hajjaj ibn Yusuf al-Thaqafi. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S685 | Ibn Mahdi was likewise from the curses of the punishments and the conception of the opposites, poured upon the people of Tihamah. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S686 | A curse (la'ana) means seeking that Allah distance someone from His mercy; in prayer, seeking mercy (tarahhum) for opposites is impermissible while cursing is permissible. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S687 | For the bodies and souls of the opposites (azdad), at death the single soul spreads throughout its body and both become one thing, transforming into dust, ascending as vapor, and descending as rain. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S688 | In the believer the bodily parts become soul, whereas in the opposite the soul becomes body (the process is reversed); the soul, being a divine creation, cannot be destroyed. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S689 | From the opposites' rain are produced destructive lightnings, annihilating thunderbolts, ruinous floods, and harmful hail, which is the action of the opposites in all times. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S690 | The opposites' substance pours onto earth becoming vegetation and animals, by which the nearer torment (al-'azab al-adna) is unleashed upon them. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
