| S691 | The first level (dark) of torment is named al-rijs, which is the human tunic (qamis al-bashar). | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S692 | The consumer of the poisoned substance becomes a nutfah, ascends to a womb, and emerges as a fetus in a tunic from peoples like the Zanj, Zabab, Barbar, Turk, for whom addressing the people of truth is unsuitable. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S693 | He transfers from one tunic to another, completing seventy tunics (qamis) in each type. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S694 | The second level (dark al-thani), khums al-'aks, is the level similar to human composition: the ape, bear, nasnas, ghoul, and lizard, each with seventy tunics. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S695 | In these temporal tunics, the existence of torment is confirmed for him because he opposed the lords of truth, and he becomes one who knows the people of truth and falsehood. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S696 | The third level (dark al-thalith), khums al-'ars, is the predators of land and sea (lions, tigers, wolves), every creature with a canine (nab) and a claw (mikhlab), each with seventy tunics. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S697 | God grants the punished soul enough cognitive power to understand it is in this animal state because of its prior opposition; this awareness was not granted in the first (human) tunic. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S698 | The fourth level (dark al-rabi'), khums al-khars, is the crawling creatures of land and sea (vipers, snakes, scorpions); in this level he is mute (khars), each type with seventy tunics. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S699 | The fifth level (dark al-khums), named al-najis, is the predatory birds of land and sea, each type with seventy tunics. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S700 | Were it not for the soul's knowledge of its punishment, there would be no pain in punishment nor comfort in reward — knowledge is the precondition of both 'azab and thawab. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S701 | Lawful (ascending) animals are not in these torment levels; when a lawful animal is slaughtered its part becomes the human body and to that extent its salvation occurs, returning by the lawful (ascending) path. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S702 | After completing the five khums levels, the soul passes into the sixth level (dark al-sadis) named al-naqs (deficiency). | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S703 | In the sixth level (naks), no knowledge of his state remains because he has departed from the world of sense ('alam al-hiss). | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S704 | Quran 16:70 ('and among you is he who is returned to the most decrepit of age, so that he knows nothing after having known') is cited as the proof of the matter being obscured and knowledge snatched away from the descending soul. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S705 | When he reaches this limit, nothing remains except the faculty of growth ('alat al-nama'), entering the plant state where the growing soul remains but the sensing soul is removed. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S706 | This sixth-level form is the forbidden plant (al-nabat al-mahzur) that is a killer and destroyer of the living being (hayawan). | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S707 | While descending, souls descend from bad bodies; while ascending, they ascend from good bodies — the same vegetative state operates in both directions. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S708 | The seventh level (dark al-sabi'), named al-daks, is the hard sanj mineral and the filthy, wicked stone, each type with seventy tunics; good stones like those of the mosque or Ka'bah are excluded. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S709 | For each of these seven levels (adrak) there are seventy types (naw') and seventy tunics (qamis), yielding 4900 manifestations per level. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S710 | Fallen souls reach the mineral state by two paths: either their intentions at descent were so bad they became stones directly, or they ascended and then fell back; not everyone completes all levels — many return after one to seven levels. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S711 | In the shari'ah, animals whose meat is forbidden are forbidden because they are in the descending levels (in punishment), while ascending animals are permissible to eat. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S712 | After completing the seven levels, the most stubborn enemies (mutakallifin) are immersed with the opposites of the divine stations, messengers, and prophets, and exiled to the four uninhabited corners of the earth outside the boundary of moderation. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S713 | The northern region (place of the star Jady), frozen and dark with the sun absent six months, receives the freezing cold of zamharir; there the opposites of the Imams' stations exist as terrifying frozen-mountain animals. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S714 | Zamharir is the mist/wind of intense cold, while asir is the mist of intense heat (the sphere of fire). | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S715 | The southern region (orbit of Suhayl/Canopus), day for six months, receives the heat of asir; there the opposites of the messengers and prophets are punished as huge ugly mountain-like animals with no apparent senses but feeling great punishment. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S716 | The eastern region (sunrise), with lofty mountains nearly reaching the sphere of fire, holds the punishment of the assistants (a'wan) of the opposites of the messengers and prophets. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S717 | The western region (sunset) holds a vast dark turbulent sea called the Sea of the Jinn (the Pacific) with mountainous waves, holding the punishment of the assistants of the opposites of the Imams' stations, who return as misshapen sea-animals devouring one another. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S718 | Souls are rotated through all four regions in turn (north, south, east, west) so the periphery-punishment ('azab al-atraf) is undergone in all directions. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S719 | This is the reality of Sayyidna al-Mu'ayyad's saying (in the 70th of his first hundred Majalis al-Hikmah) that whoever proceeds upon other than the right proportion slips from the luminous center (al-markaz al-nurani) and is cut off from the wilayah of the friends of Allah. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S720 | Quran 77:30-31 ('a shadow with three branches, neither cooling nor availing against the flame') refers to the three-branched periphery punishment, identified as the three dimensions (length, breadth, depth). | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S721 | All the foregoing (seven levels plus four-periphery punishment) is the nearer punishment (al-'azab al-adna); afterward souls are returned to the inhabited quarter (rub' al-maskun) until the Resurrection. | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S722 | When returned to the inhabited quarter, that quarter is for them like a garden/paradise by comparison to the peripheries, until they manifest with the signs of the millennium (alfiyyah), are brought for the accounting, and then meet the greater punishment (al-'azab al-akbar). | yes | yes | §20 (The Counter-Types and the Poison of Enmity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S723 | As for the followers (atba') from the pillars ('umud) and subjects (ra'aya), some return after the perception-levels and some complete them; their attainment of punishment is according to their earnestness for the people of truth and their actions. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S724 | On the ascending return, the soul begins in minerals beneficial to mankind, then non-forbidden plants, then lawful animals — the good path, as opposed to the bad bodies entered while descending. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S725 | The lawful animal is used by whoever must use it (a wali or a zidd) and becomes a passage (ma'bar) for the person to emerge in the fifth millennium, where tribulations (balaya) are presented to him. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S726 | Those undergoing punishment are not subject to shari'ah rulings, since shari'ah obligations apply to persons of intellect, not to animals or those in the qisas period. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S727 | The returning soul is presented with the wilayah of the hadd it had denied; if it accepts, it is saved (naja) and enters the company of the people of truth — a clean-slate state like the first perfection (kamal-e-awwal). | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S728 | If it refuses and is arrogant, it returns falling (saqitan) into the classes of punishment; this continues until the rising of the Qaim. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S729 | When the Qaim's appearance draws near, all of creation from the time of Adam to the era of his cycle manifests with his appearance; no one remains, obedient or sinner, except he appears, journeying to earth then to temperament and compounding, then into foods and seminal drops, ascending in ranks to appear as a person upon his first invited state. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S730 | Each soul is restored upon the very state (old age, youth, smallness, bigness, acceptance, denial, even skin color) in which it was first invited to the truth. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S731 | Every prophet, wasi, imam, and hadd is personified (yashkhasu) for the people of his era as a witness over them. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S732 | Quran 39:69 ('the prophets and the witnesses will be brought, and it will be judged between them with truth') means each maqam is appointed a witness (shahid) over the deeds of the people of its time; shahid here means a saint of higher rank, not a literal witness. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S733 | Quran 56:49-50 ('Say, indeed the former and the latter will surely be gathered for the appointed time of a known day') narrates the gathering of all creation at the appointed time. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S734 | Each maqam/hadd disputes (yunafishu) with the people of his era — the Qaim among them and the one sent to them — confronting them about their belying, stubbornness, hostility, and all their actions and sayings, small and great. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S735 | The gathering of resurrection occurs in the plains of qiyamah, identified as the western line of the equator (khatt al-istiwa al-gharbi), the land of Ma'rib, because the rising of the Qaim will be there. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S736 | Only those creatures (khala'iq, including animals) whose time for qiyamah has come will gather at Ma'rib, rushing from all islands; not the entire world, since the place would become constricted. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S737 | Quran 21:96-97 ('Until, when Ya'juj and Ma'juj are released, and they from every elevation descend... and the true promise has drawn near, the eyes of those who disbelieved will stare') is interpreted: Ya'juj and Ma'juj are the opposites (azdad), their followers are the disbelievers, and their objects of worship are their chiefs who stood in the station of the true Imams. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S738 | When the creatures gather before the Qaim and every hadd, imam, wasi, and natiq is individualized for the people of his era and made to face all their deeds, the necks of the opposites (azdad) are struck and a fire is sent that burns them all, transforming them into vapor at that moment. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S739 | The vapor of the destroyed opposites ascends to the two knots because the two knots attract it through the foul affinity (al-munasabah al-khabithah) between them. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S740 | From the two knots beneath the earth, the Sakhrah (the rock in the lowest part of the earth) absorbs that vapor through affinity, drawing it into its parts and pores until it reaches its center. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S741 | By the will of the Mudabbir, a reaction occurs in the Sakhrah's center that re-forms each individual upon the state (the millennial stature, qamat al-alfiyyah) in which he opposed the Master of the Age (Sahib al-Zaman). | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S742 | The Sakhrah is shaped like a brass pot (qidr al-sufr): hard smooth bottom, hollow middle of sulfuric (kibritiyyah) stones, a hard rock roof — closed (mulamlamah) on all sides with pores only in its lower part to vent the vapor. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S743 | The Sakhrah is what the Noble Book names Sijjin, and the wise ones (hukama') modeled the bathhouse (bayt al-hammam) upon its pattern. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S744 | The fuel (waqid) beneath the Sakhrah is the heat of the sun, which heats it from below as the sun passes beneath the earth. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S745 | Upon reaching the center, the individuals observe (mu'ayanah) those who preceded them, recognize they deserve the punishment they are in, and confess their negligence and wrongdoing. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S746 | Quran 21:97-98 ('woe to us, we were in negligence... you and what you worship besides Allah are the fuel of Hell') is applied to the punished opposites and their false objects of worship. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S747 | Quran 21:99 ('Had these been gods they would not have entered it') means: had the chiefs they followed been true Imams of truth, they would not have entered the Sakhrah. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 1/2 key terms located in section |
| S748 | The Quran's 'khalidina fiha' without 'abadan' for the disbelievers/People of the Book indicates the abiding in Hell is temporary (khulud 'aridi) for purification, whereas 'khalidina fiha abadan' for the righteous indicates eternal abiding (khulud abad) in Paradise. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S749 | When the sun's path goes beneath the earth each night, it heats the Sakhrah like the bottom of a hot pot, igniting the sulfuric, calcareous, and glassy stones so the accursed ones tumble in the boiling middle, crying for help unanswered. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S750 | This Sakhrah/Sijjin punishment is specific to the opposites (azdad) of the nutaqa', awsiya', a'immah and all their hudud — and their hostile claimants, fanatical helpers, doubt-whisperers, and mockers of the people of truth. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S751 | The foolish rabble (huhud and ru'a') who merely followed are turned back from the last perception-level and are not put into the periphery or Sakhrah punishment, because they acted without knowing intent. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S752 | The pores of the Sakhrah are located beneath the Ka'bah, since by 'ard' (earth) the Ka'bah is always meant; entry into the Sakhrah occurs from beneath the Ka'bah. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S753 | The earth is both elliptical (baydwi) and flat (musattah): a flat platform for humans (the land of da'wat) whose center on one surface is the Ka'bah and on whose opposite surface is the Sakhrah/Sijjin (the Great Hell), the abode of the Ahl al-Tabut. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S754 | The earth has four corners (atraf al-zamin) adjacent to the Sakhrah where the periphery-punishment befalls the opponents, and on which the Sakhrah's vents (manafiz) are located, with cold so severe no bird can fly past. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S755 | Besides the periphery vents, there are many vents in the inhabited quarter, the largest being Durdur, located in the sea between China and India, toward which all rivers flow and from which various sounds emanate. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S756 | The Ahl al-Sakhrah experience reversed day/night relative to earth's people, with intense heat in their day and intense cold in their night, the punishment increasing a hundredfold during the sun's revolution. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S757 | God created the Sakhrah before the earth, and in the Quran earth, Sakhrah, and sky are mentioned as three separate individualities in a single ayah. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 1/2 key terms located in section |
| S758 | Around the earth and Sakhrah revolve nine celestial spheres (aflak) with imaginary lines revolving on a plane, and seven planets (kawakib) that are circular and revolve both around and on their axes. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S759 | Beneath the Ka'bah, Maqam Ibrahim, Ghadir, and the Rawda places there is no Sakhrah but a void containing the luminous and good souls (arwah nuraniyyah, arwah hasanah), while at amyal, Wadi al-Shaytan, and the Jamarat al-Thalathah there is a great multitude of filthy souls (arwah khabithah). | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S760 | Sayyidna al-Malik states the pores' locations are not in the aflak but are free of physical limits — all spiritual and imaginary, not physical. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S761 | Quran 19:71 ('And there is none of you except he will come to it; this is upon your Lord an inevitability decreed') means everyone must arrive in human form on the earth (Sakhrah included), after which progress occurs. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S762 | The order of creation: from the remorseful (nadimin) the aflak were made; the ummahat (fire, air, water, earth-ball) were made; from the insistent (musirrin) the Sakhrah was made; and after 51,000 years of revolution the earth on which we pray was created. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S763 | The 'alam al-'uqul (World of Intellects) was made in the luminous world; the delayed tenth became the contented soul (nafs al-mutma'innah, per Quran 89:27) which ascends to take the place of the final intellect. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S764 | Upon the command 'ihbitu' (descend), some immediately repented (forming the World of Life), and the increasingly delayed ones formed the falak al-muhit (imaginary, no materiality, state of nafs), then the falak al-buruj where materiality and near-material light first appear with the fixed stars (thawabit). | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S765 | Thawabit (fixed stars) revolve on their own fixed center, while planets (sayyare/kawakib) revolve around a larger star. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S766 | The spheres of Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, and Sun derive from the nadimin and ta'ibin (repentant), while Venus, Mercury, and Moon are all from the nadimin, the more delayed but repentant elements whose effects can carry residual badness. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S767 | The Sakhrah was made from the most insistent and arrogant (musirrin, mustakbirin), and the most arrogant of all became the station of Sijjin, which contains the Tabut. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S768 | The Prophet said 'Mu'awiyah min al-Tabut' (Mu'awiyah is from the Tabut), placing him in the lowest station (asfal). | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S769 | The opposites remain in the belly of the Sakhrah until the end of the Great Cycle (al-kawr al-a'zam), which is 360,000 years multiplied by its like, equaling 129,600 million years. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S770 | Within that cycle the opposites perpetually curse and kill one another in eternal enmity and warfare, and whoever is killed is transformed into an uglier deformed person (two/three heads, faces turned backward or sideways) burning in the fires. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S771 | Upon completion of the Great Cycle, the opposites kill one another, are transformed into vapor, ascend, and exit from four vents (manafiz) at the top of the Sakhrah to the earth's surface. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S772 | The four vents are: a well called Barhut (and Shafahu) in a valley of Hadramawt (east); a well in Khurasan; a well in Arabia; and a fourth in an unnamed land — named in the four directions as Barhut/Shafahu (east), Buqa/Han/Har (west), Saydum/Jub/Kharqa (north), Sabr/Sahiq (south). | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S773 | After exiting as vapor, the souls re-enter the world through temperament and compounding (mizaj wal-muntazij), then praiseworthy minerals, plants, and lawful animals, until appearing in the millennial form (qamat al-alifiyyah) as individuals invited again to the truth — a clean-slate choice like the first perfection. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S774 | Three things created by God can never be destroyed but only temporarily hidden: existence (wujud), knowledge/intellect, and free choice (ikhtiyar/freedom); intentions made in the eternal pre-existence ('alam al-azal) are matters of this choice. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S775 | The current Qaim (qaim al-muntadhar) is cyclical and is the first cycle of the 400th cycle; 399 cyclical resurrections (qiyamahs) have already passed and we are at the start of the 400th, in its seventh satr cycle. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S776 | The true universal resurrection (qiyamat-e-kubra) is the resurrection of the cycle (qiyamah of the dawr), when 'the earth will be changed to another earth' (Quran 14:48), distinct from the partial cyclical resurrection. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S777 | Earthly life cannot be older than four lakh (400,000) years because at each cycle's end everything is destroyed and the earth begins anew; the first jussa of the current cycle manifested at Sarandip. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S778 | The 400th jussa will manifest in the Ka'bah (Khana-e Ka'bah) and will gather the 399 prior cycles (jami'-e-arba'); cyclical jussas of the past manifested at holy places like Ghadir Khumm, Rawda, Bayt al-Muqaddas, Najaf, Karbala, Maqam Ibrahim, and Hijr Aswad, which are visited, whereas Sarandip is not counted among the holy places. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S779 | The greatest jussa is the initial jussa (jussa-e-ibtida'iyyah) at the beginning of a mawr; no other jussa in the whole mawr can reach it, and the final jussa at the world's end may equal it; the true successor of a mawri jussa is the next mawr's jussa (kulli successor), not a cyclical jussa. | yes | yes | §21 (The Hudud, the Rock, and the Sacred House); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S780 | Returning to the discourse of the Qaim's state: when he appears, the reckoning (hisab) occurs, the people of punishment meet their torment, and the reward of the people of reward is multiplied. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S781 | The Qaim establishes the da'wah to extract a successor whose station is in establishing the da'wah, and he transfers with those in his fold to the rank of the Tenth Intellect (al-'aql al-'ashir), a perfect luminous intellect, one of the Primordial Intellects. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S782 | The principle 'la yukhlifu ahadun illa mithluhu' (no one succeeds except one like him): no one can be promoted until a universal (kulli) successor like himself is born; a partial (juzwi) successor does not enable promotion. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S783 | Sayyidna Sadiq told Sayyidna Jumali that where 'juzwi' is said the meaning is 'kulli' and vice versa — each term implies the other. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S784 | The Tenth Intellect ascends to the rank of the Ninth, and this Qaim, in the rank of the Tenth Intellect, becomes administrator (mudabbir) of the entire world of nature, celestial and terrestrial, perpetually transferring upward as each successor is appointed. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 2/4 key terms located in section |
| S785 | The chain of intellects ascends: ninth takes eighth's place, and so on, but the second (Munba'ith al-awwal, the First Emanation) remains in its sphere and is exalted in its second perfection (kamal-e-thani); the sphere of the First Intellect (the circle of unity, wahdat) can be reached by no one. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S786 | Every station transfers to the horizon (ufuq) of the Tenth by the customary habit until the completion of the term, which is 7,000 years (in the period of concealment, satr). | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S787 | In the dawr-e kashf the period is 10,000 years; after each 10,000 years a Qaim manifests, reaches the horizon of the primordial jussa, and the jussa places the Imams' forms under his authority; the Qaim manifesting after 50,000 years gathers all five qawa'im and becomes a universal Qaim. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S788 | After seven dawr-e kashf and seven dawr-e satr, a universal-of-universal Qaim gathers the qawa'im of the seven satr cycles (under which sit the kashf-cycle Qaims); when a qawr ends a new primordial jussa comes, is promoted, and the prior one is exalted, with each succession resting under the jussa-i ibda'iyyah's horizon (ufuq). | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S789 | At the start of the dawr-e kashf the resurrection (ba'th) of the bodies of the people of the dawr-e satr occurs; 'innal-ba'tha haqq' (indeed resurrection is true). | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S790 | The first bodies taken/resurrected are those of the people of the Muhammadan dawr, due to their nearness to the Qaim's dawr, their nobility, and their being last in the dawr-e satr; then the bodies of the awliya' whose souls ascended to the world of purity ('alam al-safa') and whose noble bodies fermented bit by bit. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S791 | Then the bodies of all the sects (firaq) and the opposites whose souls transformed in their bodies are resurrected and made to taste the lesser punishment (al-'azab al-adna), not the greater. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S792 | Each dawr is established for 7,000 years, after which its Qaim rises and faces the resurrection (ba'th) of the people of the preceding nabi's dawr, in sequence: 'Isa, then Musa, then Ibrahim, then Nuh, then Adam — completing the dawr-e kashf. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S793 | After the dawr-e kashf comes the dawr-e satr of 10,000 years, of which 3,000 years is the dawr of the people of astronomy, philosophy, and mathematics (nujum, falsafah, riyadiyyah) and 7,000 years is of Shari'ah. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S794 | The dawr of kashf and satr, the Imam and Imamate, continue in unbroken chain (one to another, child after parent, no interruption or deviation from lineage) until the passing of the qawr of Adam, which is 360,000 years multiplied by its like. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S795 | A small cycle (qawr asghar / dawr) is 400,000 years; the great cycle (qawr a'zam) is 360,000 years multiplied by itself. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S796 | At the universal resurrection, the tethers of the celestial spheres (rubatat al-aflak / pegs/gravity) loosen, everything on earth perishes (plants, animals, humans), the affair becomes as at first, and administration (tadbir) passes to Zuhal (Saturn). | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S797 | Mountains and stones are pulverized, extreme cold and piled snow, clouds, fog, atmospheric darkness arise, vapor rises, rains and rivers burst until the entire earth becomes like a turbulent sea covered in darkness, the sun's light unable to penetrate the dense clouds due to Saturn's influence. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S798 | When the time comes for the Qaim of the millennium to appear from the caves (mugharat) as at first, a pure light/substance ascends from the subtle parts (lata'if) and purest of the earth to become a successor (khalifah) to the sun and the rest of the stars and constellations. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S799 | Things from the Ka'bah ascend to take the place of the sun; the sun, the six (other) stars, and the twelve constellations descend to earth and become fine dust, from which the new primordial jussa is nurtured after the earth settles. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S800 | From the earth arise twenty-eight noble, excellent persons (fudala al-kurama) of the primordial corporeality (jussa al-ibda'iyyah); the sun becomes their Imam and chief, and the remaining stars and constellations are the twenty-seven who appear with his appearance. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S801 | The Hudud, peace be upon them, are servants (khuddam) in the world of the body ('alam al-jirm) and the world of religion ('alam al-din). | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S802 | When a Hudud's subtle essences transfer to the spiritual world at death, his body remains in the earth; for three days whatever remained of the sensory soul (nafs al-hissiyyah), also called the airy soul (nafs al-rihiyyah), ascends to the sphere of the sun. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S803 | How much of the vegetative soul (nafs al-namiyyah) ascends or remains in the grave for punishment depends on each Hudud's rank and degree of respect for the shari'ah during life. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S804 | Upon the body's dissolution it becomes pulverized dust; the flesh, blood, nerves, veins, and bodily excesses (except the bones) become subtle vapor, ascend to the sphere of the constellations (falak al-buruj), take the place of a constellation, then descend to earth as dust, then to temperament and the blended, returning to edibles that do not enter fire. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S805 | The nourishment becomes a nutfah, then a person who is invited and answers, joining the awliya', ascending in the gnostic sciences (ma'arif) to become one of the serving Hudud in the Da'wat of Truth, with his final rank matching his initial rank. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S806 | A believer crosses one rank per cycle (there are ten ranks for a believer); only a martyr (shahid) crosses one or two ranks at once ('darajatan aw darajatayn'). | yes | yes | §22. martyr-crossing-ranks rule confirmed verbatim ("darajatan aw darajatayn", refined-english.md:4908); term match low only due to translit variance |
| S807 | The seventeen-fold du'a of light (Nuran fi...) names ten internal locations (heart, hearing, sight, tongue, hair, skin, blood, flesh, bones, nerves) and seven directions (front, behind, right, left, above, below, in the grave). | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S808 | The bones, due to their hardness (salabah), remain in the earth a long period as the Disposer (mudabbir) wills; they are then ground to dust, ascend as vapor to the temperament and blended, return to earth, and solidify in mines as rubies, pearls, and precious stored jewels. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/6 key terms located in section |
| S809 | A human comprises an essence/soul (the divine creation), a sensory soul, and a vegetative soul; the luminous form (rational soul), developed through knowledge and action and free will granted by God, becomes greater than the soul itself. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S810 | The vegetative soul is purified by ascending to the moon via the sun's rays, refined with the sun, passing through Mercury and Venus, then descending until the physical form (nasut) of the Imam's progeny is formed, after which the connection of Lahut (divinity) is established — this nasut-Lahut union is the meaning of 'La ilaha illallah'. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S811 | The full return cycle is 1,000 years: 360 years residing in twelve constellations (30 years each) ascending and 360 returning, plus 280 years for ascending/descending to manifest as a human, so that a believer manifests again as a believer with consolidated point. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S812 | In each cycle one soul is consumed/transformed (the crimson heart-blood taking the form of soul); over 360 cycles (360,000 years) a person of straight path who avoided major sins reaches the station of the heart, and a respondent attains the rank of a natiq. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S813 | At the time of return, all the dispersed elements (vegetative soul, sensory soul, form, body, points) are assembled at the Qaim (the universal-intellect court), where individuality both merges and remains distinct, ultimately forming a single whole that seeks to become the Universal Intellect. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S814 | The primordial jussa (jussa-i ibda'iyyah) that gathers the great cycle holds all the points (nuqat) from a single respondent up to a Qaim, each point's intensity (hiddat) established according to the intensity of faith in the prior cycle. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S815 | The bones progress and solidify in mines as jewels, preserved long because they must manifest with the primordial jussa; old bones preserved from past cycles come to take the place of the body that has gone. | yes | yes | §22. bones-solidify-in-mines-as-jewels confirmed verbatim ("yanʿaqidu fi'l-maʿadin yaqutatan wa durran", refined-english.md:4916); translit variance only |
| S816 | When the Great Cycle's fulfillment arrives, the jewels (transformed bones) are ground, ascend, and become planets succeeding the seven planets and twelve constellations, preserving their places arranged by rank until the supports of the heavens loosen at the next cycle. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S817 | From the descending planets/constellations becoming dust, the physical form (nasut) of the primordial jussa is formed; the sun becomes the unique individual of the rank of oneness (the Qaim in the station of the First Originator), and the moon becomes its gate (bab) receiving the ascending forms. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S818 | All the Hudud follow in service upon this pattern until all their bodies are purified, and nothing of them remains in the world of generation and corruption ('alam al-kawn wal-fasad) except returning to the abode of purity and eternity — honored, gifted, rich, forgiven, living, eternal, happy. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S819 | Quran 37:61 ('and for the like of this let the workers work') is cited as an exhortation to strive for this eternal return. | yes | yes | §22. Quran 37:61 ("Wa li-mithli hadha fal-yaʿmali'l-ʿamilun") confirmed verbatim with gloss + exhortation (refined-english.md:4966); "al-" prefix vs "l-" caused translit miss |
| S820 | The paradise for the soul is Jannat al-Ma'wa (Sidrat al-Muntaha); the paradise for the body is the paradise of the presences (jannat al-hadarat) — worldly peace and comfort free of social strife — distinct from the realm of kashf. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S821 | In the lofty paradises (jannat al-'aliyat) fasting is not obligatory and there is no hunger or hardship, since obligations of hardship are removed in the state of free choice. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S822 | The seven planets and twelve constellations total nineteen, equal to the numerical value of Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S823 | Of the 354-day (lunar) year, 100 days are the highest, marked by a tasbih; within these, 18 special days include 1st and 10th Muharram, 1st/15th/27th Rajab, 15th Sha'ban, the odd nights of Ramadan, Eid al-Fitr, Yawm Nuzulika, 'Arafah, Eid al-Adha, the three Ayyam al-Tashriq, and 18th Dhu'l-Hijjah. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S824 | The 18th of Dhu'l-Hijjah, Yawm al-Ghadir, is the most excellent day of the year; Imam Ja'far al-Sadiq affirmed three times by oath ('No, by God') that no day is better than it. | yes | yes | §22 (The Creative Sphere and the Resurrection Cycle); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S825 | In the common speech of the exoteric people, at the time of death there must come a mubashshir and bashir (bearers of glad tidings) for the people of good, and Munkar and Nakir for the people of evil, and these are two angels present with the dying person. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S826 | This saying about the two angels at death is a true saying heard from the people of truth (ahl al-haqq), though its inner reality became hidden from the world. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S827 | Every Natiq of a cycle (dawr) and his Wasi are themselves the two angels referred to in their era: mubashshir and bashir for their friends (awliya') and Munkar and Nakir for their opponents (addad). | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S828 | The two angels are a single personality that appears as Munkar and Nakir to opponents and as mubashshir and bashir to the friends, depending on whether the person's end is with good and love or not. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S829 | Likewise every Imam of the Time and his Hujjat is mubashshir and bashir for his friends and Munkar and Nakir for their opponents, so this reality persists in every age. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S830 | Amir al-Mu'minin Ali said in verse to Harith Hamdan: whoever dies, believer or hypocrite, will see me soon; he recognizes me with his eye and I recognize him by his name and kunya and what he did. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S831 | The reality is that the form/imagination (khayal) of every station (Natiq, Wasi, Imam) at the time of each one's death moves the Pillar of Light ('umud al-nur), which flows from the world of holiness ('alam al-quds) and is connected to the form of every wali, the Master of the Time. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S832 | Between the Originator (mubdi') and the originated (mubda') is a Word (kalimah), which is the kalimah 'La ilaha illa Allah,' and from it the Pillar of Light is connected. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S833 | All luminous forms up to the First Intellect ('aql al-awwal) are connected to the kalimah 'La ilaha'; above the First Intellect is the kalimah, which connects to the Originator, and no one can pass beyond the Originator. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S834 | The detailed explanation of the Pillar of Light ('umud al-nur) has come in Akhbar al-Latifa. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S835 | The Pillar of Light has different centers, the largest being the First Intellect, then the 'ashir, then the Imam of the time, like graded centers of electricity, and all good forms are connected to it like a beehive. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S836 | At the last minute of his divine life and righteous deed, the one transitioning imagines a luminous person, rejoices and is delighted at seeing it, and knows by its pleasure that he is headed toward good as he departs the moment. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S837 | That luminous form is the form the believer acquired from the walayah (friendship/allegiance) of the people of truth, their divine knowledges, and their righteous deeds with the friends of God. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S838 | Mawlana al-Sadiq alluded to this, transmitted by Sayyidna al-Nu'man in Da'a'im al-Islam: when God raises the believer from his grave, a beautiful image (mithal hasan) emerges with him and says through the hardships 'Do not fear, there is no harm upon you.' | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S839 | That luminous image is the believer's real master/guardian, acting as a supervisory angel over his body, keeping watch over every part of it. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S840 | The image continually reassures and gives glad tidings to the believer until it brings him to God, who calls him to an easy reckoning (hisab yasir). | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S841 | When the believer asks the image who it is, it answers: 'I am a creation from the creation of my Lord, created from the joy (surur) you used to bring upon the believers, so today I make you happy.' | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S842 | By God's decree, no particle can go astray; like a magnet each particle draws its connected particles, so the believer's parts cannot be scattered and progress to their station. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S843 | This is the reality of mubashshir and bashir for the friend (wali). | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S844 | For the opponent, the evil dark magnet — the two knots (al-'uqdatan) — by the movement of the station moves the satanic dissolver (al-muhill al-iblisi) and the evil deeds, knowledge, enmity, and hatred he harbored against the people of truth. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S845 | One center (the Pillar of Light) was made for the good; two (the two knots) were made for the evil — only one is mentioned for the Pillar of Light but the knots are two. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S846 | The two knots were formed at the head of the snake when God made the celestial spheres (aflak); high beings of the world of light who took precedence into satanic nature (iblisiyyat) yet repented were placed there, kept high beneath the sphere of the moon rather than with the hellfire (saqar), by a specific intention of the 'ashir within divine providence. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S847 | Through the movement of the Imam's station, the decree (tadbir) of the Imam is in effect on the two knots, so the Imam also grants the requital of his opponents' evil deeds. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S848 | The opponent's dark disfigured form is what he acquired bit by bit from his evil; at his last minute he imagines a dark, disfigured, most-ugly person which he rejects, fears, and is horrified by, then it is cut off and he knows he is of the people of evil — this is the reality of Munkar and Nakir. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S849 | The Messenger of God alluded to this in his sermon to Qays ibn 'Asim: you have a companion who is buried with you alive while you are buried dead, and he will not be resurrected except with you. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S850 | If the companion is noble he honors you, and if base he abandons you; you are raised and questioned only about him, so make him only righteous — for he is your deed ('amal): your luminous form built by knowledge and deed is your provision for the hereafter, higher than your self (zat). | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S851 | If the companion is righteous you find comfort only in him, and if obscene you feel terror only from him, for he is your deed. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S852 | Sayyidna Hamiduddin alluded to this in the third mashrab of the fourth tasawwur of Rahat al-'Aql where the opposite is mentioned: the interpretation of one's evil deeds is a harmful animal that harms and terrifies him. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S853 | Rahat al-'Aql by Sayyidna Hamiduddin is profoundly difficult (mughlaq); a learned Arabic scholar (the Nawab of Rampur) could not understand it, and Mawlana called it 'Kulfat al-'Aql' (agony of the intellect) for the uninitiated. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S854 | The dark form (surat zulmaniyyah) is formed from knowledge and deeds done contrary to God's commands, knowledge acquired from people from whom it should not be taken, and enmity held against the people of truth. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S855 | The dark form separates from the souls of bodies; the form's station is the two knots (uqdatayn), while the soul, instead of progressing with the form, expands within the body and acquires materiality — the opposite of believers' souls which progress with the form. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S856 | The dark form roams (ja'ilah) in the air and in vile places — latrines, places where blood flows, where wine and filthy forbidden things are made — because it is inclined toward what is forbidden. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 1/2 key terms located in section |
| S857 | The dark forms reach toward the barzakh which is dark and vile, namely the two knots (uqdatan); barzakh is the path between paradise and hell, and the uqdatayn is the quarantine/short-stay station before reaching their final hell. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S858 | For the punished (mu'adhdhabin) the uqdatayn (beneath the sphere of the moon) is the barzakh; their final destination is the sakhrah, where they remain until the Great Cycle (kawr-e-a'zam) of four hundred thousand cycles, each cycle being four hundred thousand years. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S859 | The dark forms pass in turn through the air, vile places, empty habitations, and terrifying wells (bir muhawwishah) before reaching the uqdatayn, and they are the blameworthy jinn given the subtle lower punishment each according to his measure. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S860 | It is necessary that every Imam in his time calls the punished jinn to Islam, for the Imam knows them by their names and places — as Sayyidna Ali did, calling each by name with nothing of their affair hidden from him. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S861 | Whoever among the punished completes his lower punishment ('adhab al-adna) and is permitted deliverance submits to the one who calls him, repents, and becomes one of the praiseworthy jinn (jinn al-mahmudin). | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S862 | Upon becoming a praiseworthy jinn, he ascends from the side of the moon to the sphere of the sun, serving there as a servant (khadim) among the mustaqdimin in a glorious station. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S863 | The jinn-e-mustaqdimin respond to the call to prayer (adhan); Sayyidna Ali bin Sulayman recounts a crow that came at the adhan guiding lost travelers to a village, an instance illustrating the mustaqdimin. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S864 | The adhan recited by the saints (e.g. Hazrat Fathullah, Hazrat Ishaq, Hazrat Nasrullah, and the narrator at Arafat during Hajj) brought about miraculous reversals — receding floodwaters, redirected fires and winds. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S865 | There are one hundred and forty thousand mustaqdimin (suwarat-e-mustaqdimin) centered in the sun, who are entrusted ministering angels (mala'ikah). | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 1/2 key terms located in section |
| S866 | Among the mustaqdimin are attractors (jadhibah) who draw up the praiseworthy forms, repellers (dafi'ah) who send forms down to the world, and restrainers (masikah) for what travels within; when one ascends another descends in his place to be born a believer, perpetually. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S867 | The one hundred and forty thousand mustaqdimin and the one hundred and twenty-four thousand prophets (anbiya') are separate counts and must not be confused. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S868 | The second chapter (al-bab al-thani) of the fourth path contains five sections (fusul). | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S869 | Whoever among the mustakhdamin (those made to serve) completes his service is brought down to earth and travels into a thing from the edibles (ma'kulat) and drinkables (mashrubat), taking sustenance suited to him. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S870 | He then appears at the union (mulaqah) between the seed (nutfah) of the da'i and his wife as a trustee, progresses through the stages of the fetus (janin) until born a complete person, and is raised until puberty (bulugh). | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S871 | At puberty he is called and responds, entering the circle of the people of truth (ahl al-haqq); he can be born to anyone but will come to the people of truth. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S872 | Because his purification is done and his service accepted, he progresses through the religious ranks (maratib al-diniyyah) in the swiftest and nearest manner, reaching the high ranks and lofty stations with no need ever to return. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S873 | He never returns because he fulfilled what was upon him of sins, repented, and accepted the caller of God (da'i Allah) in the beginning and in the end — in the beginning he accepted the covenant (qawl) in the world of light, and again here. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S874 | His swift progress is because he rendered his service, stood by his obligation, entered the sacred sanctuary (haram-e-amin), and obeyed the Lord of the worlds. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S875 | On the platform of this world gather various kinds of people — those destined for punishment by their prior states, and those who arrive with a clean slate now in the realm of free choice to accept or not. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S876 | As animals are halal or haram, so among arrivals some will progress (halal) and some descend (haram), and within halal some become food for a believer and some food for a disbeliever (kafir). | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S877 | In the realm of free choice no one can pull a person from his choice between the straight and crooked path; the call is presented and he chooses. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S878 | Dark forms (suwarat-e-zulmah) can only progress by becoming mustakhdam: held in the uqdatayn, they complete the lower punishment, serve, are presented with the call, and if accepting become mustakhdam. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S879 | The station of the cursed forms (surat-e-la'inah) whose end was not good is the sakhrah; their barzakh is the uqdatayn, their bodies undergo the lower punishment in the world, and bodily purification occurs through halal animals. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S880 | The mustakhdamin remain in service until the rising of the Qa'im (qiyam-e-qa'im), after which accounting occurs, fire descends from the sky to burn the enemies' bodies, and they enter the sakhrah through its mouth, remaining there for one kawr — the greater punishment. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S881 | The lower punishment ('adhab-e-adna) has two forms: the punishment of the seven levels of hell (adrak) and the punishment of the peripheries (atraf); the great punishment ('adhab-e-akbar) is the punishment of the sakhrah. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S882 | Whoever refuses, is arrogant, and persists in disobedience and disbelief is made to tread the bridges (qanatir) of the lower bodily punishment until the Qa'im stands, then is made to tread the greater punishment. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S883 | Sayyidna Zu'ayb alluded in his Risalat al-Nafs: at every transition there is a halt (tawqif); if they repent they return to a higher rank, and if they insist they descend to the lowest rank, to the Great Cycle, the product of three hundred and sixty thousand multiplied by its like. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S884 | God said He will make them taste the lower punishment short of the greater punishment so that perhaps they may return — evidence that the lower punishment offers repeated chances to return, a mercy of God. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S885 | Amir al-Mu'minin indicated this in his saying 'wa yathanuhum al-dawru fi madarij al-kawr' — the cycle grinds them in the stages of the cycle; in every cycle they are presented the call, and if they reach the sakhrah, lava comes out of it. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S886 | The white days (ayyam-e-bid) are the thirteenth, fourteenth, and fifteenth, the best of days; of the 354 days in a year about one hundred fifty are distinguished days. | yes | yes | §23 (The Knots, the Barzakh, and the Great Kawr); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S887 | Changing the vowels (zabar, zer, pesh) or dropping/consuming words alters Allah's speech in the Qur'an by the time it reaches the servant, and this alteration becomes a cause of sin. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S888 | The completion (khatm) prayer affirms 'Sadaqa Allah' — Allah has spoken the truth, there is no god but Him, the Most Gracious and Merciful, nothing is like unto Him, and He is All-Hearing All-Knowing; His messengers delivered His messages and we are witnesses, in defiance of the deniers (irghaman lil-jahidin). | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S889 | The one completing the Qur'an prays that God benefit him with it, make it the spring of hearts, healing of chests, remover of worries, atonement for sins, purification, and a guide and proof toward Him and toward the gardens of bliss in the company of prophets, truthful, martyrs, and righteous. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S890 | The completion prayer asks God to overlook errors in recitation — mistakes, forgetfulness, altering words from their places, changing a letter, transposition, addition, omission, wrong interpretation, doubt, haste, slips of the tongue, wrong stopping, wrong merging, wrong clarity, errors in extension, emphasis, hamza, jazm, or vocalization other than as written. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 7/8 key terms located in section |
| S891 | One must be extremely careful in saying, writing, or speaking the Qur'an because scribes often write something else and changes occur in Allah's word. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S892 | Adding words such as a virtue-saying within Yasin is an addition (ziyadah) and a sin; the letters of the Qur'an must not be changed, though words of separate prayers differ. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S893 | Madd (elongation) occurs when a vowel letter (alif, waw, ya) is followed by an alif or hamza, in order to clarify both letters distinctly (e.g. ula'ika, yasha'u, qa'idina). | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S894 | There are six guttural letters (huruf halqi) — ha, kha, 'ayn, ghayn, and others — that must be pronounced properly from the throat; the science of recitation (ilm al-tajwid) details this. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S895 | Tashdid doubles a letter for two durations and must be pronounced clearly (e.g. iyya, sabbar); the final letter (e.g. the dal) must not be dropped. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S896 | The prayer continues: awaken us before death from the sleep of heedlessness, secure us from the punishment of the grave and the questioning of Munkar and Nakir and from being eaten by worms (didan), make our faces white on the Day of Resurrection, free our necks from the Fires, and protect from the evil of the reckoning and loss. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S897 | The vocalizations (i'rab) — zabar, zer, pesh — are placed upon the 28 letters of the alphabet. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S898 | The 28 letters correspond to the 28 of the Qisa-i Ibda'iyah; the Juz'-i Ibda'i manifested them materially in the world, having received them from the First Intellect via the kalimah 'La ilaha illa Allah,' which contains 28. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S899 | The vowels (i'rab) were placed on the 28 letters in the World of Intentions ('alam al-niyyat); the First Emanation (Munba'ith al-Awwal) also has 28, and every circle formed in every intellect, including the Imam's, reduces to 28 — a uniquely special number. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S900 | Each emanated being was given three things — life/existence, knowledge, and free will; those who used free will to come foremost (pesh-pesh) received a pesh, those who delayed remained with zabar, and those lower with zer, so the ranks of movement are pesh (highest), then zabar, then zer. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/5 key terms located in section |
| S901 | Jazm is a bent/crooked zabar (movement not straight); madd combines two movements; the doubled forms (maddo, madda, maddi) join a jazm with pesh, zabar, or zer respectively, signifying a joining of crooked and straight. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S902 | Madd commands elongation wherever two entities of the same nature meet whose distinction in rank must be clarified, because God created two same-rank entities that must differ — two individuals cannot hold the identical status. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S903 | These letters that came from the realm of spirits and the kalimah 'La ilaha illa Allah' constitute the original Arabic; the Qur'an names no language but Arabic ('bi-lisanin 'arabiyyin mubin'), the language God chose for His expression. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S904 | While reading the Qur'an the entire letter must be pronounced completely and clearly; turning a zabar into a zer or otherwise mispronouncing changes the meaning entirely (illustrated by the Arabic vs. Hindi 'ha' distinction). | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S905 | It is good for all to read aloud in the gathering so mistakes can be known and corrected, or to read privately for correction, removing the barrier of reading. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 0/0 key terms located in section |
| S906 | The Maqamat (the Imams, peace be upon them) issue a call (du'a) both to humans, who are mortals, and to the jinn, who appear as 'these forms' (suwar). | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S907 | The purpose of the Imams' call (tawqif, to make aware) is so that whoever is destined to turn back may turn back and whoever will insist on disbelief may insist. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S908 | Faith is established through three things: by word, by bringing truth into the heart, and by action. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S909 | Jinn who accept the call are assigned services within the rank of Shams and are then called jinn-e-mahmud (praised jinn) and mustaqdimin (the forerunners). | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S910 | There is a permanent congregation of one hundred and forty thousand in the Shams; when one departs or falls, another becomes his successor (khalifa), keeping the number constant. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S911 | The call to these wretched forms and the response of those who answer is a manifestation of the miracle the Maqam reveals to those around him from among the ally (wali), the opponent (zidd), and the hypocrite (munafiq). | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S912 | The miracle serves to make the establishment of proof (iqamat al-hujjah) greater and to reveal the clear path (mahajjah) more swiftly, so that the People of the Book gain certainty and believers increase in faith. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S913 | The miracle's purpose is so that he who is to perish perishes by a clear proof and he who is to live lives by a clear proof. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S914 | The Messenger of Allah sent companions of Amir al-Mumineen to Yemen with the command to call the jinn dwelling in the valley of Zahr and Musayyab. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S915 | The Prophet informed the companions of the jinn's names so they could call those jinn by name in the desolate well. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 1/2 key terms located in section |
| S916 | The Prophet declared that whoever the jinn answer would be the wasi (successor) of the Prophet — an incident narrated in detail in Sayyidna Hatim's work (Saluni). | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S917 | The Prophet ordered that Abu Bakr call the jinn first; if they did not respond then Umar, then Uthman, and finally Ali ibn Abi Talib. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S918 | The Prophet declared that whoever among them the jinn answer is his wasi among them and his khalifa over them. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S919 | The Prophet declared that whoever obeys his answered successor has obeyed the Prophet and Allah, and whoever disobeys him has disobeyed the Prophet and Allah. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S920 | The Prophet's arrangement was meant to urge the companions toward obedience to and recognition (ma'rifah) of Amir al-Mumineen. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S921 | In Arabic grammar, the particle 'ila' is the last of the seventeen letters that govern the genitive (zer/kasra), yet the noun 'haythu' remains indeclinable (mabni) and is always read 'haythu' even after 'ila'. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S922 | Abu Bakr went forward alone and called out loudly to the named jinn, but no one answered, so he returned to his companions disappointed. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S923 | Umar likewise went forward and called the jinn loudly, but no one answered, and he returned disappointed. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S924 | Uthman did as his two companions had done, calling the jinn, and no one answered, so he too returned disappointed. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S925 | Ali ibn Abi Talib then went forward, his companions secretly following to observe; when he called out loudly the jinn answered one after another and responded to him. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S926 | Ali greeted the jinn with peace and they returned the greeting, addressing him as Amir al-Mumineen with 'wa alaykum al-salam wa rahmatullahi wa barakatuh'. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S927 | Ali then called the jinn to the two testimonies (shahadatayn): 'There is no god but Allah, Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah'. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S928 | Those jinn who testified affirmed Allah's Oneness (wahdaniyyah) and Muhammad's Messengership and servitude (abduhu wa rasuluh), and Ali's wilayah as Amir al-Mumineen. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S929 | Two of the jinn took bodily form and accompanied Ali; they were named Abdullah Mard-shah and Abdullah Ard-shah. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S930 | The two jinn reached the Messenger of Allah, renewed their Islam before him, and their Islam became excellent, then they returned to their companions at the Well of Alam. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S931 | This affair was among the greatest miracles for the Natiq and the Wasi, yet it increased the hypocrites (the first and second who went) only in distance and disbelief. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S932 | Amir al-Mumineen descended into the Well of Dhat al-Alam twice to commune with the jinn there, killing those of them he killed and accepting the Islam of those who submitted — a separate incident from the San'a (Wadi Zahr/Musayyab) episode. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S933 | Ham bin al-Him came to the Prophet while he sat in his mosque among the Muslims and greeted him with a voice that was heard though his person was not seen. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S934 | Ham identified himself as Ham son of Him, son of Laqis, son of Iblis, and stated he had accepted Islam for the Prophets Nuh, then Ibrahim, then Musa, then Isa. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S935 | Ham bore witness that there is no god but Allah and that Muhammad is His servant and Messenger, which was among the greatest miracles, yet it increased the disbelievers only in aversion. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S936 | After his Islam for Nuh, Ham ascended to the rank of Shams through the intermediary of the Qamar (moon). | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S937 | At the appearance of each successive Natiq (Ibrahim, then Musa and Isa, then Muhammad) Ham descended from his rank to accept Islam anew for that Natiq, then returned to his place. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S938 | In Arabic, the names Ibrahim, Ishaq, and Yaqub are non-Arab (Hebrew) and indeclinable, taking a zabar (fatha) rather than zer (kasra) even in the genitive case (li Ibrahima). | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S939 | When Muhammad appeared Ham descended to accept Islam for him, was then made to die (tuwuffiya), and returned in the swiftest time to the lofty stature (qamah saniyah). | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S940 | It is necessary for everyone among the jinn who accepted Islam in past eras to accept Islam for Muhammad, who is the sixth Natiq. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S941 | By the completion of Muhammad (the sixth Natiq) the creation of the religious body (jism al-dini) is completed. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S942 | Such a believing jinn ascends through the ranks until reaching the rank of the Bab (Gate), which is the limit of infallibility (hadd al-ismah). | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S943 | The rapid ascent of ranks up to the Bab applies to the mustaqdimin (forerunners), who because of their long service can attain a rank higher than ordinary believers. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S944 | Both the Imam and the Bab al-Abwab (Gate of Gates) possess ismah (infallibility). | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S945 | Amir al-Mumineen declared from the pulpit 'Saluni qabla an tafqiduni' (Ask me before you lose me), an incident recorded in Saluni and in Tawil al-Da'a'im. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S946 | When asked 'Where is Jibril?'
