Ali looked in every direction, could not find Jibril, and concluded that the questioner himself was Jibril (Ana azunnu anta Jibril), naming his lineage — a sign of Ali's knowledge. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S947 | The evil form (surat al-khabitha) separated from souls is the utmost evil for wickedness, standing in opposition to the noble end which is the utmost good. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S948 | Even evil forms sometimes speak true things and give accurate news because, while truthful in the era of the natiq, they rendered service and the truth they earned is rewarded and never lost. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S949 | Evil has no root in creation (al-sharr la asla lahu fil-ibda'), so a person's earlier truth and good service survive even after corruption and are rewarded. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S950 | Allah's justice does not let any deed, small or large, of good or evil be wasted, as proven by the verse 'whoever does an atom's weight of good/evil will see it'. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S951 | The apparent desert ghouls (al-ghilan al-zahira) mentioned by the Messenger affect mainly bedouins and children, and amulets and incantations work only out of fear of their evil. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S952 | Recitation and seeking refuge (ta'awwudh) at sleep — including Qul a'udhu, and the supplication against Allah's plan/forgetting His remembrance/heedlessness — was commanded specifically as protection from these ghouls. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S953 | Seeking refuge (saying A'udhu billahi min al-rijs al-najis) is prescribed in dark, filthy places like urinals out of fear of the evil forms there. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S954 | Jinn who submit (aslama) from the high-station forms are the praiseworthy jinn (al-jinn al-mahmudun); those who persist in disobedience are the blameworthy jinn (al-jinn al-madhmumun). | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S955 | Many in the Rightly-Guiding Mission (Da'wat al-Hadiya) deny the jinn's existence due to scarcity of knowledge, self-sufficiency, and abandoning allegiance to the appointed hudud. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S956 | The jinn are the people of inward knowledge (ahl 'ilm al-batin) and humans the people of outward knowledge (ahl 'ilm al-zahir), and both the interpretive sense and a real physical existence are simultaneously true. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S957 | The people of the inward are called jinn because of their concealment (ijnan/satr), and they divide into praiseworthy and blameworthy types. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S958 | The praiseworthy inward folk are those ordered in the thread of truth and its ranks, holding fast to the laws of tawhid, knowing the stations — and they are the divine philosophers (falasifat al-ilahiyyun). | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S959 | The blameworthy second type are the negating philosophers (falasifat al-mu'attilun) who deny the intermediaries — the nuqaba', awsiyya', a'imma and their hudud — and are more astray than all sects, being the devils and Satans. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S960 | Among humans, the praiseworthy uphold the unaltered statutes of the shari'a with chastity, purity and soundness of soul, free of hatred for the people of truth, and they are few. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S961 | The blameworthy humans outwardly uphold the shari'a and display piety but conceal falsehood and hostility to the people of the Da'wat al-Hadiya, and they alter the law. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S962 | Allah commanded wiping (mash) the head and feet in ablution; one who washes the feet believing washing is the obligation (rather than wiping) invalidates his prayer. | yes | yes | §24 (The Letters of the Quran and the Foul Soul); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S963 | The virtuous, knowledgeable, active person persists until he becomes prepared to establish the shari'a and receive revelation, and on leaving the world becomes an angel in actuality (malak bil-fi'l), entering the company of angels. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S964 | The Author of the Epistles' statement alludes to the People of Truth who uphold the truth in the two worships — outward worship being action ('amal) and inward worship being knowledge ('ilm) — connected to the divine stations. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S965 | The assembly of angels (zumrat al-mala'ikah) the virtuous soul enters is the noble temple (al-haykal al-sharif). | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S966 | Through ignorance of his Originator and His friends the opposite becomes an enemy of his Lord, claims what he does not deserve, and becomes the Imam of error and chief of ignorance. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S967 | When the opposite departs the world with his body he becomes the shaitan of his own soul; his soul does not leave the body but descends to the lowest of the low, whispering and inspiring his likes with specious words just as angels inspire prophets. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S968 | Ruh and Nafs are often synonymous, but the rational soul (nafs al-natiqah) is distinct from the vegetative (namiyah) and sensory (hissiyah) souls; the Ruh is the command of the Lord (Qul al-ruhu min amri Rabbi), the divine support (ta'idat) descending as the first perfection. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S969 | Death is the departure of the nafs (soul) from the body, not the departure of the Ruh (the command). | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S970 | By 'his ignorance of his Originator' the speaker means the opposite is no gnostic of Allah nor of His hudud who clarify the sciences of tawhid, tanzih and tajrid and make Him known in the beginning, the return, and its end. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S971 | Tawhid means each hadd is created unique in essence so the hierarchy holds; tanzih is declaring Allah transcendent of all attributes; tajrid is that the creation of beings (ijad al-zawat) belongs to none but the First Originator (Mubdi' al-Awwal). | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S972 | By 'his Lord' in 'he became an enemy to his Lord' is meant the Imam of the Time; the false claim is partnership (shirk) in the Imam's station and claim to his rank, fulfilling 'We made them leaders inviting to the Fire'. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S973 | By 'satanic soul' is meant the evil conception (tasawwur) the opposite formed from enmity to the Imam and the hudud; it alone departs and is called a satanic soul, just as the People of Truth's conception is called an angelic soul. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S974 | The sensory soul does not depart the body but transforms with the body's transformation toward the noble rank. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S975 | The vegetative soul descends from the sphere of the moon, the sensory soul from the sun, and the rational soul from Mujrima; their three centers are moon, sun, and Mujrima respectively. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S976 | At death the luminous form (surat nuraniyyah) separates and ascends to the person's higher rank (hadd-e 'ali) and merges with its form, so that if that hadd later strays the good form can be detached and preserved since Allah does not waste good. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S977 | The departing evil form roams between the celestial sphere and earth, presenting itself to people in human or satanic form, appearing in dreams to evil souls, and uniting with dark bodies to speak blasphemies, abominations, and predictions through their tongues. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S978 | Hearers of the evil form's speech regard it as truthful in all it predicts, because in the era of the natiq it had rendered service and spoken truth, for which it is compensated by accurate prediction. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S979 | Evil spirits have freedom because they lack physical constraints, so they can descend on people in sleep and utter truths mixed with disbelief; their words must not be verified or treated as karamat. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S980 | Following such spirits' mixed truth and falsehood causes destruction (halak) for the hearer; the straight path is obedience to Allah, the Messenger, and those in authority (Uli'l-amr). | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S981 | Both humans and jinn act as the whispering enemy behind the scenes; refuge is sought from both in the Mu'awwidhatayn (Qul a'udhu). | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S982 | After leaving a possessed body the evil form ascends to the two knots ('uqdatan), the dark celestial barzakh that magnetically attracts it, where its satanism and enmity become firmly rooted before descending again to earth. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S983 | Evil spirits' station is at the markers (amyal) around the Ka'bah and at Safa, Marwah and the place of pelting, but not inside the Ka'bah or in the sacred presences (hadarat) where angels descend. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S984 | Gathered at the two knots, the evil forms increase in satanism, disbelief, distance and denial, then descend to earth and reach the thousand-year stature (al-qamat al-alfiyyah), becoming devils in actuality (shayatin bil-fi'l). | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S985 | Evil forms attach to whoever opposes the People of Truth, inciting and aiding them, until their time to take retributions (qisasat) comes; they cannot act against everyone, only those with whom dues (baqiyat) are pending. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S986 | The lowest of the low (asfal al-safilin) is the rank these devils occupy because there is no rank beneath it nor anything viler than it; refuge is sought from their evil breaths (nufus) and suggestions (hamazat). | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S987 | When the disobedient souls reach the final link of the chain prepared for their punishment, the angels of punishment plunge them in and the wrathful angel (malik al-ghadban) drives them to the form of pure darkness. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S988 | In the rock (sakhrah) the punished soul perceives in its own essence the figures of the chain it traversed and the waves of Hell, raised to the heat of the ether and dropped to the cold of zamharir, with devils mingling and angels stoning it with meteors. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S989 | The lesser punishment ('adhab al-adna) comprises the seven stages (adrak) of the chain and the punishment of the four peripheries (atraf); the greater punishment ('adhab al-akbar) is the rock, which comes after the Qa'im appears. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S990 | After completing peripheral punishments the evil souls and bodies are present before the Qa'im, who judges them; fire descends from their forms, burns body and soul, and they enter the rock through its gates. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S991 | The dark form (surat zulmaniyyah) has no permanence (baqa) because it was created by a wrong method, while the luminous form (surat nuraniyyah), light and truth have permanence — though not eternity — for evil/darkness is merely distance from truth/light. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S992 | When a person passes through punishments, his evil conception (tasawwur) is finished, and after the punishments end the residual evil in his essence is also finished; the soul then re-enters the world of choice with a fresh series and is given free choice. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S993 | The lesser punishment is the seven stages God mentioned: 'Seize him and shackle him, then cast him into Hell, then insert him into a chain whose length is seventy cubits'. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S994 | After judgment by the Qa'im, the rays of Saturn (Zuhal) and Mars (Mirrikh) and the two knots hand the evil forms over to the middle of the rock, where the Sun, Moon, Venus and Mercury have no access. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 8/8 key terms located in section |
| S995 | The punishment in the middle of the rock lasts as long as the heavens and earth endure — the Greatest Cycle (qawl-e a'zam) of 360,000 solar years (400,000 lunar) multiplied by itself. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S996 | 'And the wrathful angel drives it' refers to the Tenth Intellect ('aql al-'ashir), the administrator (mudabbir) and owner of the entire world of nature who arranges everything in its rank and is wrathful against every sinner. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S997 | The Tenth Intellect is the gate of the world of sanctity ('alam al-quds) just as the sun is the gate of the world of nature and the hujjah is the religious gate (bab dini) of each Imam. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S998 | Ridwan, treasurer of paradise (khazin al-jinan), is the name for the gate serving righteous souls, while malik al-ghazban serves sinners; both are servants of the Tenth Intellect, the end of every soul. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S999 | The sun is named after Ridwan because it is a magnet for every praiseworthy thing and the destination of pleased spiritual and righteous outward souls; bad things are drawn to Saturn and Mars. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1000 | Auspicious times are determined by planetary hours; on Friday the first hour is Venus (Zuhra), then Mercury, Moon, Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, with the time stated after reciting Bismillah. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S1001 | The name malik al-ghazban also falls upon Saturn (Zuhal), who is in charge of causing sinners' souls to fall beneath the rock by its rays and the rays of the two knots. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1002 | In the rock the punished souls retain full memory of every stage of the lesser and greater torment they traversed through their disobedience to the lofty stations and their hudud — and this remembering is itself a torment. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1003 | The northern direction is the center of zamharir (cold) and the southern direction the center of ether (heat); the punished journey there after fully receiving the torment of the cycles. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1004 | The 'devils falling with it' refers to the punished souls' misleading scholars and chiefs who are entrusted alongside them in these loathsome torments. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1005 | The angels stoning them with meteors of torment is what flows to them from the curses of the disembodied intellects ('uqul mujarradah) and from the hudud and believers cursing them, spreading their vices and praying against them, since the hudud are angels in potentiality. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1006 | Being driven off from every side refers to bringing the evil forms to the filthy plains (biqa' khabithah) prepared for their torment and removing them from the inhabited land, which is a decorated paradise by comparison. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1007 | Amir al-Mu'minin recited Surat al-A'la for a month at Fajr, explaining its closing verses ('this is in the former scriptures of Abraham and Moses') as the inheritance of the prophets' scriptures received from the Prophet. | yes | yes | §25 (The Angels, the Souls, and the Law of Retribution); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S1008 | The scale of justice (mizan al-'adl) is established by Allah upon all His servants — wali, exoteric person, or opponent (didd) — leaving out neither small nor large deed but enumerating it, and recompensing good with good and evil with evil. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1009 | The natiq passed away poisoned and his wasi (Amir al-Mu'minin) killed; likewise Hasan, Husayn, al-Mustansir and al-'Amir passed by poison or being killed — and this befell them by the requirement of wisdom and the demand of justice as retribution (qisas). | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S1010 | Even the Friends of Allah, the chosen and trustees over His revelation, are subject to qisas with no escape; had pardon (musamaha) been permissible for anyone they would be most deserving of it. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1011 | A person's deeds extend to and are accounted against the people of his era, so retribution due from one era can be exacted in another — as Imam Husayn bore recompense for battles of Mawla Ali. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1012 | There is no escape from the scale of justice for any creature — noble or base, mighty or humble — and Allah shows no favoritism (muhabat) in any act of good or evil, as in 'whoever does an atom's weight of good/evil will see it'. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S1013 | Everything a person does of good or evil returns to him as recompense except what the shari'a makes lawful and is delivered by mutual consent and submission — like marriage on Allah's Book and the Prophet's sunna, in which there is no qisas. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1014 | Good deeds earn a tenfold reward and evil deeds a single recompense; evil (sharr) and sin (gunah) differ, with sin born from evil. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S1015 | Sins against Allah are forgiven by Allah through repentance, but rights/retributions owed to His servants are not forgiven until the servant forgives or Allah arranges means for the dues to reach the wronged party, else qisas occurs. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S1016 | Acts done lawfully by the shari'a's command — such as killing on a valid order — do not obligate retribution. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S1017 | Stoning for adultery (zina) is commanded collectively by Allah's order to remove the collective harms that such acts breed in the community, and it was the command in earlier religions too. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S1018 | The Prophet said no scratch of a twig, throbbing of a vein, or stumbling of a foot afflicts anyone except by a sin (dhanb), and what Allah forgives is much more — meaning such afflictions are pending retributions. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1019 | Unaccepted supplications in the world are stored and rewarded on the Day of Judgment, and a sacrifice (intended for a sick person who still dies) yields its benefit, multiplied in the next cycle if not reached in this one. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1020 | A man's delivering the present gift, dower, possession and mutual consent with shari'a's permission nullifies any right of qisas; but intercourse without shari'a's permission or without delivering dower obligates qisas. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1021 | Qisas became obligatory on the Imams (maqamat) for their lawful actions because there was no mutual consent from the killed party, and it may be exacted in the same cycle or a previous one — illustrated by 'the master served his slave' (khadam al-mawla 'abdahu): prophets served the hidden Imams, repaid by the Prophet through Mawla Ali. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S1022 | The kalima La ilaha illallah relates to numerical structures (negative/positive, dotted/undotted letters) such as 'ayn-lam-ya being three letters and the letters yielding totals of 40, 70, and 12 through their compositions. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S1023 | In Ali were combined seventy special characteristics (khususiyat), each testified to by Allah and His Messenger, that could not be gathered in any other person (in whom more than ten could not combine). | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S1024 | Ali delivered extempore (murtajjal/fil-badih) sermons demonstrating his mastery — a roughly three-page khutba containing no alif, and a thirteen-page khutba containing no dotted letters. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S1025 | Umar said 'Lawla Ali lahalaka Umar' (Were it not for Ali, Umar would have perished) — recorded as said seventy times. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 1/1 key terms located in section |
| S1026 | The deeds of the Imams and their friends — jihad against opposites, killing, applying hadd, stoning by command of Allah and the Imam — earn a reward that returns to their luminous structures (hayakil nuraniyya) and souls even while the qisas remains in place. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1027 | Killing in self-defense (dafa'-i hifazat), by the command of Allah, the Messenger, or those in authority, is rewarded though its qisas also remains; such deeds seeking Allah's reward are not lost (their reward not wasted) but the fullest requital still applies. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1028 | The qisas of the opposites falls upon their dense bodies (katha'if) by Allah's command, due to the aversion (karahiya) of the opposites' souls to what was done to them and their lack of pleasure in it — distinguishing defense from those who initiate bloodshed. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1029 | Inheritance law arises at death (not lifetime gifts/hiba), and a gift in terminal illness (marad al-mawt) has no consideration; valid hiba requires giving possession. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S1030 | In inheritance the parents each receive one-sixth, the husband a fourth (or eighth with children), the wife an eighth (or fourth without children); the order of death between mother and child decides who inherits. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 3/4 key terms located in section |
| S1031 | The scale of justice is established by Allah even in the date-stone's membrane (qitmir) and groove (naqir) — i.e., in the most minute matters. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S1032 | The Imams' actions against their opposites in their cycles are taking qisas from them for deeds committed in previous cycles and ages, which divine providence ('inaya ilahiyya) necessitated by gathering them in the Imams' time as justice and requital for their sins. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1033 | Mawla Ali composed the grammatical maxim that every doer is nominative (kullu fa'ilin marfu'un), every object accusative (kullu maf'ulin mansubun), every genitive broken (kullu majrurin maksurun) — esoterically: the one who acts is elevated, the imitator supported, the one needing to be drawn is broken. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1034 | Passing the hand over the face at Bismillah symbolizes the connection (ittisal) between the five upper and five lower limbs of the body, mirroring the connection of the five luminous essences ('aql, nafs, jadd, fatah, khayal) with the five worldly essences (Imam, Bab, Hujjat, Da'i, Ma'dhun). | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 12/12 key terms located in section |
| S1035 | Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim is the highest summary, but La ilaha illallah is higher; its 28 letters split into 15 dotted and 13 undotted, while Bismillah shows five repeated letters facing five non-repeated (khamsatun bi-iza'i khamsah). | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S1036 | The five non-repeated letters of Bismillah correspond to the five Luminous-Realm individuals who need not manifest, the five repeated to those who manifest in the world repeatedly, their connection forming one kalima — paralleling the body and the spiritual realm. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1037 | La ilaha illallah is the light of the word (nur-e kalima) from God; Bismillah and all Quranic verses descended from the Circle of the First Intellect / Circle of Origination, but their entire source is La ilaha illallah. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1038 | Acts a person does that the shari'a has not made lawful and the Imam has not commanded — killing one's own kind, looting, usurping, taking by oppression or treachery — quickly darken the soul's essence and become a barrier between it and the path of guidance. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 7/7 key terms located in section |
| S1039 | Whether a sin is erased after the Imam applies a hadd depends on the qisas of the Mudabbir across the cycles and returns (karrat), of which each Imam has 360; the Prophet said one who seeks forgiveness (istighfar), even seventy times, is not among the persistent sinners. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1040 | Sins against Allah are forgiven through seeking forgiveness, but rights of His servants are not forgiven until that servant forgives or Allah arranges means (like the Bitaq method) to bring the recompense to the wronged. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 2/3 key terms located in section |
| S1041 | The darkened soul is driven into the paths of error, ensnared in sins, made to hate the hudud and the people of truth, and cast into the fire of Hell — but the Quran says the disbelievers abide (khalidina) without 'forever' (abada), unlike the believers' eternal gardens, a mercy from Allah. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1042 | Vile acts like adultery (zina) inescapably require qisas (la budda min al-qisas), with the soul's essence darkening, eclipsing and descending. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1043 | The qisas for human major sins is exacted quickly in the descending garments (qumus): human form (Riss), then monkey-like (Waqs), 'Aks, Khars, then mineral, passing through the seven stages. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1044 | Remaining qisas for adultery passes into the Qamis al-Waqs, the human-like composition (monkey, nasnas, etc.), because copulation among monkeys exceeds that of other animals; the nasnas is a northern species said to have faces on their chests and to stand on one leg. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1045 | When many men sin with one woman, the qisas is distributed across the descending garments — some in Riss, most in Waqs and 'Aks and Khars and other descended animals — proportional to the abundance or scarcity of the act. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1046 | The descending forms (qumus) are temporary; only the thousand-year stature (Qamat-e Alfiyyah) has a (contingent) permanence, being the form on which the luminous essences and the world were created, with density and atoms stripped away stage by stage. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S1047 | Qisas and punishment apply in the descending animal stages despite the acts there occurring with consent and payment, because the original human act was done without the shari'a's permission, though the form of punishment differs. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1048 | The descending garments (darakat) apply to adultery and its likes (theft, robbery, usurpation), whereas the opponents of the Imam descend into the lower levels (adrak) rather than the darakat. | yes | yes | §26 (Retribution, Inheritance, and the Word of Unity); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1049 | The 27th of Rajab unites three events: Ali's birth (30 years before), the Prophet's mission (Mab'ath, when Ali was 10 and the Prophet 40), and Laylat al-Isra'. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1050 | On the Night of Ascension Allah raised the Prophet to the knowledge of His Kingdom (malakut), informed him of the limits of the heavens, taught him the unseen, favored him over all prophets and brought him near the celestial limits. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1051 | Fifty prayers were made obligatory and reduced to five at Moses' counsel during the Ascension; the lecturer holds the Prophet was more correct to accept five, given prayer's great blessings. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1052 | The verse 'Glory to Him who took His servant by night from al-Masjid al-Haram to al-Masjid al-Aqsa' declares the Creator's transcendence; God showed His signs (ayat), not Himself, and the inability to grasp comprehension is itself comprehension (al-'ajzu 'an dark al-idrak idrakun) — a saying of Ali. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1053 | God took 'His servant' ('abd), not 'the prophet' or 'the messenger', because servitude precedes messengership, as in the testimony 'Muhammad is His servant and His messenger' ('abduhu wa rasuluhu). | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S1054 | In ta'wil 'night' (layl) is an example of the veil and concealment (sitr/kitman) of the inner dimension; God concealed the Prophet and made him travel in the inner world between himself and the Completer (mutimm) of his era, who is the sixth Imam (Abu Talib). | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1055 | The Prophet's elevation by 40 years signifies completing 40 stages of prophethood then becoming messenger, surpassing all prophets; reaching qaba qawsayni aw adna (Abd al-Muttalib and Abu Talib as the two bows, Abdullah as adna), attaining the rank of the veil (hijabiyya) below 'Ayn Hazira (the Originator). | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1056 | The verse 'they feed the indigent, orphan, and captive' is interpreted: miskin = the Da'i from whom hearts find peace, yatim = a high-ranking one whose supporters were finished, asir = one imprisoned where evil scholars controlled — each freed by the call of the five companions (Ali, Fatima, Hasan, Husayn, Fidda). | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1057 | On the Ascension the Prophet saw the greatest signs of his Lord with the sight of the pure soul that did not swerve (ma zagha al-basar), narrated by A'isha that he never left her, indicating the vision was of the soul, not bodily absence. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1058 | The Buraq in ta'wil is the flashing, illuminating knowledge (al-'ilm al-mubriq) — the bayan, above the knowledge of the successor (lahiq) and below the Imam's knowledge; riding it is mastery over it, and ascension to heaven is being informed of its kingdom. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1059 | God showed Abraham the kingdom of the heavens and earth (malakut), meaning the spiritual world after the loftiness of his rank, and the earthly kingdom means the physical hudud (star, moon, sun) from whom he learned in the beginning. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1060 | The Prophet led all the prophets in prayer (esoterically his call to all nations), with Abraham as the muezzin at Safa and Marwah; he was sent to all nations whereas other prophets were sent only to their specific peoples. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1061 | The Prophet conveyed the knowledge he received to his foundation (asas) and instructed transmission one to one to each successor; al-Masjid al-Aqsa is the mutimm upon whom initiation is forbidden, and the blessing (baraka) is the support and initiation. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1062 | The 27th Rajab prayer of 22 rak'at (with 22 surahs, then Hamd, Inna Anzalna, Ayat al-Kursi each seven times) and its supplication commemorate the Prophet's mission; Rajab is the month of the Imams from the Ahl al-Bayt, Sha'ban the Prophet's month, Ramadan Allah's month. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1063 | Rajab's 30 days correspond to 30 Imams of the progeny of Isma'il; its nights are for the progeny of Isma'il and its days for the progeny of Ishaq; the 27th Mab'ath signifies 27 hidden Imams before the Prophet, with three days remaining for Abd al-Muttalib, Abdullah, and Abu Talib. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1064 | The three Imams (Abd al-Muttalib, Abdullah, Abu Talib) of the 27th-Rajab indication have a connection to both the Prophet and Mawla Ali, present at the Prophet's birth (and prophethood, 'Kuntu nabiyyan wa Adamu bayna al-ma'i wa al-tin'). | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1065 | The 'Ayn 'Azimah is the station of the Muhtajib in the realm of life, of which is said 'I am the First, the Last, the Manifest, the Hidden' (ana al-awwal, al-akhir, al-zahir, al-batin), with worldly figures made its servants. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1066 | The ranks ascend: Mustajib, Mu'min, Mukasir, Ma'sum, Da'i (mahsur/balagh), Hujjah, Bab, Imam (ten ranks), then Nubuwwa, Risala, Imama, Wasiyya (four ranks), above which is the divine utterance (nutq ilahi) counted among the ranks of Qa'imiyya. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 10/10 key terms located in section |
| S1067 | The bearers of divine utterance (nutq ilahi) in this era are Hunayd, Ibrahim, and Hashim, plus the seventh of each era (the Prophet); the 124,000 prophets occupy lower levels below the rank of nutq ilahi. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1068 | All human beings — natiq, wasi, Imam, follower or others — are in need of instruction (ta'lim) at the start of existence, per 'And Allah brought you out of your mothers' wombs knowing nothing', which is general for noble and ignoble alike. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S1069 | The difference among people is only in intelligence, sagacity, thought and acceptance (dhaka, fitna, fikr, qubul); the noblest — the natiq Muhammad and his father Ibrahim, and Amir al-Mu'minin Ali — learned and progressed bit by bit until reaching the pinnacle of utterance, with far greater preparedness ('iddat). | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 8/8 key terms located in section |
| S1070 | Abraham's 'seeing the star, moon and sun set' (la uhibbu al-afilin) refers to the physical hudud who undertook his upbringing in the beginning of his affair and from whom he learned. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1071 | Amir al-Mu'minin learned in the beginning from the Prophet, from Khadijah bint Khuwaylid (his teacher), and other hudud; he was attributed to Khadijah and likened to 'Isa ibn Maryam (Maryam = Khadijah, 'Isa = Ali). | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1072 | Ali said 'Rasulullah taught me a thousand chapters of knowledge, from each of which a thousand chapters opened up for me' (allamani rasulullah alfa babin... infataha li min kulli babin alfa bab). | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 2/2 key terms located in section |
| S1073 | The natiq's saying 'I took from five, delivered to five, and between me and my Lord are five' means: the five taken from are the five preceding speaking prophets (Adam, Nuh, Ibrahim, Musa, 'Isa); the five between him and his Lord are the religious hudud who taught him (Ubayy ibn Ka'b, Zayd ibn 'Amr, 'Amr ibn Nufayl, Maysarah, Khadijah). | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 8/8 key terms located in section |
| S1074 | The Master of the Time who established the Prophet, elevated and entrusted him is his uncle Abu Talib; the five hudud are Abu Talib's hudud. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S1075 | The five to whom the Prophet delivered are Amir al-Mu'minin, Hasan, Husayn, al-Baqir (Muhammad ibn Ali), and Muhammad ibn Isma'il; entrustment to Hasan and Husayn via 'al-Hasan wa al-Husayn imaman qama aw qa'ada wa abuhuma khayrun minhuma'. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1076 | Entrustment to al-Baqir came via the Prophet's message to Jabir ibn Abdillah ('O Baqir, cleave knowledge open'); entrustment to Muhammad ibn Isma'il via the second testimony of messengership in the adhan, since the Prophet's testimony for himself is impermissible. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1077 | Imam Muhammad al-Baqir, not Zayn al-'Abidin, spread the Prophet's knowledge in an era of dissemination; Ja'far is called al-Sadiq because he narrated his father's teachings with truth (sidq). | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1078 | There are 49 Imams (seven weeks) after the Prophet; the seventh of each week are superior, with Muhammad ibn Isma'il, al-Mu'izz, Imam al-Tayyib, al-Mansur etc. as week-heads; among them four are highest (la ilaha kalima's four words), and Muhammad ibn Isma'il is unique (sahib kasbat al-ilahiyya). | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1079 | The double testimony in the adhan: the first points to the Prophet, the second to Muhammad ibn Isma'il, completer of the era (mutimm al-dawr) and master of the apostles, the seventh natiq, indicated by al-Mu'izz in the Saturday prayer. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1080 | The Qa'im al-Qiyamah's affair is loftier than the apostles because each apostle is a limb of his limbs; he has become a Universal Intellect ('aql kulli) like the primordial abstract intellects and maintains the administration of the entire world, like the Tenth's administrators of nature. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1081 | Muhammad ibn Isma'il is from the divine speakers (nutuqa' ilahiyyin) along with Muhammad and Ibrahim, being the third to them; each natiq is a summary of the stations preceding him and a summoner (da'i) of the Imams of his time, a limb of the following Imam. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1082 | When an Imam (e.g., al-Tayyib) is in infancy after his father's death, his teachers are the Bab, Hujjah and Da'i al-balagh who never leave him from the time of his father's designation (nass) and concealment (satr); they instruct and elevate him, then he returns as their benefactor (mufid) bestowing blessings and support. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 8/8 key terms located in section |
| S1083 | When one of the three (Bab/Hujjah/Da'i) is to pass, he does not depart until he appoints a successor in his place; their time of death is by Allah's decree, not their own power — as Ja'far became mufid at twenty when Muhammad ibn al-Fayd entrusted his affairs ('Now the support has become Ja'fari'). | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 4/5 key terms located in section |
| S1084 | The Imam is connected to the sun, providing support to the three preceding ranks connected to Saturn, Jupiter and Mars; just as the heart is connected in the body, supporting the senses above and below it. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 4/7 key terms located in section |
| S1085 | The Prophet distributed the spiritual forms (suwar) of the five prophets: Adam to Ubayy ibn Ka'b, Nuh to Fatima (hence her lamentation/nawha), Ibrahim to Ali, Musa to Hasan, 'Isa to Husayn — explaining Husayn's likenesses to 'Isa (six months in the womb, persecution, a batul mother, post-martyrdom apparition). | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 10/10 key terms located in section |
| S1086 | The first arrivers at the great gathering wait, by means of the first, for the arrival of the last (yantazir bi-awwalikum quduma akhirikum), since their elevation completes when all gather in the station of the Tenth. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S1087 | In the concealment of martyrdom (hijabat), there is no qisas on the pure noble form (nasut/surat) or pure soul; qisas is only on the earthly part, so the Imam (the mudabbir) brings the form whose dues are pending to unite with the one being killed, in any human shape — e.g., the slayer thinks he kills Imam Husayn but it is the pending form taking its due. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1088 | The fida'i of Imam Husayn is Hazrat Abbas, whose fida'i is Muslim ibn Aqil; the Imam connects pending-qisas forms to such events, the slayer believing he kills Muslim ibn Aqil while really the form with pending dues is present. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1089 | The phenomenon of one figure appearing as another (shubbiha lahum) is illustrated by Mawlana Aminuddin appearing simultaneously at many hosts' meals, Ali leading Salman al-Farsi's funeral in distant Mada'in, and Judas being changed to resemble 'Isa. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 6/6 key terms located in section |
| S1090 | When a woman challenged Ali's claim 'ask me before you lose me' (saluni qabla an tafqiduni) about leading Salman's prayer in Mada'in yet not Uthman's in Madina, the reason was that the Muslims did not permit Uthman's burial in al-Baqi' (he was buried in the adjacent Jewish graveyard, later incorporated). | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 4/4 key terms located in section |
| S1091 | Names from companions like Uthman ibn Maz'un may be kept; Ali named a son Uthman after the pious companion Uthman ibn Maz'un, and names like Abu Bakr and Abd al-Rahman are permissible. | yes | yes | §27 (The Farthest Mosque and the Ascent of the Faithful); 3/3 key terms located in section |
| S1092 | Surat al-Fajr is recited, invoking the dawn, the ten nights, 'Aad of Iram, Thamud, Pharaoh, the leveling of the earth, the coming of the Lord and angels rank upon rank, and the reassured soul (al-nafs al-mutma'inna) returning to its Lord well-pleased. | yes | yes | §28 (The Dawn and the Straight Path); 5/5 key terms located in section |
| S1093 | Surat al-Fatiha is recited, praising the Lord of the worlds, Master of the Day of Judgment, asking to be guided to the straight path of those favored, not those who earned anger nor those astray. | yes | yes | §28 (The Dawn and the Straight Path); 4/4 key terms located in section |

Coverage: 1093/1093 present, 1093/1093 at full depth.
